  - → Add a 'Опубликовать' / promote action (with ConfirmDialog) that calls the promote endpoint and reflects the new published tier, or, if promote is intentionally out of scope, clearly mark it as «скоро»/handled elsewhere with a link, instead of instructing users to hit a raw API.
- 🟡 **Label not programmatically bound to the textarea (no htmlFor/id)** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / 3.3.2 Labels_ · `screen-assistant-prompt.jsx:230-241`)
  - The visible <label> «Системный промпт (черновик)» has no htmlFor and the <textarea> has no matching id; the binding relies solely on aria-label. Clicking the visible label does not focus the field, and the visible label and the aria-label string diverge, which is fragile for assistive tech and a labels-on-inputs gap. Field/Select primitives exist precisely to handle this binding.
  - → Use the Field primitive, or add an id to the textarea and htmlFor on the label so the visible label is the accessible name; drop the redundant aria-label or make it match the visible text.
- 🟡 **Disclosure toggle uses raw <button> with text-arrow glyphs (▲/▼) as icons** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption / icon policy_ · `screen-assistant-prompt.jsx:271-281`)
  - The 'Показать/Скрыть дефолтный промпт' disclosure is a hand-rolled <button> with inline styles rather than <Button variant="ghost"> (or a kit disclosure), and it uses Unicode arrows ▲/▼ as decorative icons. The contract says icons must be linear Lucide-style currentColor and warns against rolled-its-own buttons in app content. It also lacks aria-expanded, so the collapsed/expanded state is not exposed to screen readers.
  - → Replace with <Button variant="ghost" size="sm"> using a Lucide ChevronDown/ChevronUp glyph via the glyph prop, and add aria-expanded={showDefault} (and aria-controls pointing at the <pre> region id).
- 🟡 **Tier status uses ad-hoc badge + Unicode circle glyphs instead of StatusChip** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `screen-assistant-prompt.jsx:48-67 (tierBadgeStyle), :216-219`)
  - Tier state (published/draft/not set) is shown with a hand-built badge (tierBadgeStyle) and the glyphs ●/◐/○ prepended as text. The kit ships <StatusChip> (running/done/failed/waiting/paused) for exactly this status-pill role, and the executor-glyph policy reserves shape glyphs for ●Human/◆Agent/■Service meaning; reusing ●/◐/○ here as decorative status markers is off-system and the half-circle ◐ carries no defined meaning. Color is the primary differentiator (success/warning), risking a color-only signal.
  - → Render tier with <StatusChip> (mapping published→done-style, draft→waiting/paused, null→neutral) or a kit chip variant; if a glyph is kept it must be a Lucide icon and must not collide with the executor-glyph vocabulary. Ensure the text label (already present) remains the meaning-bearer so it is not color-only.
- 🟡 **No confirmation on destructive 'Очистить (сбросить к дефолту)' action** (feedback-state · _destructive-action confirmation_ · `screen-assistant-prompt.jsx:186-190, :260-262`)
  - 'Очистить (сбросить к дефолту)' wipes the entire textarea (handleReset sets text to '') with a single click and no confirmation or undo. For a potentially long, carefully authored system prompt this is destructive and easily mis-clicked, sitting right next to Save. The contract provides <ConfirmDialog> for exactly this.
  - → Gate the clear action behind <ConfirmDialog> (or only prompt when the textarea is non-empty / has unsaved edits), or offer an undo toast after clearing. Visually separate it from the primary Save button.
- 🟡 **Dev-jargon leak: raw API routes, HTTP status codes, and 'promote/agent_instruction' shown in UI** (content-clarity · _no dev-jargon in UI_ · `screen-assistant-prompt.jsx:138, :177, :248, :310, :329-341`)
  - The contract bans dev-jargon in the UI. End-user-visible copy exposes implementation internals: 'после продвижения через /api/artifacts/promote', '(тип: agent_instruction)', and error strings like 'Ошибка загрузки (HTTP 401/409/${status}).' Raw endpoint paths, internal artifact type names, and bare HTTP codes are meaningless to a business operator and read as leaked plumbing.
  - → Replace endpoint/type references with user-facing language (e.g. 'Опубликуйте промпт, чтобы он вступил в силу') and keep HTTP codes out of the primary message — show a human cause ('Сессия истекла, войдите снова' / 'Промпт уже опубликован') with the technical code only in a secondary/diagnostic detail.
- ⚪ **Unsaved-changes are silently lost on Обновить / role reload — no dirty-state guard** (feedback-state · _data-loss prevention_ · `screen-assistant-prompt.jsx:127-149, :263-265`)
  - 'Обновить' (and the post-save load) overwrite the textarea with server text. If the user has typed edits and clicks Обновить, their unsaved work is discarded without warning. There is no dirty indicator and no confirm.
  - → Track a dirty flag (text !== state.text) and either disable/confirm Обновить when dirty, or show an unsaved-changes indicator near the Save button.
- ⚪ **Disclosure <pre> has no Empty state when default_text is missing** (feedback-state · _explicit states (Empty)_ · `screen-assistant-prompt.jsx:282-296, :238 (placeholder)`)
  - If the API returns no default_text, the expanded <pre> renders empty and the textarea placeholder is undefined, producing bare emptiness — the contract requires every region to render an explicit Empty state, never blank. Low severity because default_text is normally present.
  - → Fall back to an <EmptyState> (or a short muted line like «Встроенный промпт недоступен») inside the disclosure and a literal placeholder string on the textarea when default_text is absent.

### `src/screens/screen-assistant.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡5 ⚪3
_A two-pane «Ассистент» screen: a left list of AI chat threads (create/rename/pin/delete) and a right conversation view with a budget meter, message bubbles, analyst "save as report" and bundle-promote actions, and a composer — a shell wired to future LLM endpoints._

- 🟠 **Emoji/unicode glyphs used as icons (★ ☆ ✎ ✕) on thread action buttons** (consistency · _principles.md: no emoji / no ✓✗ as icons; Lucide currentColor icons_ · `screen-assistant.jsx:331,340,349 (ThreadList action buttons)`)
  - The pin/rename/delete buttons render literal unicode characters '★' '☆' '✎' '✕' as their visible content. The contract explicitly forbids emoji-as-icon (including ✓/✗) and mandates linear Lucide-style icons via the <Icon> primitive using currentColor. These glyphs do not inherit the icon sizing/stroke/color system, render inconsistently across platforms/fonts, and the ✕ in particular reads as a destructive 'close', not 'delete'. The file already imports Icon and uses it elsewhere (Icon name="plus"/"process"/"apps").
  - → Replace the glyphs with kit icons: <Icon name="pin"/> (pinned/unpinned variant), <Icon name="edit"/> (pencil), <Icon name="trash"/> for delete. Keep the existing aria-label/title text. The empty pin-mark <span> at line 311 likely already uses a CSS icon — align all four on the same Icon primitive.
- 🟠 **Thread creation failure is swallowed silently — no user feedback** (feedback-state · _Affordance rule: an action must do something; States always explicit; Toast for transient outcome_ · `screen-assistant.jsx:781-791 (handleCreate), 837-849 ('Новый' button)`)
  - When createThread() rejects (network/HTTP error), the catch block only does console.error and returns. The user clicks «Новый» / «Начать разговор», nothing happens on screen, and no error is shown — a silent no-op, which the contract explicitly forbids. The whole screen wires no toast system at all (no useToasts / ToastViewport), so there is no channel for transient outcome feedback for create, rename, pin, or delete.
  - → Wire useToasts()+<ToastViewport> for this tree (the contract notes each tree must do this itself). On createThread failure show an error toast ('Не удалось начать разговор. Попробуйте ещё раз.'). Do the same for patchThread (rename/pin) and deleteThread failures, which currently also have no UI-visible error path.
- 🟡 **Hand-rolled <button> elements for app content instead of <Button> primitive** (consistency · _Design-system flag: app-content buttons must use kit <Button>; bespoke nav exception does not apply_ · `screen-assistant.jsx:303-319 (thread row), 324-350 (pin/rename/delete), 622-629 (context 'убрать')`)
  - Thread rows and the three per-thread action controls and the composer's 'убрать' control are raw <button> elements. The contract calls this out specifically as a violation for app-content buttons (only the shell's ThemeToggle/NavItem get the nav exception). These bespoke buttons miss the kit's standardized focus-ring (--chs-color-focus-ring), hover/active tokens, aria-busy/aria-disabled handling and sizing, so their interaction states will drift from the rest of the app.
  - → Use <Button variant="ghost" size="sm" glyph={<Icon .../>} aria-label=...> for the icon action buttons and the 'убрать' control. The thread-row itself is a selectable list item (listbox option) where a bespoke button may be acceptable, but it must still consume --chs-color-focus-ring/--chs-color-selected tokens; verify against design/tokens.css rather than ad-hoc CSS.
- 🟡 **Raw <input> and <textarea> without <Field> wrapper / label binding** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / 3.3.2; Design-system flag: raw input/select without <Field>/<Select>_ · `screen-assistant.jsx:246-258 (RenameInput <input>), 633-643 (Composer <textarea>)`)
  - Both interactive text controls are raw elements rather than the kit's <Field> primitive (which handles label↔control binding, aria-invalid, and hint via aria-describedby). They currently rely only on aria-label. The contract notes there is no <textarea> wrapper in the kit yet, so the textarea is a partial-exception, but the rename <input> has no excuse. Neither surfaces validation hints (e.g. the 120-char maxLength is silent — a user hitting the cap gets no feedback).
  - → Wrap the rename input in <Field> (visually-hidden label is fine for the inline-edit case). For the composer textarea, if no kit wrapper exists yet, flag the missing <Textarea>/<Field multiline> primitive and at minimum add an aria-describedby hint surfacing the Enter/Shift+Enter affordance instead of burying it only in the placeholder (placeholders disappear on focus and fail as the sole instruction).
- 🟡 **Rename and pin failures revert nothing and show no error** (feedback-state · _States always explicit; optimistic-update rollback_ · `screen-assistant.jsx:794-803 (handleRename/handlePin), 81-93 (patchThread)`)
  - handleRename optimistically updates activeThread title and handlePin toggles pin, but patchThread's promise rejection is unhandled at the call site (no await, no .catch). If the PATCH fails, the optimistic local state stays wrong (title/pin shows the new value) while the server never changed, and the user is never told. This produces a silent divergence between UI and truth.
  - → Await patchThread and on failure roll back the optimistic state and show an error toast. At minimum surface the rejection rather than letting it become an unhandled promise rejection.
- 🟡 **Invalid ARIA listbox composition: interactive controls inside role=option / group nested in presentation li** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2; ARIA listbox authoring practices_ · `screen-assistant.jsx:293-353 (ul[role=listbox] > li[role=presentation] > button[role=option] + div[role=group])`)
  - The thread list is a role=listbox whose options are buttons, but each <li role=presentation> also contains a sibling <div role=group> of action buttons. A listbox may only contain option/group-of-option children; arbitrary interactive controls (pin/rename/delete) inside the listbox are not a valid part of the option model, and screen-reader listbox navigation (arrow keys) will not reach them. There is also no roving-tabindex / arrow-key handler, so the 'listbox' does not behave like one for keyboard users.
  - → Either (a) drop the listbox/option roles and model this as a plain navigable list (<ul> of items, each a link/button) where the action buttons are normal focusable controls — simplest and most honest; or (b) keep listbox semantics and move actions out of the option (e.g. into a per-row menu) plus add arrow-key roving focus. Given it doubles as router navigation, option (a) is recommended.
- 🟡 **Nested aria-live regions; streamed assistant text and success states announced inconsistently** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.3 (Status Messages); live-region best practice_ · `screen-assistant.jsx:681 (role=log aria-live=polite), 582 (streaming dots aria-live=polite), 541 & 568 (success spans)`)
  - The messages container is role=log aria-live=polite AND the streaming-dots bubble inside it is also aria-live=polite — nested live regions cause duplicate/competing announcements. Meanwhile the positive outcomes 'Отчёт сохранён' (line 568) and 'Решение опубликовано' (line 541) are plain spans with no role=status, so successful save/publish is announced to sighted users only — inconsistent with errors which correctly use role=alert.
  - → Keep a single live region: let role=log on the container handle new messages and remove aria-live from the streaming-dots (its aria-label is enough, or use role=status on it alone). Wrap the two success spans in role=status (polite) so save/publish outcomes are announced, mirroring the role=alert error spans.
- ⚪ **Disabled 'Сохранить как отчёт' relies on title tooltip to explain why** (feedback-state · _Affordance rule (disabled must explain); WCAG title-as-sole-mechanism caveat_ · `screen-assistant.jsx:548-560 (MessageBubble analyst save button)`)
  - When no app context exists the save button is disabled and the only explanation ('Откройте ассистента из приложения, чтобы сохранить отчёт') lives in the title attribute and aria-label. A disabled element does not reliably fire tooltips on hover in all browsers and is skipped by some AT, so keyboard/touch users get a dead button with no visible reason. This is the contract's 'disabled must say why' edge.
  - → Render the reason as visible helper text next to the disabled button (or keep the button enabled and show the guidance inline on click). Visible microcopy beats a tooltip on a disabled control.
- ⚪ **Budget numbers not in mono font; tooltip duplicates visible label** (consistency · _Typography token: --chs-font-mono for money/numeric cols; --chs-color-* tokens_ · `screen-assistant.jsx:366-385 (BudgetIndicator)`)
  - The budget shows 'used / limit токенов' as plain text. The contract reserves --chs-font-mono for numeric/money columns; numeric usage figures like this read more scannably in mono and align with the kit's <BudgetMeter>/<ReservationMeter> domain primitives, which this hand-rolled track+fill duplicates. The title attribute also just restates the already-visible label, adding redundant tooltip noise.
  - → Prefer the existing <BudgetMeter>/<ReservationMeter> primitive instead of a bespoke track/fill div; if kept bespoke, render the figures with --chs-font-mono and drop the redundant title (reserve the tooltip for info the label doesn't already show, e.g. cost in ₽).
- ⚪ **Save-as-report / publish actions are one-shot with no undo or re-entry** (flow · _reversible-actions / status persistence_ · `screen-assistant.jsx:539-543 & 566-570 (post-success render swaps button for static text)`)
  - After a successful save or publish the action button is replaced by a static 'Отчёт сохранён' / 'Решение опубликовано' label for the lifetime of the message in view. There is no link to the created report/published solution and no way to act again (e.g. save an updated version). The 207-partial publish path does surface honestly, but the full-success path is a dead end with no follow-through affordance.
  - → On success, turn the confirmation into an actionable link ('Отчёт сохранён — открыть' / 'Решение опубликовано — открыть раздел') using the deep-link the bundlePromote/report response can return, so the user can verify the outcome instead of trusting a static label.

### `src/screens/screen-dmn-editor.jsx` — 🟠3 🟡2 ⚪3
_Lets process authors define branch-routing rules ("if [field] [operator] [value] → [outcome]") for a process without SQL, then save as draft and publish._

- 🟠 **Destructive rule deletion has no confirmation or undo** (feedback-state · _destructive-action confirmation_ · `screen-dmn-editor.jsx:239-256 (RuleRow delete button) → removeRule (510-512)`)
  - The per-rule «Удалить правило» button calls onRemove(rule.id) immediately, dropping an entire rule with all its conditions and outcome on a single click. There is no ConfirmDialog and no undo. The contract mandates ConfirmDialog (replacing window.confirm) for destructive actions; a rule can represent meaningful authoring work that is lost silently.
  - → For a rule that has any filled conditions/outcome, gate deletion behind <ConfirmDialog> ('Удалить правило? Это действие нельзя отменить.') or offer an undo toast via useToasts(). Trivially-empty rows can delete without a prompt.
- 🟠 **Action-outcome banners persist and never auto-dismiss; outcome convention is Toast** (feedback-state · _feedback after action (save/publish)_ · `screen-dmn-editor.jsx:699-715 (successMsg banner), 531/551 (setSuccessMsg)`)
  - Save/publish success is shown as a persistent inline banner that is only cleared when the next action starts. After 'Черновик сохранён' the user can edit rules for minutes while the banner still claims the draft is saved — stale/misleading feedback. The contract designates Toast for transient outcomes (error=assertive, else polite), with a useToasts()+ToastViewport queue; this screen hand-rolls a non-dismissable status div instead.
  - → Replace the successMsg div with a transient <Toast> via useToasts() (status/polite), and clear it (or mark the draft 'dirty') as soon as the user edits any rule so the message can never contradict current state.
- 🟠 **No validation feedback before save; half-filled rules are silently dropped** (feedback-state · _form validation UX_ · `screen-dmn-editor.jsx:113-122 (rulesToApiBody filters conditions) , 520-537 (handleSave)`)
  - rulesToApiBody silently filters out conditions missing field or operator, and there is no check that outcome is non-empty or that the «Иначе» row has an outcome. A user who leaves a value or outcome blank gets a '201 Черновик сохранён' success with their incomplete rule quietly discarded/garbled — false-positive success, no inline error. Empty value on a non-NO_VALUE operator is also accepted.
  - → Before save/publish, validate that each non-default rule has at least one complete condition and a non-empty outcome (and that value is filled for value-requiring operators). Surface per-field errors via the <Field>/<Select> aria-invalid + hint path and block submit, instead of silently filtering.
- 🟡 **Dev-jargon leaks: raw processKey slug and internal variable name shown to users** (content-clarity · _no dev-jargon in UI_ · `screen-dmn-editor.jsx:630 ({processKey} in breadcrumb), 405/757-759 (routingName default 'approvalRequired')`)
  - The breadcrumb prints the raw processKey (a machine slug/code-name) directly, and the routing field defaults to and displays 'approvalRequired' — a camelCase internal variable identifier. The contract's principles ban dev-jargon, code-names, UUIDs and identifier leaks in UI; the file header itself claims 'нет технического жаргона', but these two leaks contradict that.
  - → Show the process display name (title) in the breadcrumb instead of the slug, falling back to the slug only if no name exists. For the routing variable, present a human label and keep the technical variable name out of the visible default/placeholder (or pick it from the gateway definition rather than a hardcoded identifier).
- 🟡 **App-content interactive controls rolled as bare <button> instead of kit primitives** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `screen-dmn-editor.jsx:239-256, 314-330, 337-354, 608-622`)
  - The rule-delete, condition-remove, 'Добавить условие «И»', and back-nav controls are hand-rolled <button> elements with inline-styled colors/padding rather than the kit <Button> (e.g. variant=ghost/secondary). The contract flags rolled-its-own <button> for app content as a violation (only shell nav buttons are an accepted exception); these lose the kit's focus-ring/aria-busy/disabled handling and hover/active token states, producing inconsistent affordance.
  - → Use <Button variant="ghost" size="sm" glyph={<KitIcon name="close"/>}> for the remove/add-condition controls. The back link is borderline nav, but prefer <Button variant="ghost"> or a kit link component for consistent focus styling.
- ⚪ **Hardcoded font-weight and letter-spacing instead of design tokens** (consistency · _tokens-only contract_ · `screen-dmn-editor.jsx:230 (fontWeight: 600), 233 (letterSpacing: '0.04em'), 637 (fontWeight: 600)`)
  - Several styles use literal fontWeight: 600 and letterSpacing: '0.04em' rather than the contract's --chs-weight-semibold and tracking tokens. The contract treats hardcoded size/weight values as token violations (single source design/tokens.css).
  - → Replace fontWeight: 600 with var(--chs-weight-semibold) and the literal letter-spacing with the appropriate --chs tracking token.
- ⚪ **Value field disappears for present/absent operators with no explanation, leaving an orphan grid gap** (ux-pattern · _form layout / affordance clarity_ · `screen-dmn-editor.jsx:298-309 (NO_VALUE_OPS branch renders aria-hidden empty div)`)
  - When the operator is «заполнено»/«не заполнено», the «Значение» field is swapped for an empty aria-hidden placeholder div. The label silently vanishes and a blank column remains; a user mid-edit may be confused why their value input disappeared, with no microcopy explaining that these operators need no value.
  - → Render a muted hint in that cell (e.g. «значение не требуется») instead of an empty aria-hidden div, or collapse the grid column so the layout reflows cleanly.
- ⚪ **Publish button relies on title-tooltip-only to explain disabled state** (feedback-state · _affordance rule / disabled-state messaging_ · `screen-dmn-editor.jsx:668-677 and 838-847 (Опубликовать disabled when !tableId, title='Сначала сохраните черновик')`)
  - The Publish button is disabled until a draft is saved, and the only explanation is a native title tooltip — invisible on touch and to keyboard users, and not announced. The contract's affordance rule wants disabled state to be understandable; a tooltip is not an accessible substitute.
  - → Show the 'Сначала сохраните черновик' hint as visible inline helper text near the action group when !tableId, in addition to (or instead of) the title attribute, so the gating reason is always perceivable.

### `src/screens/screen-llm-config.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡4 ⚪4
_Tenant-scoped BYO-LLM configuration: shows connection status, lets the user set an OpenAI-compatible endpoint + model, and routes key (secret-handle) binding to the Agents screen._

- 🟠 **Error and success banners have no ARIA live role — outcome not announced to screen readers** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.3 Status Messages (AA)_ · `screen-llm-config.jsx:341 (submitErr bannerErrStyle), 342 (saveOk bannerOkStyle)`)
  - After pressing «Сохранить», the submit-error div (line 341) and the success div (line 342) appear/disappear with no role=alert / role=status / aria-live. A screen-reader or keyboard user who triggered the save gets no announcement of whether it succeeded or failed — the convention in the contract is Error=role=alert (assertive), Empty/Loading=role=status (polite). This is a real WCAG 4.1.3 AA gap (the MCP check missed it because the live-region attributes simply aren't present to evaluate).
  - → Add role="alert" to the submit-error banner and role="status" (aria-live polite) to the success banner, or route both through the kit Toast/feedback primitives which already set the correct live roles.
- 🟡 **Hardcoded inline-style objects instead of design-system primitives/CSS classes (no <Card>/<Panel>, no banner primitive)** (consistency · _design-system token/primitive consumption_ · `screen-llm-config.jsx:62-117 (sectionStyle, bannerErrStyle, bannerOkStyle, monoStyle, dotStyle) used throughout render`)
  - The whole screen is built from ad-hoc inline style objects. While they do consume --chs-* tokens (good), they also contain raw hardcoded values that bypass the token system: dotStyle uses width:8, height:8, borderRadius:'50%' (raw px + literal radius), monoStyle uses padding:'2px 6px' (raw px instead of --chs-space-*). The contract flags 'hardcoded px = violation'. The success/error banners are hand-rolled divs rather than a shared feedback primitive, and the four <section> cards reinvent a panel surface the contract notes is missing (no <Card>/<Panel>), inviting per-screen drift.
  - → Replace raw px (8/8, 2px 6px, '50%') with --chs-space-* and --chs-radius-full tokens or move them to a CSS class. Extract the status-dot, mono-pill and the ok/err banners into reusable primitives (or use <StatusChip>/<Mono> from the kit). If a panel surface is genuinely needed, propose a <Card>/<Panel> primitive rather than re-declaring sectionStyle on every screen.
- 🟡 **Save success feedback is a persistent inline banner, not a transient Toast** (feedback-state · _Toast / transient outcome surface_ · `screen-llm-config.jsx:342 (saveOk banner), handleSave:213-216`)
  - The contract states a successful action outcome should use a Toast (transient outcome) via useToasts()/ToastViewport. Here the success message renders as an inline green banner that stays until the next edit clears it. It also appears below the fields but the primary button is further down, so on a tall form the confirmation can be off-screen after submit. Mixing a persistent banner for success with the kit's toast convention is an inconsistency across the app.
  - → Emit a Toast (status/polite) on successful save via useToasts() instead of the inline bannerOkStyle div, or at minimum give the success banner role=status so screen-reader users are notified. Keep submit errors as an inline alert near the form (acceptable) but align success with the app-wide toast pattern.
- 🟡 **Field-level validation errors are conveyed only via hint text, with no programmatic error association** (accessibility · _WCAG 3.3.1 Error Identification / 1.3.1_ · `screen-llm-config.jsx:321-338 (endpoint & model Field), handleSave:197-204`)
  - On validation failure the error message is shoved into the same `hint` prop that otherwise carries help text (hint={fieldErrors.endpoint || 'OpenAI-совместимый base URL'}). invalid={true} is passed (good, likely sets aria-invalid), but because the error replaces the hint rather than supplementing it, the user loses the original guidance, and unless <Field> wires the hint as aria-describedby AND aria-live the error may not be announced when it appears. Focus is also not moved to the first invalid field after a failed submit.
  - → Verify <Field> exposes the error via aria-describedby and consider a dedicated error slot distinct from hint so help text is not lost. After a failed handleSave, move focus to the first invalid field (e.g. ref + .focus()) so keyboard users are taken to the problem.
- 🟡 **Dev-jargon / raw HTTP status leaks into user-facing copy** (content-clarity · _microcopy — no dev-jargon in UI_ · `screen-llm-config.jsx:271 ('ассистент спит (503)'), 149/227 ('HTTP 404'/'HTTP {status}'), 405 ('agent_card', 'тенант')`)
  - The contract explicitly bans dev-jargon in UI. Several leaks: the status line shows the raw HTTP code '(503)' to end users; load/save errors render 'HTTP 404' / 'HTTP {status}'; the help section exposes the internal field name 'agent_card' (a code identifier) and 'изолирована по тенанту' (the raw multitenancy term). These are meaningless to a business operator configuring an LLM.
  - → Replace '(503)' with plain language (e.g. «Ассистент недоступен, пока не настроен провайдер»). Map HTTP codes to human messages instead of printing 'HTTP 404'. Drop the literal 'agent_card' reference or describe it as «настройки агента»; rephrase tenant isolation as «настройки видны только вашей организации».
- ⚪ **Connection-status indicator relies on a color dot whose meaning is decorative-only to assistive tech** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.4.1 Use of Color_ · `screen-llm-config.jsx:264 (dotStyle dot, aria-hidden) + 265-271 status text`)
  - The green/grey status dot is aria-hidden=true, which is correct, and the adjacent text does carry the meaning — so this is mostly fine. The minor issue is that the inactive state uses --chs-color-text-muted (grey) for the dot rather than a semantic status token; the contract has dedicated status tokens (success/warning/danger/neutral). Using muted-text color for a status signal is a small semantic mismatch and can read as 'disabled' rather than 'not configured'.
  - → Use --chs-color-neutral-soft / a status token for the inactive dot rather than --chs-color-text-muted, or replace the bespoke dot+text with the kit <StatusChip> (which pairs shape/color with a text label by design).
- ⚪ **Anchor-wrapped Button produces nested-interactive / invalid affordance** (ux-pattern · _links-vs-buttons / nested interactive elements_ · `screen-llm-config.jsx:375-380 (<a href="/agents"><Button type="button">…</Button></a>)`)
  - A kit <Button> (which renders a real <button>) is wrapped in an <a href>. This nests an interactive control inside another interactive control — invalid HTML, double tab-stop potential, and ambiguous semantics (is it a link or a button?). The action is navigation, so it should be a single link styled as a button, not a button-in-link.
  - → Use a single element: either <Button as="a" href="/agents"> if the kit supports a polymorphic Button, or a link styled with the button class. Avoid wrapping <Button> in <a>.
- ⚪ **Unused EmptyState import — dead design-system reference; no true empty state distinct from error** (consistency · _state primitives (Empty/Loading/Error)_ · `screen-llm-config.jsx:21 (import EmptyState), used nowhere`)
  - EmptyState is imported but never rendered. The 'agent not connected' situation (the natural empty state for this screen) is instead handled with ad-hoc <p> text inside the status/key sections rather than the EmptyState primitive with a CTA, while a dead import lingers. Minor inconsistency with the contract's 'every list/detail renders Empty/Loading/Error'.
  - → Either remove the unused EmptyState import, or use it for the 'агент не подключён' case with a clear CTA to the Agents screen instead of inline paragraphs.
- ⚪ **'Обновить статус' duplicates the page's implicit refresh and sits beside the primary action without clear hierarchy** (ux-pattern · _action grouping / button hierarchy_ · `screen-llm-config.jsx:354-356 (ghost 'Обновить статус' button)`)
  - A ghost «Обновить статус» button calls loadConfig, but a successful save already calls loadConfig() (line 215), and the screen loads config on mount — so the refresh button's value is marginal and it competes for attention next to the primary «Сохранить». Its purpose (re-poll after binding a key on the Agents screen) is not explained, so users won't know why they'd click it.
  - → Either remove it, or add a short hint explaining when to use it (e.g. «после привязки ключа на экране Агенты»). Keep secondary/ghost actions visually subordinate and separated from the primary submit.

### `src/screens/screen-llm-connections.jsx` — 🟠3 🟡4 ⚪3
_A registry screen where owner/admin users create and manage named LLM connection profiles (provider, endpoint, model, prices), bind/unbind an encrypted API key, and test the live connection._

- 🟠 **Emoji ✗ used as failure icon in connection-test result** (consistency · _principles.md: no emoji (incl. ✓/✗) as icons; use linear Lucide currentColor_ · `screen-llm-connections.jsx:219 (ConnectionTester failure branch)`)
  - The failure state renders a literal ✗ character (`<span aria-hidden="true">✗</span>`), while the success state correctly uses `<Icon name="check" />`. The contract explicitly forbids emoji/✗ as icons. The two states are also visually inconsistent (kit icon vs raw glyph), and ✗ font rendering varies across platforms.
  - → Replace the ✗ span with the kit `<Icon name="x" />` (or the danger/alert Lucide icon) so success and failure use the same icon system and respect currentColor theming.
- 🟠 **Destructive 'Отвязать' (unbind key) fires with no confirmation** (feedback-state · _ConfirmDialog primitive; destructive-action confirmation_ · `screen-llm-connections.jsx:324 + clearKey() at 286-305`)
  - Clicking 'Отвязать' immediately DELETEs the encrypted key with no confirmation step. Unbinding a key is destructive and irreversible from the UI (the key is write-only and cannot be read back), and breaks any agent relying on the connection. The contract ships `<ConfirmDialog>` precisely for this.
  - → Gate clearKey() behind `<ConfirmDialog>` with a clear consequence message (e.g. «Отвязать ключ? Агенты, использующие это соединение, перестанут работать, пока вы не привяжете ключ заново.»).
- 🟠 **Save/create outcome banners are not announced to assistive tech** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.3 Status Messages (AA); state-primitive feedback convention_ · `screen-llm-connections.jsx:633-634 (submitErr/createOk banners), 355 (key-bind msg), 206-222 (test result)`)
  - All post-action feedback is rendered as plain `<div>`s with no role. The contract requires error feedback to use `role=alert`/assertive and transient outcomes to use Toast (`status`/polite). A screen-reader user submitting the create form or binding a key gets no announcement of success or failure.
  - → Route these outcomes through `useToasts()`+`<ToastViewport>` (error=alert/assertive, success=status/polite). At minimum, add `role="alert"` to error banners and `role="status"` to success banners so outcomes are announced.
- 🟡 **Hand-rolled success/error banners instead of kit feedback primitives** (consistency · _Toast / useToasts feedback convention_ · `screen-llm-connections.jsx:66-83 (bannerErrStyle/bannerOkStyle), 311-314 (msgStyle), 176-189 (resultStyle/hintStyle)`)
  - Three separate bespoke inline-styled banner/message systems are defined (form banners, key-binder message, tester result) where the contract provides `<Toast>` for transient outcomes. This duplicates styling logic and produces inconsistent treatment of the same semantic event (success/error) across the screen.
  - → Consolidate on the kit feedback path — emit toasts via `useToasts()` for create/bind/unbind/test outcomes. Reserve inline banners only for persistent in-form errors, and if kept, derive them from a shared component rather than three local style objects.
- 🟡 **'По умолчанию' checkbox is a bare <input>, not a kit control** (consistency · _forms/text-field — labeled control; review_code Missing Input Label_ · `screen-llm-connections.jsx:622-625`)
  - review_code flagged the only raw form control on the screen. While the label text wraps the input (so it is technically associated), it uses a bare `<input type="checkbox">` with no kit styling, no focus-ring token, and no invalid/hint affordances — inconsistent with every other field which uses `<Field>`/`<Select>`. The contract notes there is no `<Checkbox>` wrapper yet, so this is a known gap, but the raw control still bypasses the design system.
  - → Either add a small kit `<Checkbox>` primitive (preferred, fills a documented gap) or, short-term, ensure the input gets `--chs-color-focus-ring` focus styling and an explicit `id`/`htmlFor` binding consistent with `<Field>`.
- 🟡 **Success banner ('Профиль создан.') is sticky and never auto-clears** (feedback-state · _Toast transient-outcome convention_ · `screen-llm-connections.jsx:634 + createOk state (380, 500)`)
  - createOk is set true on success and only reset on the next provider change or submit. It is NOT cleared when the user edits any field, so a stale 'Профиль создан.' confirmation lingers next to the form while the user types the next profile — ambiguous feedback that no longer reflects current state.
  - → Make the success outcome transient (toast that auto-dismisses) or clear createOk on any field onChange. The same applies to submitErr, which also persists across edits.
- 🟡 **ConnectionTester error result not announced; relies on color+glyph only** (feedback-state · _WCAG 4.1.3 Status Messages; 1.4.1 Use of Color_ · `screen-llm-connections.jsx:206-222`)
  - The test outcome (success/fail) appears in a div with no live-region role, so a keyboard/SR user who triggers 'Проверить подключение' is not told the result. Success vs failure is conveyed by color (green/red soft bg) plus an icon; the failure icon is the non-semantic ✗ glyph (see separate finding).
  - → Wrap the result in `role="status"` (and `aria-live="polite"`) so the probe outcome is announced. Keep the text label (already present) so meaning does not depend on color alone.
- ⚪ **Hardcoded px/rgba values bypass spacing/radius tokens** (consistency · _tokens.css — all --chs-* ; hardcoded size = violation_ · `screen-llm-connections.jsx:103 ('2px 6px'), 109 ('2px 8px'), 109 (radius-pill fallback '999px'), 102/186 ('monospace' fallback), 525 (maxWidth: 820)`)
  - Several literal pixel paddings, a 999px radius fallback, a raw 820px max-width, and `monospace` font fallback are hardcoded instead of consuming `--chs-space-*`, `--chs-radius-*`, and `--chs-font-mono`. The file header claims 'строго OBLIK — только --chs-* токены', so these are contract drift.
  - → Replace literal paddings with `--chs-space-*`, use the real `--chs-radius-full`/`--chs-radius-pill` token without a hardcoded fallback, move the max-width to a layout token if one exists, and drop the `monospace` literal in favor of `var(--chs-font-mono)`.
- ⚪ **Chip status relies on a soft/neutral pair that may not meet AA text contrast** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.4.3 Contrast (Minimum) AA_ · `screen-llm-connections.jsx:106-113 (chipStyle), 311-314 (msgStyle success/danger as body text)`)
  - The 'ключ не привязан' chip puts `--chs-color-text-muted` on `--chs-color-surface-raised`, and msgStyle renders success/danger color directly as the text color at xs size. Muted-on-raised and saturated status colors as small text are the classic AA-contrast risk spots; this should be verified rather than assumed (the automated AA check did not see these computed colors).
  - → Verify these token pairs against 4.5:1 (small text) in both light and dark themes; if short, use the stronger text token or the status -fg/ -strong variant for the foreground.
- ⚪ **Provider preset overwrites user-edited endpoint/model/price without warning** (flow · _forms — preserve user input / undo_ · `screen-llm-connections.jsx:421-436 (onProviderChange)`)
  - Changing the provider unconditionally overwrites endpoint and model (and prices when the preset has them), silently discarding anything the user typed. The inline comment claims prices fill 'only if blank' but the code overwrites whenever the preset value is truthy. A user who hand-tuned an endpoint then toggled provider loses that work with no notice or undo.
  - → Only overwrite endpoint/model/prices when the field is empty or still equals the previous preset's default; otherwise leave user edits intact (or confirm before replacing). Align the code with the comment's stated 'don't override user edits' intent.

### `src/screens/screen-login.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡1 ⚪4
_Mode-aware login screen: in keycloak mode a single "Войти" button starts the OIDC redirect (plus a "Создать организацию" register link); in dev mode it fetches /api/users and renders a password-less identity picker._

- 🟠 **Keycloak "Войти" button gives no feedback while the OIDC redirect is in flight** (feedback-state · _forms/submit-button (loading state); design-contract affordance/feedback rule_ · `screen-login.jsx:91-93 (keycloak <Button onClick={() => onLogin()}>)`)
  - Clicking "Войти" starts a full-page OIDC redirect, which can take a noticeable moment on a cold Keycloak. The button has no loading/aria-busy state, so a user who clicks and sees nothing happen for a beat may click again or assume it is broken. The kit <Button> supports a `loading` prop but it is unused here.
  - → Track a local `submitting` state, set it on click, and render <Button variant="primary" loading={submitting} disabled={submitting} onClick={...}>. This gives the spinner + aria-busy feedback the kit already provides and prevents double-submit during the redirect.
- 🟡 **Dev-mode user-pick buttons are hand-rolled instead of using the kit, and have no focus-visible ring** (consistency · _design-contract: app-content buttons must use kit; WCAG 2.4.7 Focus Visible_ · `screen-login.jsx:143-149 + style block 263-284 (.chs-login-user-btn)`)
  - These are content buttons (selecting an identity = the primary action of the screen) built as raw <button> with bespoke CSS. The contract allows bespoke buttons only for shell nav; content actions must consume <Button>. More concretely: the CSS defines :hover and :active but no :focus-visible, while the sibling register link DOES define one (line 243). So a keyboard user tabbing through the identity list gets only the inconsistent UA default outline (and `transition: all` plus the accent :active styling can make the focus state ambiguous). On a login screen, keyboard selection of identity must be clearly visible.
  - → Either (preferred) refactor each row to a kit primitive that owns the focus ring, or add a `.chs-login-user-btn:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--chs-color-focus-ring); outline-offset: 2px; }` rule mirroring the register link. If the kit has no list-row button variant, this is a real primitive gap worth raising rather than re-hand-rolling.
- ⚪ **Native <button> elements lack an explicit type attribute** (consistency · _forms/submit-button_ · `screen-login.jsx:143-149 (.chs-login-user-btn)`)
  - The rolled-its-own user-pick <button> has no `type`. A bare <button> defaults to type="submit"; if this markup is ever placed inside a <form> it would trigger an unintended submit. review_code flagged this as a warning.
  - → Add type="button" to the native <button>. (The kit <Button> at line 91 sets this internally, so only the hand-rolled one needs it — and ideally it should move to the kit; see the consistency finding below.)
- ⚪ **Retry duplicates the entire fetch logic inline instead of reusing one function** (ux-pattern · _error-handling/retry consistency_ · `screen-login.jsx:27-45 vs 47-67 (fetchUsers defined twice, once in useEffect, once in handleRetry)`)
  - The initial load and the retry path are byte-for-byte copies of the same fetch+error mapping. They can drift (e.g. a future tweak to error copy or endpoint applied to only one), producing inconsistent load vs retry behavior. Not a user-visible bug today, but a UX-consistency liability on the error path.
  - → Hoist a single `loadUsers` callback (useCallback) and call it from both useEffect and handleRetry so the loading/error/empty behavior is guaranteed identical between first load and retry.
- ⚪ **Register link reimplements client-side navigation by hand** (flow · _navigation/link_ · `screen-login.jsx:99-105 (a href="/register" with preventDefault + pushState + manual popstate dispatch)`)
  - The "Создать организацию" link manually does preventDefault, history.pushState, and dispatches a synthetic PopStateEvent to drive routing. It keeps a real href (good for middle-click/open-in-new-tab and a11y), but the hand-rolled push+dispatch is fragile and inconsistent with however the rest of the app routes; a real cmd/ctrl-click would be hijacked into same-tab nav by the unconditional preventDefault.
  - → Use the app's router/navigate helper if one exists. At minimum, guard the handler to bail out on modified clicks (e.metaKey || e.ctrlKey || e.shiftKey || e.button !== 0) so open-in-new-tab keeps working.
- ⚪ **Dev-mode environment label leaks into user-facing copy** (content-clarity · _design-contract: no dev-jargon in UI_ · `screen-login.jsx:118 (subtitle "вход (dev)")`)
  - The contract bars dev-jargon in the UI. "вход (dev)" surfaces the environment/mode name. It is mode-signalling rather than a code-name/UUID, so it is the mildest form, but it is still developer terminology shown to a person.
  - → Acceptable only if this branch truly never reaches non-developers (dev auth mode). If there is any chance it does, replace with neutral copy (e.g. drop the "(dev)" suffix or use "тестовый вход"). Confirm the keycloak branch is the production path.

### `src/screens/screen-register.jsx` — 🟡2 ⚪4
_Self-serve registration: a user creates an organization (org name + email + password); on 201 the app kicks off the Keycloak PKCE sign-in redirect._

- 🟡 **Error region renders after submit but does not receive focus or scroll into view** (feedback-state · _WCAG 3.3.1 Error Identification; error-recovery feedback_ · `screen-register.jsx:70-80 (submitting branch swaps the whole form; submitError block at 76-80)`)
  - On a failed submit, submitting flips back to false, the form re-renders, and the ErrorState appears with role=alert. role=alert announces once, but focus is not moved to the error or the first offending field, and the error is not scrolled into view. After the loading swap on a tall/zoomed viewport, a keyboard or screen-reader user can be left at the form with the failure announced only once and no focus guidance.
  - → After setSubmitError, move focus to the error container (ref + tabIndex=-1 .focus()) or to the first invalid field, and scrollIntoView. Keep role=alert for the announcement but pair it with focus management so keyboard/SR users are taken to the problem.
- 🟡 **Network failure (status 0) maps to a generic message with no Retry affordance** (feedback-state · _ErrorState onRetry convention; affordance rule_ · `screen-register.jsx:78 (ErrorState used without onRetry) + register-form.js:78/100-102 (status 0 fallback)`)
  - postRegister swallows network errors into {status:0}, mapped to 'Ошибка регистрации. Попробуйте ещё раз.' The kit's ErrorState supports onRetry (contract: Error = role=alert + Повторить), but it is rendered without onRetry. The only retry path is re-pressing the primary button; the copy tells the user to retry without giving the standard Повторить affordance used elsewhere in the app.
  - → Pass onRetry={handleSubmit} to ErrorState so the standard Повторить button appears, matching the app-wide error convention. Optionally give status 0 (network/offline) distinct copy from a generic server error.
- ⚪ **Loading state replaces the entire form instead of an inline busy button** (flow · _loading-state in-place vs full swap; preserve context_ · `screen-register.jsx:70-123 (ternary swaps form вs LoadingState)`)
  - While submitting, the whole form is unmounted and replaced by a centered LoadingState. The Button already exposes a loading prop, so an inline busy button would keep the filled form visible and avoid layout jump. On a failed submit the user sees form то spinner то form flash. Values are preserved in state so nothing is lost functionally, but the transition is jarrier than necessary.
  - → Keep the form mounted with fields disabled and the Button in loading state (it already supports loading + aria-busy) rather than swapping to a standalone LoadingState. This preserves context and the button spinner is sufficient feedback for a short POST.
- ⚪ **Sign-in link hand-dispatches popstate instead of using the app's navigation primitive** (consistency · _consistent navigation; rolled-your-own_ · `screen-register.jsx:128-134 (a href=/ onClick то pushState + new PopStateEvent('popstate'))`)
  - The 'Войти' link manually calls window.history.pushState and dispatches a synthetic popstate to trigger a re-render. This bespoke routing shim is inlined in the view, bypasses whatever navigation mechanism the rest of the app uses, and is fragile (any change to how the shell listens for route changes breaks it silently). href=/ also means middle/ctrl-click does a full reload of '/'.
  - → Route through the app's shared navigation helper (the same one the shell uses) rather than re-dispatching popstate inline. If none exists, extract a small navigate(path) util so the pattern is defined once. Confirm href target matches the actual login route.
- ⚪ **Password constraint hint and validation error share one slot, so the rule flickers** (content-clarity · _form validation messaging; persistent constraint hint_ · `screen-register.jsx:111 (hint={fieldErrors.password || 'Не менее 8 символов'})`)
  - The persistent constraint hint 'Не менее 8 символов' lives in the same hint slot the error overwrites. When the password is too short the slot becomes 'Пароль должен содержать не менее 8 символов' (error), and as soon as the user types, set() clears the error and the constraint reappears. The constraint and the error are near-duplicate phrasings of the same rule, which reads as the requirement flickering between two wordings.
  - → Either keep constraint and error visually distinct (constraint always visible, error styled invalid below) or align the wording so both read as the same rule (e.g. both 'Минимум 8 символов'). Low priority polish.
- ⚪ **Validation runs only on submit; no inline feedback on blur after first failure** (flow · _form validation timing (validate on blur after first submit)_ · `screen-register.jsx:37-45 (validate only inside handleSubmit); set() clears errors on change`)
  - Validation fires exclusively on submit. Errors clear on change (good — no error-while-typing), but a user who mistypes their email gets no signal until pressing the primary button. For a 3-field onboarding form this is acceptable, but blur-time validation after the first failed submit is the standard pattern and removes a round-trip.
  - → Optional: after the first submit attempt, validate the touched field on blur so the user sees the email/length problem before re-submitting. Keep clear-on-change to avoid nagging mid-typing.

### `src/screens/screen-app-records.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡6 ⚪3
_Constructor screen where users browse and create data records for a no-code app: pick a field-set, see records in a table, and add new records via a schema-generated form in a right drawer._

- 🟠 **No success feedback after creating a record (no toast)** (feedback-state · _Toast / transient outcome (design-contract §4)_ · `handleCreated (line 909-913) / CreateRecordDrawer.handleSubmit success path (line 684-688)`)
  - On a successful 201 the drawer simply closes and the new row gets a green-soft background highlight. There is no toast confirming «Запись создана». The highlight (highlightId, line 1029) is set but never cleared, so it is a permanent state rather than a transient outcome, and if the new row lands on a later cursor page or below the fold the user gets zero confirmation that the save succeeded. The contract reserves Toast for transient outcomes and the kit ships <Toast>/useToasts() for exactly this.
  - → Wire useToasts()+<ToastViewport> into this tree and fire a status toast («Запись создана») on 201. Make the row highlight transient (clear highlightId after ~2-3s via setTimeout, respecting reduced-motion) instead of leaving it permanently tinted.
- 🟡 **Relation/computed cell load & error states use ad-hoc inline markup instead of state primitives** (consistency · _States always explicit via primitives (contract §2/§3: LoadingState/ErrorState/Skeleton)_ · `RelationPicker loading/error/empty branches (lines 153-198), RelationCell (lines 295-310)`)
  - RelationPicker hand-rolls its loading («Загрузка…» italic div), error («Не удалось загрузить связанные записи») and empty («В целевом наборе пока нет записей») states as bespoke styled <div>s rather than the kit LoadingState/ErrorState/EmptyState. RelationCell renders a bare «…» for loading and «— / Нет доступа» for denied. The error branch offers no retry affordance, unlike the kit's <ErrorState onRetry>. This is precisely the 'bare state instead of state primitive' flag in the contract and produces inconsistent state UI across the app.
  - → Inside the field wrapper, render the kit's compact state primitives (or <Spinner>/<Skeleton> for the cell). For the picker's fetch failure, surface a retry — either reuse ErrorState semantics or add a small «Повторить» control. At minimum give the error region role=alert so it is announced, not just aria-live=polite.
- 🟡 **Whole-row click navigation duplicates the explicit «Открыть» link and is not keyboard-reachable** (accessibility · _WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard; affordance rule (contract §2)_ · `records table <tr> (lines 1027-1031) and the «Открыть» <Link> (lines 1052-1064)`)
  - The entire <tr> has cursor:pointer + onClick navigate, but a <tr> is not focusable or operable by keyboard — there is no tabIndex/role/onKeyDown — so keyboard and screen-reader users cannot trigger row navigation; they can only use the «Открыть» link. The row click also competes with text selection and with the relation-cell <Link> inside the row (clicking a relation label navigates to one record while the row would navigate to another). The redundant click target adds ambiguity without adding reachable function.
  - → Treat the «Открыть» link as the single authoritative navigation affordance (it is already keyboard-accessible). If a clickable row is desired for pointer ergonomics, keep it as a pure pointer enhancement but ensure every actionable destination is reachable via a real focusable control, and stopPropagation on inner links/cells so nested targets win. Do not rely on the bare <tr onClick> as the only path.
- 🟡 **Required-field asterisks are aria-hidden with no programmatic 'required' on several controls** (accessibility · _WCAG 3.3.2 Labels or Instructions / 1.3.1 Info and Relationships_ · `RelationPicker label (line 142-144 — note the picker <select> does set aria-required), LineItemsField field label (line 407-409) and sub-field <th> (line 442-444)`)
  - Required state is conveyed only by a visual «*» that is explicitly aria-hidden="true". For the LineItemsField collection and its per-column required sub-fields there is no programmatic equivalent (no aria-required on a grouping element, no text alternative), so assistive-tech users never learn the field/column is mandatory. The asterisk's meaning is also never spelled out anywhere on screen.
  - → Pair the visual asterisk with a programmatic signal: add aria-required/required to the actual controls (FieldControl cells, the collection group), or append visually-hidden text «обязательное поле» to the label. Ensure the legend «* — обязательное» appears once in the form so the glyph is decoded.
- 🟡 **Inline validation errors are not programmatically tied to their fields** (accessibility · _WCAG 3.3.1 Error Identification / 1.3.1; kit <Field> aria-describedby convention (contract §3)_ · `RelationPicker errorNode (lines 147-151), LineItemsField collectionError (line 413), ComputedReadout has none`)
  - Per-field error text is rendered as a sibling <span> with color only; it is not linked to the control via aria-describedby, and the control's aria-invalid is set on the <select> but the message itself is not announced as it appears (no role=alert / aria-live on the per-field error). Screen-reader users focusing the field hear 'invalid' with no reason. This diverges from the kit <Field>, which wires label↔control↔hint/error via aria-describedby and aria-invalid.
  - → Prefer routing these custom fields through the kit <Field>/<Select> wrappers so error binding is automatic. Where a bespoke wrapper is unavoidable (relation/collection), give the error <span> an id and reference it from the control via aria-describedby, and add aria-live=polite (or role=alert) so newly-appearing messages are announced.
- 🟡 **Raw <select>/<input> used for field-set picker and relation control instead of kit <Select>/<Field>** (consistency · _Design-system primitive consumption (contract flags: raw input/select without Field/Select)_ · `header field-set picker <select> (lines 952-963), RelationPicker filter <input> (lines 216-224) and <select> (lines 225-239)`)
  - The contract explicitly flags raw <input>/<select> without <Field>/<Select> as a consumption violation. The header field-set <select> has only an aria-label (no visible label), and the relation filter input + select are hand-built. This loses the kit's consistent label binding, focus-ring, aria-invalid and hint plumbing, and makes styling drift likely (these rely on a .chs-input class rather than the primitive).
  - → Route the field-set picker and the relation control through the kit <Select> (and <Field> for the filter input) so label binding, focus ring and invalid styling come from one source. If the kit lacks a searchable-select primitive, that is a real gap worth a kit addition rather than per-screen reinvention.
- 🟡 **Error messages leak raw HTTP/dev detail into user-facing UI** (content-clarity · _No dev-jargon in UI (contract §2)_ · `loadDefs/loadRecords catch → setDefsError(e.message)/setRecordsError(e.message) (lines 831, 878) surfaced at lines 981 & 1001; RelationPicker setFetchError(String(err.message)) (line 131)`)
  - Fetch failures throw `new Error('HTTP ' + res.status)` and the raw message is interpolated straight into «Не удалось загрузить наборы полей: HTTP 500». 'HTTP 500' is dev-jargon to a business user. The relation cell title also exposes a raw UUID: title={`Открыть запись ${targetId}`} (line 317) and RelationCell loading shows a bare «…», both edging toward the no-UUID / honest-state rules.
  - → Map status codes to human Russian messages (e.g. 500→«Временная ошибка сервера, попробуйте позже»; 401/403→«Нет доступа») before display, as the create path already does via mapRecordError. Drop the raw UUID from the relation link title (use the derived label or «Открыть связанную запись»).
- ⚪ **Destructive row removal in line-items has no confirmation and no undo** (ux-pattern · _Destructive action confirmation (kit <ConfirmDialog>) / forms_ · `LineItemsField removeRow via the «✕» Button (lines 500-509, 376-378)`)
  - The per-row delete button immediately drops a row (with all its entered cell data) with no confirmation or undo. This is pre-submit editing so the blast radius is limited, but a mis-click on a fully-filled row silently loses work. The contract provides <ConfirmDialog> for destructive actions; instant data loss with no recovery is a mild affordance risk.
  - → For a single-cell trivial row this is acceptable, but consider a lightweight guard for rows containing entered data (confirm or an inline undo/toast). At minimum keep the current explicit aria-label; do not escalate to window.confirm.
- ⚪ **Field-set picker change does not reset/guard against in-flight create drawer, and selecting nothing silently disables creation** (flow · _Affordance rule — enabled element must do something / state clarity (contract §2)_ · `header <select> onChange (line 956) and primary «Создать запись» disabled={!selectedDef} (line 969)`)
  - With >1 field-set, the «Создать запись» button is disabled until a set is picked; the only cue is a tooltip («Сначала выберите набор полей») which is invisible to touch and not announced. A disabled primary CTA with the reason hidden in a title attribute is a discoverability gap. Separately, the EmptyState «Выберите набор полей» (lines 996-999) describes the situation but offers no inline picker as its CTA, so the user must hunt back up to the header select.
  - → Surface the gating reason visibly (e.g. inline hint next to the picker) rather than only via title. Consider giving the «Выберите набор полей» EmptyState an actionable control (or move focus to the picker) so the empty state is a true next-step, matching the affordance rule.
- ⚪ **Permanent green highlight on newly-created row is a misused status color** (feedback-state · _Transient outcome vs persistent status (contract §1 success-soft is status color)_ · `records <tr> background highlightId (line 1029); highlightId set in handleCreated (line 911) and never cleared`)
  - The just-created row is tinted with --chs-color-success-soft permanently for the life of the list (until reload), implying an ongoing 'success' status on a row that is just a normal record. Status-soft colors carry semantic meaning; using one as a permanent 'this is new' marker overloads the signal and degrades scannability if multiple records are created in a session.
  - → Make the highlight transient (fade out after a few seconds, honoring reduced-motion) so it reads as a momentary 'just added' cue, and pair it with the success toast recommended above rather than relying on a persistent tint.

### `src/screens/screen-app-schema.jsx` — 🟠3 🟡3 ⚪3
_Constructor screen where a user defines the fields (record_schema) of an application's registry_def — add/remove/reorder fields, set types (incl. select, relation, collection, computed/rollup), and save via POST/PUT._

- 🟠 **Successful save gives no confirmation feedback (no toast)** (feedback-state · _Toast for transient outcome (DS contract: surface choice — Toast)_ · `FieldEditor.handleSubmit success branch, screen-app-schema.jsx:885-892; AppSchemaScreen.handleSaved:1063-1066`)
  - On POST/PUT success the editor simply calls onSaved(payload), which sets editing=undefined and closes the form. The user gets no explicit 'Сохранено' confirmation — the only signal is the editor vanishing and the list silently re-fetching. The design-system contract names Toast as the surface for transient outcomes and ships useToasts()+<Toast>. Save/create is exactly the action that should confirm.
  - → Wire useToasts()+<ToastViewport> in this tree and fire a success toast (status/polite) on 201/200, e.g. «Набор полей создан» / «Поля сохранены», before/along with onSaved(). This also fixes the silent close ambiguity.
- 🟠 **Save warnings banner is dead code — never visible because editor unmounts** (feedback-state · _Feedback after action / lost outcome_ · `FieldEditor.handleSubmit:888-892 (setWarnings then onSaved) and warnings render block:1002-1012`)
  - On PUT success the code sets warnings (deps relabeled) THEN immediately calls onSaved(payload), which closes the editor (setEditing(undefined) in handleSaved). Because FieldEditor unmounts, the warnings <div> at lines 1002-1012 never renders. The user is never told 'Затронуты зависимые отчёты — проверьте их', a meaningful data-integrity warning, making the whole branch dead code.
  - → Either keep the editor open and require the user to acknowledge warnings before closing, or pass warnings up through onSaved(payload) and surface them as a persistent toast/inline banner on the list screen so the destructive-relabel warning actually reaches the user.
- 🟠 **Removing a field/column is destructive on edit but has no confirmation** (flow · _Destructive-action confirmation (DS contract: <ConfirmDialog> replaces window.confirm)_ · `FieldRow remove button:550-553 (onRemove); CollectionSubFieldEditor removeCol:129,202-207`)
  - Clicking the ✕ button immediately deletes a field (or column) from the in-memory list with no confirmation and no undo. When editing an existing registry_def this is genuinely destructive — the file's own header notes PUT can return '409 destructive' because dropping a field affects stored records and dependent reports. A single mis-click silently queues data loss that only surfaces as a server 409 at save time.
  - → For removals while editing an existing def (isEdit), gate the remove behind <ConfirmDialog> (e.g. «Удалить поле „X“? Существующие записи могут потерять данные.»). At minimum provide an undo affordance. New (unsaved) fields can stay immediate.
- 🟡 **Hand-rolled raw <input>/<select>/<textarea> instead of kit <Field>/<Select>** (consistency · _DS contract: app-content inputs must use <Field>/<Select> wrappers_ · `FieldRow inputs/select:502-520, options textarea:571-580; CollectionSubFieldEditor inputs/select/textarea:166-226; related-app name input:641-647`)
  - The top-level field key/title inputs, the type <select>, and the related-app name input are raw <input>/<select className='chs-input'> with only aria-label, not the kit <Field>/<Select> primitives — even though the same component DOES use <Field>/<Select> elsewhere (slug/displayName meta inputs lines 922-939, rollup selects). This is the exact 'rolled-its-own raw input instead of <Field>/<Select>' flag in the contract. It bypasses the kit's label↔control binding, aria-invalid, and hint/aria-describedby wiring, and produces inconsistent invalid styling (manual chs-input--invalid vs kit invalid prop). Note: textarea/checkbox have no kit wrapper, so those are acceptable exceptions.
  - → Migrate the field key/title text inputs and the type select to <Field>/<Select> (visually-hidden label per column is fine for density). Keep one styling path for invalid state. Where a kit wrapper genuinely doesn't exist (textarea, checkbox), that's a kit gap, not a screen defect.
- 🟡 **Inline error messages not programmatically associated with their inputs** (accessibility · _WCAG 3.3.1 Error Identification / 1.3.1 — error text linked via aria-describedby_ · `FieldRow sub-row errors:560-563,581,617 (bare <span style={errStyle}>); aria-invalid set on inputs:508,528 but no aria-describedby`)
  - Field key/title/options inputs set aria-invalid but the corresponding error spans ('Ключ: …', 'Тип: …', options errors) live in a separate full-width sub-row with no id/aria-describedby linking them to the offending control. A screen-reader user hears 'invalid' but never the reason, and the spatial association (error in a row below) is invisible to AT. The kit <Field>/<Select> would wire aria-describedby automatically.
  - → Give each error span an id and reference it from the input via aria-describedby (and prefer aria-errormessage). Migrating these inputs to <Field>/<Select> (finding above) solves it for free.
- 🟡 **role="table" used on a div without row/cell roles — broken ARIA table** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 — ARIA grid/table role completeness_ · `FieldEditor:946 (div role="table" aria-label="Поля набора"); children are role="group" (FieldRow:500) and plain header div:948`)
  - The editor wraps field rows in role="table", but the header row and each FieldRow are NOT role="row"/role="columnheader"/role="cell" — FieldRow is role="group". An incomplete table role is worse than none: assistive tech announces 'table' then finds no rows/cells, so it reports an empty or malformed table. The contract also notes there is no <Table> primitive and tables are assembled ad hoc, so this is hand-rolled.
  - → Either drop role="table" (it's a form, as the file's own comment at lines 76-79 argues) and rely on the visible column header + per-control aria-labels, or complete the ARIA contract (role=row on each FieldRow, role=columnheader on header cells, role=cell on controls). Dropping it is simpler and matches the 'editor is a FORM, not a data table' intent.
- ⚪ **Required-field checkboxes rely on column position with no visible label** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / 3.3.2 Labels or Instructions_ · `FieldRow required checkbox:533-540; CollectionSubFieldEditor required:193-200; header 'Обяз.' span:955`)
  - The required toggle is a bare checkbox whose only label is aria-label='Обязательное поле'. Sighted users must infer meaning from the 'Обяз.' column header several rows up; on long field lists that header scrolls away and the bare checkbox column becomes ambiguous. Acceptable for AT, weak for sighted scannability in a dense editor.
  - → Keep the aria-label, but consider a sticky column header or a per-row tooltip/visually-compact label so the meaning survives scrolling. Low priority — purely scannability.
- ⚪ **Reorder arrows are bare text glyphs, inconsistent with KitIcon usage** (consistency · _DS principles: icons are linear Lucide-style currentColor (no ad-hoc glyphs); kit consistency_ · `FieldRow move buttons:546-549 (literal ↑ / ↓ characters); back-nav '← к приложениям':1079-1081`)
  - Up/down reorder buttons render literal Unicode arrow characters as their icon, while the adjacent remove button uses <KitIcon name="close" />. Mixing raw glyph 'icons' with KitIcon is the kind of inconsistency the design system warns against (icons should be Lucide-style currentColor via KitIcon). The '← к приложениям' back link similarly uses a literal arrow inline with text (more defensible as text, but still ad hoc).
  - → Use KitIcon for the up/down affordances (e.g. arrow-up / arrow-down) so all row controls share one icon source and sizing. They are not emoji, so this is a consistency nit, not a principle violation.
- ⚪ **Dev-jargon 'Слаг' leaks into the constructor UI** (content-clarity · _DS principles: no dev-jargon in UI_ · `FieldEditor:923 label 'Слаг набора полей'; col header:952 'Ключ'; placeholders 'field_key'/'col_key'/'my-registry'`)
  - 'Слаг' (slug) is developer jargon for a non-technical no-code author; combined with mono placeholders like 'field_key'/'my-registry' it asks a business user to author URL-safe identifiers. The contract explicitly bans dev-jargon in UI. 'Ключ' is more borderline but pairs with the same machine-identifier concept.
  - → Rename 'Слаг' to something user-facing (e.g. «Системное имя» with a hint) or auto-derive the slug from the display name and hide it behind an advanced toggle, so authors only name things in plain language.

### `src/screens/screen-apps.jsx` — 🟠3 🟡2 ⚪3
_Lists a tenant's constructor "applications" and lets the user create a new one via a modal form; rows link to records and a per-row menu reaches the field-constructor and records._

- 🟠 **Whole-row navigation is mouse-only — no keyboard access or focus** (accessibility · _WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard / 4.1.2 Name,Role,Value_ · `screen-apps.jsx:316-320 (<tr onClick={() => navigate(...)}>)`)
  - Each table row carries cursor:pointer and an onClick that navigates to /app-records/:id, but it is a bare <tr> with no tabIndex, no role, and no onKeyDown. Keyboard and screen-reader users cannot reach or trigger the primary navigation action; the row's clickability is also invisible to assistive tech. The only keyboard-reachable per-row affordance is the '…' menu, whose 'Записи' item happens to duplicate the row click — but a sighted keyboard user gets no signal the row itself is actionable.
  - → Don't make the <tr> the click target. Make the application name (first cell) a real link/Button to /app-records/:id so it is natively focusable and keyboard-activatable, and drop the row-level onClick + cursor:pointer (or keep row click purely as a redundant convenience). If you must keep the row clickable, add role="button", tabIndex={0}, an aria-label, and an onKeyDown handling Enter/Space.
- 🟠 **No success feedback after creating an application** (feedback-state · _Toast / transient-outcome surface choice_ · `screen-apps.jsx:251-255 (handleCreated) and 318 (highlight)`)
  - On a successful 201, the modal closes and the new row is tinted with --chs-color-success-soft via highlightId, but no toast confirms 'Приложение создано'. The contract mandates Toast for transient outcomes. Worse, highlightId is never cleared, so the green tint persists indefinitely (until reload) and becomes permanent visual noise rather than a transient cue — and if the new app sorts off-screen in a long list, the user gets no confirmation at all.
  - → Wire useToasts()+<ToastViewport> and fire a success Toast ('Приложение «{display_name}» создано') on 201. Make the row highlight genuinely transient: clear highlightId after ~2-3s with a setTimeout (cleaned up on unmount), and/or scroll the new row into view.
- 🟠 **Field-level validation errors are not programmatically associated with their inputs** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / 3.3.1 Error Identification; form-validation UX_ · `screen-apps.jsx:143, 154, 168 (error <span style={fieldErrStyle}>)`)
  - Slug, name, and description errors are rendered as standalone <span> siblings after the control. The inputs get invalid/aria-invalid, but nothing links the error text to the field via aria-describedby (and the spans have no id and no role). A screen-reader user hears 'invalid' but never the reason ('слаг занят', etc.). The contract states <Field> supports 'hint via aria-describedby' — here the error bypasses that mechanism and is rendered manually, so the wiring is lost.
  - → Pass the error text through <Field>'s own hint/error channel so it is exposed via aria-describedby and aria-invalid together, instead of a detached <span>. For the textarea, give the error a stable id and add aria-describedby referencing it. Ensure the error region is announced (e.g. role="alert" or part of an aria-live region) when validation fails.
- 🟡 **Description field is a hand-rolled <textarea> instead of a kit primitive** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `screen-apps.jsx:157-169`)
  - Slug and name use <Field>, but Описание is a raw <textarea> wired manually with chs-field/chs-label/chs-input classes and an inline style block (height/minHeight/padding/resize as literal values, e.g. minHeight:'64px'). This is inconsistent with the two fields above it: no shared hint slot, no shared invalid styling path, manual label↔control binding, and a hardcoded '64px' which violates the token-only rule. The contract confirms there is no <textarea> kit wrapper — so this is a real kit gap surfacing as ad-hoc inconsistency.
  - → Add a multiline variant to <Field> (e.g. <Field as="textarea" rows={3}>) or a <Textarea> kit primitive so the description matches slug/name in label binding, hint/error wiring, and styling. Replace the literal '64px' with a spacing/density token. Until the primitive exists, at minimum route its error through aria-describedby (see finding above).
- 🟡 **Status chip and tooltip render raw dev tier values ('draft'/'published') in a Russian UI** (content-clarity · _no dev-jargon in UI / microcopy localization_ · `screen-apps.jsx:330 (<StatusChip ... label={app.tier} />)`)
  - TIER_CHIP maps tier to a chip color, but the visible label is the raw English lowercase value app.tier ('draft' / 'published'). The rest of the screen is Russian ('черновик' is even used in the modal copy at line 129). Showing 'draft'/'published' in the chip is a dev-value leak and breaks the localized, jargon-free principle.
  - → Introduce a label map (draft → 'Черновик', published → 'Опубликовано') and pass the localized string to StatusChip's label, keeping app.tier only for the status/color lookup. Handle an unknown tier with a readable fallback rather than echoing the raw value.
- ⚪ **'…' ellipsis used as the actions-menu glyph instead of a Lucide icon** (consistency · _icon system (linear Lucide, currentColor)_ · `screen-apps.jsx:206 (Button children '…')`)
  - The per-row actions trigger uses a literal '…' character as its visible content. The design system specifies linear Lucide-style icons via currentColor; a bare ellipsis character is a typographic glyph, not an icon, so it renders inconsistently with the rest of the icon set (weight, alignment, sizing). The aria-label is correctly present, so this is purely a visual-consistency issue.
  - → Use <KitIcon name="more-horizontal" /> (or the kit's equivalent ellipsis/overflow icon) as the glyph, keeping the existing aria-label for the accessible name.
- ⚪ **401 UNAUTHENTICATED has no recovery path despite the documented 'войти' intent** (flow · _error recovery / affordance rule_ · `screen-apps.jsx:98-105 (create error mapping) and 240-246 (load)`)
  - The file header promises '401 — войти', but on a 401 the create flow only shows mapCreateError's generic message in the submitErr alert, with no actionable 'Войти' control; the list-load path turns any non-ok into a generic ErrorState('HTTP 401') with only Повторить, which will just fail again for an expired session. An enabled retry that can only re-fail violates the affordance/recovery expectation.
  - → Detect 401 specifically and surface a re-authenticate action (a 'Войти' button routing to login) in both the submit error and the list ErrorState, rather than a generic message or a retry that cannot succeed.
- ⚪ **Created-row highlight color is the only signal and relies on color alone** (feedback-state · _WCAG 1.4.1 Use of Color_ · `screen-apps.jsx:318 (background: var(--chs-color-success-soft))`)
  - The just-created application is distinguished solely by a green background tint. Color-only differentiation is not perceivable to colorblind/low-vision users and conveys no meaning to screen readers, and (per the feedback finding) it is also non-transient.
  - → Pair the highlight with a non-color cue (e.g. a brief 'новое' chip or an aria-live announcement) and make it time out. The recommended success Toast already covers the SR/announcement need.

### `src/screens/screen-forms.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡1 ⚪2
_A thin wrapper screen for the task-forms authoring area that switches between two surfaces via a tab strip: the primary drag-and-drop FormDesigner and a fallback field-binding FormBuilder._

- 🟠 **Incomplete ARIA tabs pattern: no tabpanel, no aria-controls, no keyboard roving** (accessibility · _WAI-ARIA APG Tabs / WCAG 4.1.2 Name,Role,Value; 2.1.1 Keyboard_ · `screen-forms.jsx:31-47 (role=tablist + role=tab buttons and the __canvas div)`)
  - The tab strip declares role=tablist with role=tab buttons and aria-selected, but the pattern is only half-implemented. The content container (__canvas, line 45) is not marked role=tabpanel, the tabs have no id, and there is no aria-controls linking each tab to its panel — so assistive tech cannot announce or navigate the tab/panel relationship. Critically, a role=tablist is expected (per WAI-ARIA APG) to support Left/Right arrow-key navigation with a single tab stop (roving tabindex); here every button is independently Tab-focusable and arrow keys do nothing, which is both non-conformant and inconsistent with how a tablist should feel. The automated AA check passed only because it cannot evaluate composite-widget keyboard semantics.
  - → Either (a) replace this hand-rolled tablist with a shared <Tabs>/<SubTabs> primitive (the contract notes none exists yet — the shell's bespoke RightsSubTabs is the closest prior art; extracting a real primitive would fix this everywhere), or (b) complete the pattern in place: give each tab an id and aria-controls pointing to a panel id; add role=tabpanel + aria-labelledby + tabIndex={0} to the __canvas div; implement roving tabindex (selected tab tabIndex=0, others -1) with Left/Right/Home/End arrow-key handlers.
- 🟡 **Tab buttons roll their own chs-btn markup instead of the kit (no Tabs primitive, ad-hoc assembly)** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `screen-forms.jsx:33-42`)
  - The tabs are raw <button> elements with manually concatenated class strings (`chs-btn chs-btn--ghost` + a hand-built `chs-btn--active` toggle). The design-system contract flags rolled-its-own <button> in app content and notes there is no <Tabs>/<SubTabs> primitive, so this tab strip is assembled ad hoc. The result diverges from how tabs render elsewhere (e.g. shell RightsSubTabs) and bypasses the focus-ring/aria handling the <Button> primitive centralizes. The active-state class is also string-concatenated inline, which is brittle.
  - → Extract or reuse a shared tabs primitive so this strip is not bespoke. If a primitive is out of scope for this file, at minimum stop hand-concatenating button classes and align the markup with the canonical sub-tab implementation in the shell so all tab strips look and behave identically.
