- ⚪ **Layout uses inline style objects instead of the BEM classes that already exist** (consistency · _tokens / styling convention_ · `screen-forms.jsx:31 (tabs) and 45 (canvas)`)
  - Both containers already carry BEM classes (chs-forms-screen__tabs, chs-forms-screen__canvas) yet duplicate their layout via inline style={{}} objects (display/gap/padding). The values do correctly consume --chs-space tokens (no hardcoded px, good), but putting layout in inline styles rather than the stylesheet splits the source of truth, defeats theme/media overrides, and is inconsistent with the class-driven styling the rest of the kit uses.
  - → Move the flex/gap/padding declarations into the .chs-forms-screen__tabs and .chs-forms-screen__canvas CSS rules (keeping the same --chs-space tokens) and drop the inline style props, so styling lives in one place.
- ⚪ **Tab label microcopy mixes English tech-jargon into a Russian UI and labels the mode opaquely** (content-clarity · _microcopy / no dev-jargon in UI_ · `screen-forms.jsx:23-24 (TABS labels)`)
  - The primary tab reads «Конструктор (drag-n-drop)» — 'drag-n-drop' is untranslated English developer jargon inside an otherwise Russian B2B UI. The fallback tab «Привязка полей (запасное)» labels itself 'запасное' (spare/backup) with no explanation of when or why a user would choose it, which is confusing as a primary navigation label.
  - → Use Russian phrasing, e.g. «Конструктор» (the drag-and-drop behaviour is self-evident on the canvas, or describe it as «перетаскивание» if needed) and rename the fallback to something user-meaningful like «Привязка полей» with the 'fallback' rationale surfaced as a hint/tooltip rather than baked into the tab label.

### `src/screens/screen-inbox.jsx` — 🟠4 🟡4 ⚪2
_Dense task-inbox table where assignees filter/sort tasks, claim pool tasks, open a detail drawer, fill a bound step form, and complete/approve workflow steps._

- 🟠 **Errors after claim/approve surfaced via window.alert() instead of the toast/state kit** (feedback-state · _Toast / feedback convention (kit useToasts/Toast)_ · `screen-inbox.jsx:634 (claimTask catch) and :659 (approveTask catch)`)
  - Both async mutations report failure with raw alert(e.message) (with eslint-disable no-alert). The design-system contract explicitly flags window.alert/confirm as a convention violation and provides Toast + useToasts() + ToastViewport. A blocking native alert is jarring, untokenized, not theme-aware, not screen-reader-polite-vs-assertive aware, and inconsistent with the ErrorState/Drawer error UI used elsewhere in this very file.
  - → Wire useToasts()+<ToastViewport/> into this screen and replace both alert() calls with an error toast (role=alert/assertive). Keep the already-good user-facing messages (e.g. «Задача уже взята другим пользователем»).
- 🟠 **Destructive/irreversible «Согласовать» has no confirmation** (ux-pattern · _Destructive-action confirmation (kit ConfirmDialog)_ · `screen-inbox.jsx:788-790`)
  - The primary «Согласовать» button approves a workflow step (POST action:'approve') directly from the table with a single click and no confirmation. Approval advances/closes the process instance and is not undoable from this UI. The contract ships <ConfirmDialog> precisely to replace silent commits of consequential actions; a one-click irreversible approval in a dense table invites mis-clicks.
  - → Gate the inline approve behind a <ConfirmDialog> (e.g. «Согласовать шаг и завершить задачу?»), or route approval only through the detail Drawer where the user sees full context before the primary action.
- 🟠 **Tabs use aria-selected but lack the tablist/tab/tabpanel ARIA roles and keyboard model** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2 Name,Role,Value; APG Tabs pattern_ · `screen-inbox.jsx:687-693`)
  - The filter tabs are bespoke <button className="chs-tab" aria-selected=...> inside a plain <div className="chs-tabs">. aria-selected is only meaningful on an element with role=tab inside a role=tablist; here there is no tablist container role, no role=tab, no role=tabpanel, and no arrow-key roving-tabindex. Screen readers announce these as plain buttons with an unexpected 'selected' state, and keyboard users get Tab-through-each instead of the expected arrow navigation. The contract notes there is no <Tabs> primitive, so this hand-roll must implement the roles itself.
  - → Either add role=tablist on the container, role=tab + roving tabindex + arrow-key handling on each button and associate a role=tabpanel; or, since these are really filter toggles not tabbed panels, drop aria-selected and model them as a single-select toggle group (radiogroup) with aria-checked. Pick one coherent semantic.
- 🟠 **SLA meter bar has no accessible text/role — countdown is visual-only** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.1.1 / 1.4.1 (non-text + use-of-color); progressbar semantics_ · `screen-inbox.jsx:290-295 (SLACell) and table cell :778`)
  - SLACell renders a fill bar plus a text span, but warn/over state is conveyed by class-driven color only (chs-sla__fill--warn/--over, chs-sla__txt--warn/--over). The bar itself is a decorative <span> with no role=progressbar / aria-valuenow / aria-valuetext, and the warn/over urgency is not exposed to assistive tech beyond the «−N мин» text. A color-blind or screen-reader user cannot perceive 'overdue vs warning vs normal' reliably.
  - → Give the meter role=progressbar with aria-valuemin/max/now and an aria-valuetext like «Просрочено на N мин» / «Осталось N мин (скоро дедлайн)», and pair the warn/over color with a non-color cue (icon or text label) so urgency is not color-only.
- 🟡 **No success feedback after claim or approve from the table row** (feedback-state · _Action feedback / optimistic UI_ · `screen-inbox.jsx:615-638 (claimTask), :641-663 (approveTask)`)
  - On success both handlers silently call load() and the row mutates/disappears. For «Согласовать» especially, the task vanishes from the list with no confirmation that the step was approved. The contract requires explicit outcomes; the Drawer path shows a «Шаг выполнен» notice, but the inline row path gives no positive confirmation, so the user is left guessing whether the irreversible action succeeded.
  - → Emit a success toast on claim («Задача взята») and approve («Шаг согласован») via the same useToasts() wiring, mirroring the Drawer's outcome notice.
- 🟡 **Row status marker conveys status by color alone** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.4.1 Use of Color_ · `screen-inbox.jsx:761 (chs-task__marker with inline MARKER_COLOR background)`)
  - The leading dot uses background: MARKER_COLOR[t.status] (info/success/danger/warning/faint) as the sole indicator of task status in the row, with no text, title, or aria-label. Status is therefore color-only and invisible to screen readers and hard for color-blind users. The contract's principle is shape+color dup with text (cf. executor glyphs).
  - → Add an accessible label to the marker (aria-label/title with the localized status, e.g. STATUS_LABEL[t.status]) or render a <StatusChip> so status is perceivable without color.
- 🟡 **Executor-type filter shows raw English codes (agent/human/service) in a Russian UI** (content-clarity · _Microcopy / no dev-jargon (design principle)_ · `screen-inbox.jsx:700 (`Тип: ${exec}`)`)
  - When a filter is active the button label interpolates the raw exec code — «Тип: agent», «Тип: human», «Тип: service». These are internal English enum values leaking into a Russian B2B UI, violating the 'no dev-jargon in UI' principle and the kit's executor vocabulary (●Человек/◆Агент/■Сервис).
  - → Map exec codes to localized labels (human→Человек, agent→Агент, service→Сервис), ideally reusing the kit ExecutorBadge/ExecGlyph label source so terminology stays consistent.
- 🟡 **Multi-state cycling filter is opaque and misuses aria-pressed** (ux-pattern · _Toggle vs cycle affordance; WCAG 4.1.2 (aria-pressed semantics)_ · `screen-inbox.jsx:695-701 (executor filter cycle)`)
  - One button cycles through four states (agent→human→service→null) on each click with aria-pressed reflecting only 'any filter set'. The user cannot see which state comes next or jump directly to a value, and a screen reader hears a toggle button whose pressed/label relationship is ambiguous across four states. aria-pressed is a binary on/off semantic, not a 4-way cycle.
  - → Replace the cycle with an explicit single-select control (kit <Select> or a small popover/radiogroup listing Все/Человек/Агент/Сервис) so the available values and current selection are visible and directly reachable; reserve aria-pressed for genuine binary toggles (the SLA sort).
- ⚪ **SLA sort toggle uses a bare arrow glyph as its only label** (content-clarity · _Button labeling / affordance clarity_ · `screen-inbox.jsx:702-708 («SLA ↑»)`)
  - The sort button's label is «SLA ↑» — the Unicode arrow is the sole indicator of sort direction/active state, and it never changes when toggled off, so the pressed state is conveyed only by aria-pressed/CSS. Meaning of the arrow (ascending headroom) is not obvious, and there is no accessible name beyond «SLA ↑».
  - → Give the button a clearer label (e.g. «Сортировать по SLA») and reflect the active/direction state both visually and via aria-label, rather than relying on a static arrow glyph.
- ⚪ **Form-collected state and the submit it gates are split across two buttons without clear linkage** (flow · _Form submission flow clarity_ · `screen-inbox.jsx:170-198 (InboxTaskForm «Готово») + :471-493 (formData notice) + footer «Выполнить шаг» :422-424`)
  - Inside the Drawer the user first submits the bound form with «Готово», which only stashes values into formData and shows a «Форма заполнена … Нажмите Выполнить шаг» notice; the actual step completion is a separate footer button «Выполнить шаг». This two-step (collect then commit) is non-obvious: «Готово» looks terminal but does nothing visible except a notice, and a user may stop there believing the task is done. There is also no way to re-open/edit the form once «заполнена».
  - → Either merge the two actions (let «Выполнить шаг» read+validate the form directly) or relabel «Готово» to something clearly intermediate (e.g. «Сохранить данные формы») and keep the notice; ensure the required-field validation that lives in «Готово» still runs when the footer action is pressed.

### `src/screens/screen-process-editor.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡4 ⚪4
_BPMN process editor: embeds the real bpmn-js modeler with a toolbar (undo/redo, load, validate, export, branch-rules, save, publish), a properties panel, and inline banners for validation and save/publish outcomes._

- 🟠 **Save/publish/error banners are not announced to screen readers (no role=status/alert)** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.3 Status Messages (AA)_ · `screen-process-editor.jsx:163-182 (StatusBanner root div), :116-146 (ValidationBanner root div)`)
  - StatusBanner and ValidationBanner render outcome text ("Черновик сохранён", "Опубликован", error messages, validation errors) in a plain <div>. The contract mandates Loading=role=status, Error=role=alert + Повторить, and toasts error=alert/assertive else status/polite. Here a keyboard/AT user gets no announcement after Save, Publish, Validate, or on error — the feedback is purely visual. The banners also auto-dismiss (5s/8s), so a non-sighted user may never perceive them at all.
  - → Add role="alert" aria-live="assertive" to the danger variants and role="status" aria-live="polite" to success/warning variants (or route these through the kit <Toast>/useToasts(), which already wires the correct roles). Ensure error content is announced before any auto-dismiss.
- 🟡 **Hand-rolled banner/toast system instead of kit <Toast>/useToasts()** (consistency · _design-system: Feedback primitives (Toast queue)_ · `screen-process-editor.jsx:149-183 (StatusBanner), :82-147 (ValidationBanner)`)
  - The contract provides <Toast>+<ToastViewport>+useToasts() for transient outcomes and explicitly flags hand-rolled feedback as a violation. This screen instead defines its own StatusBanner with a bespoke setTimeout auto-dismiss, raw <button> close, and ad-hoc danger/success class strings. This diverges from the rest of the app's feedback convention and duplicates queueing/dismiss logic the kit already owns. Only one status message can be shown at a time (new setStatusMsg overwrites the previous), so a save-then-validate sequence can drop feedback.
  - → Replace StatusBanner with useToasts()+<ToastViewport> for transient save/publish/export outcomes. Keep ValidationBanner inline only if persistent validation detail is intentional, but build it from kit ErrorState styling/tokens rather than bespoke classes.
- 🟡 **Error banners auto-dismiss, hiding actionable failure detail** (feedback-state · _error-message / notifications_ · `screen-process-editor.jsx:155-159 (setTimeout(onDismiss, isError ? 8000 : 5000))`)
  - Publish/save error messages (including lint violations and Flowable deploy failures, which can be long lists) auto-dismiss after 8 seconds. Destructive-to-recover errors that the user must read and act on should not disappear on a timer; 8s is often not enough to read a multi-line violations list, and once gone there is no history to recall it.
  - → Make error/danger banners persistent (dismiss only on user action or on the next successful action). Auto-dismiss is fine for success confirmations. If using the kit Toast, configure error toasts as non-auto-dismissing (assertive + manual close).
- 🟡 **Publish (deploys to live engine) has no confirmation step** (ux-pattern · _confirmation / destructive-action_ · `screen-process-editor.jsx:316-324 (Опубликовать button) → :625-676 (handlePublish)`)
  - "Опубликовать" runs save → lint → Flowable deploy and flips status to published — a consequential, externally-visible action (it deploys the process into the running engine). A single click with no confirm makes accidental publishes easy, especially since the button sits immediately next to "Сохранить". The contract supplies <ConfirmDialog> precisely for focus/confirm moments.
  - → Gate Опубликовать behind <ConfirmDialog> summarizing what will happen (deploy version N to the engine), at least when publishing over an already-published definition. Less critically, visually separate Опубликовать from Сохранить to reduce mis-clicks.
- 🟡 **Dirty-state tracked but no unsaved-changes guard on navigation/unload** (flow · _data-loss-prevention / unsaved-changes_ · `screen-process-editor.jsx:353 (isDirty state), :251-253 ("несохранённые изменения" indicator), :721 (navigate to branch-rules), :251`)
  - The editor knows when there are unsaved edits (isDirty) and even surfaces a "несохранённые изменения" label, but nothing prevents loss: navigating to the branch-rules editor, route changes, or a browser tab close/refresh will silently discard modeler changes. For a no-code modeling tool where a diagram represents real effort, silent loss is a significant UX failure.
  - → Add a beforeunload handler when isDirty, and a confirm/guard before in-app navigation (e.g. the Правила ветвления link and router transitions) when there are unsaved changes. React Router's useBlocker/prompt or a ConfirmDialog interstitial covers this.
- ⚪ **Busy state disables all toolbar actions but gives no per-button progress affordance** (feedback-state · _loading-indicators / button-loading_ · `screen-process-editor.jsx:259-324 (all toolbar Buttons share disabled={isBusy}); handleSave :522 / handlePublish :630 set a status banner only`)
  - During save/publish the only in-place signal is a banner reading "Сохраняю…"/"Публикую…" elsewhere on screen; the clicked button itself just goes flat-disabled with no spinner. The kit <Button> exposes a loading prop (handles aria-busy) that is unused here. Users may not connect the disabled toolbar to the action they triggered, and AT users get no aria-busy on the control.
  - → Pass loading={isBusy} (or a per-action flag) to the active Сохранить/Опубликовать <Button> so it shows the kit spinner and sets aria-busy, rather than relying solely on a remote banner.
- ⚪ **Process name/id derived from slug — risk of dev-jargon-looking labels and an unhelpful empty title** (content-clarity · _microcopy / no-dev-jargon_ · `screen-process-editor.jsx:384-386 (processName/processId derivation), :221 (MonoId fallback 'PRC-UNKNOWN'), :386 ('PRC-NEW')`)
  - When backend metadata is absent, the visible process name is generated by title-casing the URL slug (key.replace(/-/g,' ')...) and the id shown via MonoId falls back to 'PRC-UNKNOWN'/'PRC-NEW' / uppercased slug. The contract forbids dev-jargon/UUID/code-name leaks in UI; a raw uppercased slug and placeholder codes like PRC-UNKNOWN read as internal identifiers rather than human labels.
  - → Show a clean human title (e.g. "Новый процесс" until named) and avoid surfacing the raw slug as the primary name. If an id chip is needed, only render MonoId when a real, meaningful identifier exists; drop the 'PRC-UNKNOWN' placeholder.
- ⚪ **Undo/Redo are icon-only with the same glyph distinguished only by a CSS mirror** (ux-pattern · _icon-button / affordance-clarity_ · `screen-process-editor.jsx:226-248 (two chs-iconbtn buttons, both <Icon name="chevron"/>, first with transform: scaleX(-1))`)
  - Both Отменить and Повторить use the same chevron icon, differentiated only by a horizontal flip — a weak visual signifier for two opposite, consequential actions. aria-labels and title tooltips are present (so it is accessible and not a blocker), but visually the pair is ambiguous compared to conventional undo/redo curved-arrow glyphs.
  - → Use distinct, conventional undo/redo icons (e.g. Lucide undo-2/redo-2, currentColor) instead of a mirrored chevron, keeping the existing aria-labels.
- ⚪ **Inline color/size styles bypass design tokens** (consistency · _design-system: tokens (no hardcoded px)_ · `screen-process-editor.jsx:234 (style transform on undo btn), :683/:691 (inline flex+height centering on loading/error wrappers), :743 (inset:0 on wrapper)`)
  - Several inline style objects encode layout/transform directly in JSX rather than via CSS classes/tokens. While not raw hex colors, the contract favors token/class-driven styling; inline one-offs (centering, inset, transform) erode consistency and make theming/density changes harder.
  - → Move these into editor.css classes (e.g. a .chs-editor--centered state wrapper, an .chs-iconbtn--flip modifier) so layout stays in the stylesheet and consumes spacing/density tokens.

### `src/screens/screen-processes.jsx` — 🟠3 🟡2 ⚪4
_Dense table of running process instances plus a catalog section for process definitions and process-to-application trigger bindings (with a modal to configure triggers)._

- 🟠 **Raw <select> elements bypass the <Select> primitive** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `BindProcessModal, lines 353-362 (process), 371-380 (application), 389-399 (trigger type)`)
  - Three pickers are hand-rolled raw <select> + manual <label className="chs-label"> + chs-input classes + manual aria-invalid wiring, instead of the kit's <Select> primitive. The contract explicitly flags 'raw <select> without <Field>/<Select>' as a violation. This duplicates label-binding, aria-invalid, and hint(aria-describedby) logic that <Select> centralizes, and it has already drifted: the text fields in the same modal use <Field> (lines 407, 419) while the selects do not — an internally inconsistent form.
  - → Replace each raw <select> block with <Select label id value onChange options aria-invalid hint>. If <Select> does not yet support an error/hint slot, extend the primitive rather than re-implementing per-screen. This restores the single source of truth for label↔control binding and aria-describedby on the hint.
- 🟠 **Error hint is not programmatically associated with its select (missing aria-describedby)** (accessibility · _WCAG 3.3.1 Error Identification / 1.3.1 Info and Relationships_ · `BindProcessModal selects: lines 363-365, 381-383, 400-402; also the textarea hint at 444-450`)
  - Each invalid select sets aria-invalid but renders the error text in a sibling <span className="chs-hint--invalid"> with no id and no aria-describedby on the control. A screen-reader user lands on the field, hears 'invalid', but never hears WHY. The textarea's helper/error hint (lines 444-450) is likewise unlinked. The kit's <Field>/<Select> wire hint via aria-describedby — these hand-rolled controls drop that.
  - → Give each hint/error span a stable id and add aria-describedby={errId} to the corresponding select/textarea (and switch aria-invalid to the error id only when invalid). Adopting <Select>/<Field> does this automatically.
- 🟠 **Loading and error states are bare divs instead of state primitives** (feedback-state · _explicit states (LoadingState / ErrorState)_ · `ProcessesScreen lines 500-508; ProcessCatalogSection lines 137-143`)
  - The contract mandates <LoadingState> (role=status aria-busy) and <ErrorState onRetry> (role=alert + Повторить). Here both screens render plain centered <div>Загрузка процессов…</div> and a hand-built error <div><p>…</p><Button>Повторить</Button></div>. These carry no role=status/aria-busy on load and no role=alert on error, so assistive tech is not notified of the async outcome — and the empty state (line 510) correctly uses <EmptyState>, making the loading/error states the inconsistent ones on the very same screen.
  - → Swap the loading divs for <LoadingState> and the error divs for <ErrorState onRetry={load}/>. This adds role=status/aria-busy and role=alert, gives a consistent retry affordance, and removes ~3 ad-hoc blocks.
- 🟡 **Dev-jargon (raw process_key / instance id) leaks into user-facing columns** (content-clarity · _no dev-jargon in UI_ · `Definitions table 'Ключ' column (line 171); Bindings 'Процесс' column (line 235); instances 'Инстанс' column (line 560)`)
  - The contract bans UUIDs/code-keys in the UI. The bindings table shows ONLY the raw process_key as the process identity (<MonoId>{b.process_key}</MonoId>) with no human name, and the definitions table surfaces a 'Ключ' column. While MonoId styling signals 'this is technical', a no-code B2B operator reading the bindings table cannot tell which business process a binding refers to without memorizing keys.
  - → In the bindings table, render the definition's human name (resolved from definitions by process_key) as the primary cell with the key as secondary <MonoId> beneath, mirroring the instances row pattern (name + procId). Consider demoting the standalone 'Ключ' column or pairing it with the name.
- 🟡 **Raw timestamp risks 1970/ISO leakage with no formatting guard** (content-clarity · _no dev-jargon (1970-01-01 / raw timestamps)_ · `Instances table 'Запущен' column, line 605`)
  - inst.started is printed verbatim inside <Mono> with no formatting or null guard. If the backend ever returns an ISO string, epoch, or 1970-01-01 for an unstarted/seed row, it renders raw — exactly the leak the principles call out. There is no humanized relative/absolute date formatting here.
  - → Pass inst.started through a shared date formatter (humanized, locale-aware) and render an em-dash for null/epoch values, consistent with how the 'Форма' cell already guards empty with '—' (line 243).
- ⚪ **Field-mapping textarea has no <textarea>/code-input wrapper and weak affordance** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `BindProcessModal lines 434-443`)
  - The mapping textarea is a raw <textarea className="chs-input"> with inline monospace styling. The contract notes there is no textarea wrapper primitive (a known gap), so a raw element is unavoidable today — but the inline style props (fontFamily/fontSize) re-implement what a primitive should own, and there is no aria-describedby to the helper hint below it.
  - → Short term: add aria-describedby linking the helper hint and move the monospace styling into a reusable class. Longer term: flag the missing <Textarea> primitive so this and similar code-entry fields share one component.
- ⚪ **Hardcoded hex fallback in inline border style** (consistency · _tokens-only (no hardcoded color)_ · `ProcessCatalogSection wrapper, line 90`)
  - borderTop uses '1px solid var(--chs-border, #30333d)'. The var() fallback hardcodes a dark-theme hex. Per the contract, hardcoded color is a violation, and this specific fallback is a dark value that would render wrong on the primary light theme if the token ever fails to resolve. The correct border token is also --chs-color-border, not --chs-border.
  - → Use 'var(--chs-color-border)' with no hex fallback (the token is guaranteed by tokens.css). Audit the other 'var(--chs-color-text-muted, #888)' fallbacks (lines 150, 211, 218, 243) for the same reason.
- ⚪ **'Открыть' action routes the whole instance to a generic /audit page (ambiguous affordance)** (flow · _affordance clarity_ · `Instances table action, line 614`)
  - Every instance row's 'Открыть' button navigates to '/audit' with no instance context — the user expects to open THIS process instance's detail, but lands on a generic audit screen. The label promises a drill-in the destination does not deliver, weakening trust in the row action.
  - → Pass the instance id to the destination (e.g. /audit?instance={inst.id} or a dedicated /processes/{id} detail) so 'Открыть' opens the specific instance, or relabel to match the actual destination.
- ⚪ **Disabled primary button reasons are only exposed via title tooltip** (feedback-state · _affordance rule (disabled must explain)_ · `ProcessCatalogSection 'Настроить триггер' button, lines 119-133`)
  - When disabled (no defs or no apps), the only explanation of why the user cannot proceed lives in the title attribute, which is mouse-only and inaccessible to keyboard/touch/screen-reader users. The contract's affordance rule wants disabled states to communicate why, not silently block.
  - → Surface the reason as visible inline microcopy near the button (or as the section empty-state CTA copy) in addition to the tooltip, so keyboard and touch users learn they must first create a process/application.

### `src/screens/screen-record-detail.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡4 ⚪2
_Read-only detail card for a single registry record: renders schema-labeled field values, computed rollups, async-resolved relation links, a metadata sidebar, and lazy-loaded cross-app link sections._

- 🟠 **Raw UUIDs surfaced as primary UI content (dev-jargon leak)** (content-clarity · _principles.md: no dev-jargon (no UUIDs) in UI_ · `screen-record-detail.jsx:496-498 (record.id in Mono header); :611-614 (created_by as «Автор»); :394 (title=`Открыть запись ${targetId}`)`)
  - The record's bare UUID is rendered as the first visible element of the card, the «Автор» metadata field prints the raw created_by id (a UUID/sub, not a human name), and the relation link's tooltip exposes `Открыть запись <uuid>`. The design contract explicitly forbids UUIDs/dev-jargon in the UI. A B2B end-user sees an opaque 36-char string where they expect a human-meaningful identifier or author name.
  - → Resolve created_by to a display name (reuse the same actor-resolution used elsewhere) and fall back to «—» or «Система» when unknown, never the raw id. Demote or remove the top-of-card UUID (move it behind a copy-id affordance / «скоро» detail row, or drop it). For the relation tooltip, use the resolved record label, not the UUID.
- 🟠 **Panel-level cross-app links fetch error is silently swallowed** (feedback-state · _States always explicit — every list/detail renders Empty/Loading/Error_ · `screen-record-detail.jsx:238-244, :249, :252-254`)
  - On a network/parse failure in CrossAppLinksPanel, the catch sets loadError='fetch_error' but ALSO sets links=[]. The very next guard `if (!loading && (!links || links.length === 0)) return null` makes the whole panel disappear, so loadError is never rendered. A user whose linked-data fetch genuinely failed sees no «СВЯЗАННЫЕ ДАННЫЕ» section at all and cannot distinguish 'no links' from 'failed to load links' — and there is no retry. This contradicts the explicit-error-state convention.
  - → When loadError is set, render an ErrorState (or compact inline error with Повторить) instead of returning null. Only return null on a genuine empty result (HTTP ok, zero links). Add a retry that re-runs the fetch.
- 🟡 **Timestamps rendered in 1970-01-01 ISO / dev format** (content-clarity · _principles.md: no `1970-01-01`-style dev timestamps in UI_ · `screen-record-detail.jsx:36-41 (fmtTs) used at :600, :606`)
  - fmtTs formats every timestamp as `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`, the exact pattern the principles call out as a dev-jargon leak. A null/0/invalid timestamp falls to «—», but a present value reads like a machine log line rather than a localized human date (e.g. «29 июня 2026, 14:30»).
  - → Format dates with a Russian-locale Intl.DateTimeFormat (or the shared date helper if one exists) so «Создано/Обновлено» read as human dates. Keep Mono only for genuinely numeric/ID columns, not prose dates.
- 🟡 **Linked-section load error uses bare <p> instead of ErrorState, with no retry** (feedback-state · _ErrorState onRetry; role=alert convention_ · `screen-record-detail.jsx:155-159 (Не удалось загрузить данные), :160-164 and :171-174 (bare «—»)`)
  - Inside an expanded LinkedSection, a load error renders a plain muted `<p>Не удалось загрузить данные</p>` — no role=alert, no Повторить affordance. Empty/no-field cases render a bare «—» paragraph rather than the EmptyState primitive. The contract flags bare 'нет данных' / hand-rolled error text as a consumption violation.
  - → Use <ErrorState message=… onRetry=…> for the failure and <EmptyState> (or at minimum a labeled muted line like «Нет связанных данных») for the empty case, so screen readers announce them and users can recover.
- 🟡 **Hand-rolled disclosure button instead of kit primitive; hardcoded style values** (consistency · _consume design-system primitives; tokens-only (no raw px/weight)_ · `screen-record-detail.jsx:95-129 (style objects), :141-149 (bespoke <button>)`)
  - The collapsible section header is a bespoke <button> with an ad-hoc style object. While it correctly sets aria-expanded and type=button, it bypasses the kit and carries hardcoded non-token values: fontWeight:'500' (line 111), borderLeft:'2px solid' (115), fontStyle:'italic' (128), and opacity:0.7 (544). The contract requires --chs-weight-* and forbids hardcoded sizes; there is no <Tabs>/disclosure primitive, but a kit Button variant=ghost (or a documented bespoke pattern) would be more consistent. No visible focus styling is declared beyond browser default.
  - → Replace raw fontWeight:'500' with var(--chs-weight-medium), the 2px border with a border token, and italic/opacity decoration with muted text tokens. Either build the disclosure on <Button variant=ghost> or factor a shared <Disclosure>/<Section> so all collapsibles match. Ensure the focus ring uses --chs-color-focus-ring.
- 🟡 **Relation/computed value states leak raw italic placeholders and ambiguous «Нет доступа» microcopy** (content-clarity · _honest, explicit states; clear microcopy_ · `screen-record-detail.jsx:372-377 (loading «…»), :380-387 («— / Нет доступа»)`)
  - A loading relation renders a bare italic «…» with no aria-busy/role=status, so assistive tech announces nothing and sighted users see an ambiguous ellipsis. The resolved-failure state «— / Нет доступа» conflates three distinct cases — dangling reference (target deleted), permission denied (403), and transient fetch error — under one label, so the user cannot tell whether to request access or whether data is simply gone.
  - → Give the loading span role=status/aria-busy (or use a tiny Skeleton). Split the failure copy: «Нет доступа» only for 403, «Запись удалена» / «—» for 404, and a retryable «Не удалось загрузить» for transient errors. Mirror this taxonomy with the CrossAppLinksPanel denied sentinel for consistency.
- ⚪ **Object/array field values dumped as raw JSON.stringify** (content-clarity · _no dev-jargon; human-readable values_ · `screen-record-detail.jsx:518-520 (fallback branch)`)
  - In the no-schema fallback branch, object/array values are rendered via JSON.stringify(val), producing developer-facing `{"k":"v"}` blobs in a B2B record card. This only triggers on schema-less records but still exposes machine output to end-users.
  - → Render structured values with a readable formatter (e.g. key/value sub-rows or a comma-joined summary), or show «—»/«(структурированные данные)» rather than a raw JSON string.
- ⚪ **«Назад к списку» uses a literal ← arrow glyph as an icon** (consistency · _principles.md: icons are linear Lucide currentColor, not text glyphs/emoji-as-icon_ · `screen-record-detail.jsx:455-457 (header), :473 area uses kit Button text only`)
  - The back button label is the literal string «← Назад к списку», using a Unicode arrow as a pseudo-icon. The contract specifies Lucide-style icons via currentColor (and Button supports a `glyph` prop); a bare ← is inconsistent with the icon system and renders at text weight/baseline rather than as an aligned icon.
  - → Use the Button `glyph` prop with a KitIcon arrow-left (e.g. <Button variant=ghost glyph={<KitIcon name="arrow-left"/>}>Назад к списку</Button>) so the affordance matches the kit icon treatment.

### `src/forms/Floor2Sandbox.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡4 ⚪1
_Sandboxed iframe host that mounts a flagged "class-B" custom code widget inside a form document, isolating it via an opaque-origin iframe and falling back to a governance notice when the widget is not approved._

- 🟠 **Dev-jargon leak: spec section «§9.10» shown to end users** (content-clarity · _principles.md: no dev-jargon in UI (no ticket IDs, code-names, spec refs)_ · `Floor2Sandbox.jsx:129 (governance notice text)`)
  - The unflagged fallback renders «требуется явный флаг (§9.10).» directly to the user. «§9.10» is an internal spec reference and «явный флаг» is implementation vocabulary — meaningless to an internal business user and an explicit violation of the no-dev-jargon principle. The user is told something is blocked but gets no actionable path.
  - → Rewrite in plain Russian with a next step, e.g. «Этот код-виджет ещё не проверен и не может быть показан. Обратитесь к администратору, чтобы подтвердить его.» Drop the «§9.10» reference entirely (or move it to a data-attribute / console for developers).
- 🟡 **Governance fallback rolls its own alert instead of <ErrorState>/<EmptyState> primitive** (consistency · _components.jsx state primitives: <ErrorState onRetry>, <EmptyState> — 'States always explicit' / use kit not bespoke_ · `Floor2Sandbox.jsx:127-131 (the !flagged branch <div role="alert">)`)
  - The blocked-widget state is a hand-rolled <div role="alert"> with inline styles rather than the kit's <ErrorState>/<EmptyState>. The contract flags bespoke state blocks as a consistency violation; this also means no consistent icon, CTA, or heading/body structure with the rest of the app.
  - → Render the kit primitive (e.g. <ErrorState> with a Lucide warning glyph and a clear title/body, or <EmptyState> with a CTA to request approval) instead of a custom div. If no CTA is possible, still use the primitive for visual/structural consistency.
- 🟡 **Hardcoded color and border in the governance notice instead of semantic tokens** (consistency · _tokens.css: hardcoded color/size = violation; warning surfaces use --chs-color-warning-soft / -border_ · `Floor2Sandbox.jsx:128 (border: '1px dashed var(--chs-color-warning)')`)
  - Using --chs-color-warning (a strong signal color) directly as a 1px dashed border for a passive notice is an off-pattern consumption of a status token — warning surfaces in the system use the -soft/-border variants for backgrounds/borders. The dashed border is also not a system pattern, and there is no surface fill, so the block reads as a stray dev placeholder rather than a designed warning state.
  - → Use the warning soft surface set (e.g. background var(--chs-color-warning-soft) + border var(--chs-color-warning-border)) or simply adopt <ErrorState> which already encodes the correct warning treatment. Avoid the bespoke dashed border.
- 🟡 **Active iframe has no loading or error fallback state** (feedback-state · _principles.md: 'States always explicit — every list/detail renders Empty/Loading/Error, never bare emptiness'_ · `Floor2Sandbox.jsx:134-147 (flagged iframe render) and buildSandboxSrcdoc catch at :81`)
  - When flagged, the host mounts the iframe and shows nothing until the widget posts its height (FRAME_MIN_HEIGHT placeholder, no spinner/skeleton). If the widget's user code throws, the srcdoc shows bare English text «widget error» inside the frame — an unstyled, untranslated, non-actionable error with no escape to the parent UI. The parent never learns of the in-frame failure (onError is only called for the flag gate, not runtime widget errors).
  - → Show a <LoadingState>/<Skeleton> over the iframe until the first valid height message arrives. For in-widget failures, post an error message back over the same channel and surface a kit <ErrorState> (localized, with retry) in the parent instead of the inline English 'widget error' string.
- 🟡 **Sandboxed widget body uses hardcoded font and color (#222), bypassing the theme** (consistency · _tokens.css + principles.md: both themes WCAG AA, light theme primary, --chs-* tokens only; --chs-font-sans is Golos Text_ · `Floor2Sandbox.jsx:76 (srcdoc <style> body{...color:#222} and font-family:system-ui)`)
  - The iframe content is hardcoded to system-ui / 14px / #222 on a transparent (effectively white) background. Because the iframe is opaque-origin it cannot inherit parent tokens, but the host still ships fixed values: the widget will not match Golos Text typography and, in dark theme, #222-on-unset will render dark-on-dark or as an unreadable light-fallback — exactly the 'unpropagated nodes must fall to readable light' concern, here unhandled.
  - → Inject the resolved theme tokens into the srcdoc as inline CSS variables (read computed --chs-color-text, --chs-color-bg, --chs-font-sans from the parent and pass them into the <style>), and set an explicit background so dark-theme contrast stays AA. At minimum pin a readable bg/fg pair rather than relying on the host page default.
- ⚪ **Inline styles throughout instead of class/token-driven styling** (consistency · _design-system consumption: prefer tokenized classes over inline style objects_ · `Floor2Sandbox.jsx:128 and :144 (inline style props on notice and iframe)`)
  - Both the notice and the iframe use inline style objects. While they reference some tokens, inline styles bypass the .chs-floor2-* class hooks already present (className is set but unused for layout), making theming and overrides inconsistent with the rest of the component library.
  - → Move static styling into the .chs-floor2-flag / .chs-floor2-sandbox CSS classes using tokens; keep only the truly dynamic value (height) inline.

### `src/forms/Floor2Viewer.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡4
_Renders one agent-authored Floor-2 custom widget inside an opaque sandbox iframe over a named-binding field, with validation error and loading states._

- 🟠 **Hardcoded hex color and non-existent token in error text** (consistency · _design-token contract (color)_ · `Floor2Viewer.jsx:120`)
  - The error <div> uses style={{ color: 'var(--color-error, #c00)' }}. '--color-error' is not a CHOROS token (the contract's semantic danger token is '--chs-color-danger'), so the var() resolves to nothing and the hardcoded fallback '#c00' is always applied. The contract states hardcoded color = violation and that raw/non-semantic tokens must not be consumed. This also bypasses WCAG-AA-verified theme colors, risking insufficient contrast in light theme.
  - → Replace with the kit's <ErrorState> primitive (which already carries danger styling). If keeping inline, use color: 'var(--chs-color-danger)' and remove the hardcoded #c00 fallback.
- 🟠 **Error and loading states rolled by hand instead of kit primitives (no retry affordance)** (feedback-state · _explicit states / ErrorState + LoadingState primitives_ · `Floor2Viewer.jsx:114-131`)
  - Both the validation-error branch (a bare <div role=alert>) and the loading branch (a bare aria-busy <div>) are bespoke. The contract mandates <ErrorState onRetry> and <LoadingState> primitives and lists 'bare loading/error instead of state primitives' as a flagged violation. The hand-rolled error gives the user no recovery path — a descriptor that fails validation is a dead end with no Повторить / retry and no way back, violating the 'states always explicit' and ErrorState-has-onRetry conventions.
  - → Render <ErrorState> with a Russian message and an onRetry that re-runs descriptor validation (or a 'обновить' affordance), and <LoadingState> for the pending branch. This also fixes the danger-color and spacing issues for free.
- 🟡 **Hardcoded spacing and font-size instead of scale tokens** (consistency · _design-token contract (spacing/typography)_ · `Floor2Viewer.jsx:120`)
  - The error div inlines padding: '8px' and fontSize: '0.875rem'. The contract requires spacing from '--chs-space-*' and sizes from '--chs-text-*' (base 13px); hardcoded px/rem = violation. 0.875rem (~14px) is also not on the token scale and won't track theme/density changes.
  - → Use padding: 'var(--chs-space-4)' (8px) and fontSize: 'var(--chs-text-sm)' — or, preferably, drop the inline style entirely by rendering the <ErrorState> primitive.
- 🟡 **Loading state is a silent blank box (no role=status, no label)** (feedback-state · _Loading = role=status aria-busy_ · `Floor2Viewer.jsx:130`)
  - The loading branch renders <div aria-busy='true'> with only a min-height — no role='status', no visible spinner/skeleton, and no text. The contract specifies Loading = 'role=status aria-busy'. As written, sighted users see an empty gap and screen-reader users get no announced 'loading' status; it reads as broken/empty rather than pending.
  - → Use the <LoadingState> or <Skeleton> primitive (which sets role='status' and shows a visible affordance). At minimum add role='status' and an aria-label/visually-hidden 'Загрузка…'.
- 🟡 **No distinct empty state — missing descriptor renders as perpetual loading** (feedback-state · _explicit Empty vs Loading states_ · `Floor2Viewer.jsx:83-86, 129-131`)
  - When descriptor or fields is null, useMemo returns null and the component falls into the loading branch with aria-busy='true' indefinitely. A genuinely-absent descriptor (nothing to render) is conflated with 'still loading', so the UI claims to be busy forever. The contract requires every list/detail to render an explicit Empty state, never conflated with loading or bare emptiness.
  - → Distinguish the cases: if descriptor/fields are absent, render an <EmptyState> (e.g. 'Нет данных для отображения' with appropriate CTA/context); reserve aria-busy loading for an actual in-flight build.
- 🟡 **Internal code-names and identifiers leak into user-facing error and accessible names** (content-clarity · _no dev-jargon / no code-names-UUIDs in UI_ · `Floor2Viewer.jsx:122-123, 143, 151`)
  - The error text 'Floor-2 descriptor error:' plus raw errors[].message (technical validator strings) is shown to end users; and the iframe title/aria-label embed 'Floor-2 component', the internal mode, and bindingKey (a code-level binding identifier). The contract bans dev-jargon, code-names, and UUID/identifier leaks in the UI — and accessible names are UI surfaces, so screen-reader users hear 'Floor-2 component: <key> (custom)'. 'Floor-2', 'descriptor', 'binding key', and 'mode' are all internal vocabulary.
  - → Use a human, Russian, jargon-free message for the error (e.g. 'Не удалось отобразить элемент формы') and log the technical details separately. For title/aria-label, use the field's human label rather than the raw bindingKey and drop 'Floor-2'/mode from the user-visible string (e.g. aria-label={`Поле: ${fieldLabel}`}).

### `src/forms/FormBuilder.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡5 ⚪2
_An admin tool to derive a form schema from an application's registry fields and bind it to a process step (configure which fields appear, their labels, required flags, and order, then save the binding)._

- 🟠 **Raw <input type=checkbox> and <input type=text> instead of kit primitives** (consistency · _Design-system primitive consumption (Field / Checkbox)_ · `FieldConfigRow, lines 111-116 (include checkbox), 146-154 (label override input), 167-173 (required checkbox)`)
  - The configurator row rolls its own raw <input type="checkbox"> and a bare <input className="chs-input"> with only an aria-label, bypassing the kit. The contract requires app-content inputs to use <Field>/<Select>; it explicitly notes there is no checkbox wrapper, so each raw checkbox is unstyled, gets no focus-ring/aria treatment from the kit, and depends on a hand-maintained className. This is the dominant interactive surface of the screen and the largest consistency gap.
  - → Use <Field> for the label-override text input (it already exists and gives label binding, aria-invalid, hints). For the checkboxes, either add a <Checkbox> primitive to components.jsx (the contract flags its absence as a real gap) and consume it, or at minimum ensure a kit-provided focus-ring class is applied. Do not depend on bare 'chs-input' for app content.
- 🟠 **Field-level error text not programmatically linked to its input (aria-describedby)** (accessibility · _WCAG 3.3.1 Error Identification / 1.3.1 Info and Relationships_ · `lines 548-552 (processKey error), 568-572 (stepKey error), 661-665 (registry error)`)
  - The <Field> gets invalid={...} (aria-invalid) but the matching error <span> is a sibling with no id and no aria-describedby pointing to it. A screen-reader user hears 'invalid' but never the reason ('Укажите ключ процесса'). The kit <Field> supports hint-via-aria-describedby, but the error is rendered as a detached span outside it.
  - → Pass the error message through the <Field>'s hint/error channel so aria-describedby links control→message, or give each error span an id and add aria-describedby on the Field. Apply the same to the registry-def error which is attached to a hand-rolled chs-field/chs-label/select block.
- 🟡 **Save errors shown only as inline text, never reach assertive feedback** (feedback-state · _Toast/feedback convention; WCAG 4.1.3 Status Messages_ · `handleSave lines 462-470; render lines 738-750`)
  - The save outcome (success and error) is rendered as an inline <span role="status" aria-live="polite">. The contract says transient action outcomes belong in a Toast, and errors should be assertive (role=alert/aria-live=assertive). A polite live region for a failed save can be missed, and the inline span sits next to the button — easy to overlook on a long scrolled form. There is no toast wiring (useToasts/ToastViewport) at all.
  - → Route save outcomes through useToasts(): success → status/polite toast ('Форма обновлена'), failure → alert/assertive toast. Keep an inline confirmation if desired, but the failure path especially must be assertive so it is announced and visible regardless of scroll position.
- 🟡 **Arrow glyphs ↑ ↓ used as icons instead of Lucide currentColor icons** (consistency · _Principles: icons are linear Lucide-style currentColor, no glyph/emoji-as-icon_ · `FieldConfigRow reorder buttons, lines 189 and 199`)
  - The reorder controls use literal Unicode arrows '↑'/'↓' as button content. The contract mandates Lucide-style linear icons via currentColor and forbids glyph-as-icon (it lists ✓/✗ as examples). Unicode arrows render inconsistently across platforms/fonts and break visual consistency with KitIcon used elsewhere in this same file (line 721).
  - → Replace the text arrows with <KitIcon name="arrow-up" />/<KitIcon name="arrow-down" /> (or chevron-up/down) passed via the Button's glyph prop, keeping the existing aria-label.
- 🟡 **Dev-jargon and raw identifiers leaked into user-facing copy** (content-clarity · _Principles: no dev-jargon in UI (no code-names, raw keys, ticket IDs)_ · `Field labels/placeholders lines 505-509 ('Ключ процесса', placeholder 'telLinear'), 558-562; FieldConfigRow line 132 renders raw field.key in mono`)
  - User-facing strings expose internal concepts: 'Ключ процесса' with placeholder 'telLinear' (a code-name key), and the configurator's primary column shows the raw machine field.key (e.g. 'counterparty_inn') rather than a human title. For an internal no-code tool this is borderline, but the contract is explicit about not leaking raw keys/code-names as the primary user identifier.
  - → Lead with the human title (field.title) as the primary column and show field.key secondarily/mono as a subtitle. For the process picker, prefer selecting from the catalog (process name) and de-emphasize the raw key field; avoid a code-name as the visible placeholder example.
- 🟡 **stepKey is unconstrained free text that must exactly match an opaque runtime value** (flow · _Forms: prevent invalid input / match-the-source_ · `Field 'Шаг процесса' lines 556-578; validation only checks non-empty (line 402)`)
  - The step key is free text the user must type to exactly match the BPMN userTask step identifier (hint: 'Совпадает со значением поля «Шаг» в карточке задачи инбокса'). A typo silently binds the form to a non-existent step with no feedback — validation only checks that it is non-empty, so a mistyped step passes and the binding is dead. This is the highest functional-correctness UX risk on the screen.
  - → Once a process is chosen, fetch its userTask step keys and offer them as a picker/datalist (like the process quick-pick already does), or validate the typed step against the known steps and warn when it matches none. Free text should be a fallback, not the only path.
- 🟡 **Inconsistent state-primitive usage: catalog and empty-defs use ad-hoc spans, app/defs use kit states** (feedback-state · _States always explicit via kit (LoadingState/ErrorState/EmptyState)_ · `catalog loading/error lines 512-526 (bare spans + raw <button>); empty registryDefs line 643-646 (bare span); vs. apps/defs which correctly use LoadingState/ErrorState (lines 591-606, 627-642)`)
  - The screen mixes conventions. The process-catalog load uses a plain 'Загрузка процессов…' span and an error rendered as text with a hand-rolled <button> 'Повторить' (styled inline, not the kit Button), while the application and registry-def loads correctly use <LoadingState>/<ErrorState onRetry>. The 'no registry defs' case is a bare span ('Нет наборов полей…') rather than <EmptyState>. This is exactly the inconsistent-consumption failure the contract warns about.
  - → Use <ErrorState onRetry={loadCatalog}> for the catalog failure (its retry already exists) and <EmptyState> for the empty registry-defs list. Replace the inline 'Повторить' <button> with the kit Button (variant ghost/sm) to match the kit retry affordance.
- ⚪ **Retry logic for apps/defs duplicated inline instead of reusing the load callback** (consistency · _Maintainability of feedback/retry affordance_ · `ErrorState onRetry handlers lines 596-605 (apps) and 632-641 (defs)`)
  - The onRetry handlers re-implement the full fetch/parse/error pipeline inline, duplicating the effect bodies at lines 286-291 and 311-315. Divergence here would silently produce a retry that behaves differently from the initial load (different error mapping or reset), an easy source of inconsistent feedback over time.
  - → Extract loadApplications() and loadDefs(selectedAppId) as useCallback functions (mirroring loadCatalog) and call them from both the effect and the onRetry, so the retry path is byte-identical to the initial load.
- ⚪ **Disabled-but-styled rows reduce scannability without an explicit affordance cue** (ux-pattern · _Affordance / state clarity_ · `FieldConfigRow line 106 (opacity 0.45 when excluded); label-input disabled line 153`)
  - Excluded fields are conveyed only by opacity:0.45 on the whole row and a disabled label input. Opacity alone is a weak, easily-missed signal and can drop contrast on the dimmed text below AA for the secondary type line. Color/opacity is the sole differentiator of the included vs excluded state.
  - → Pair the dimming with a non-opacity cue (e.g. the checkbox state is already there, but add a subtle 'выключено' text marker or strike on the key, and keep the type-label text at readable contrast). Verify the dimmed secondary text still meets AA (4.5:1) in light theme.

### `src/forms/FormDesigner.jsx` — 🔴1 🟠2 🟡5 ⚪3
_A three-pane drag-n-drop form-layout builder where an author picks an app/field-set, drags schema fields and layout blocks onto a canvas, edits block properties, previews, and persists the form layout._

- 🔴 **Canvas node is a clickable/draggable <div> — not keyboard operable** (accessibility · _WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:90-122 (CanvasNode) — <div className="chs-canvas-node" onClick={() => onSelect(index)}>`)
  - Each canvas block is a <div> carrying onClick (select), draggable, onDragStart/onDragOver/onDrop. It has no role, no tabIndex, and no keydown handler, so a keyboard-only user cannot select a block, reorder it, or even reach it. This is the core interaction of the builder, so the whole authoring surface is keyboard-inaccessible. Both MCP tools flagged this (check_accessibility: critical 2.1.1; review_code: non-semantic click handler).
  - → Make the node selectable via a real control: either render the node header as a <button> for selection, or give the div role="button" tabIndex={0} aria-pressed={selected} with an onKeyDown handling Enter/Space. Provide a non-drag reorder affordance (e.g. Up/Down buttons or keyboard move) since native HTML5 drag-and-drop has no keyboard equivalent — drag-only reordering fails 2.1.1 regardless.
- 🟠 **Drag-and-drop is the only way to add/reorder blocks — no keyboard or fallback path** (flow · _drag-and-drop / accessible alternative_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:100-103, 295-308, 365-369 (canvas onDrop, CanvasNode drag, palette draggable)`)
  - Reordering root nodes is exclusively drag-driven (application/x-reorder). Palette/field add has an onClick fallback (good), but reorder has none. Keyboard users, touch users on some browsers, and users with motor impairments cannot reorder. There is also no drop-target indication (no insertion line/hover state), so even mouse users get no feedback about where a block will land.
  - → Add explicit move-up/move-down controls per CanvasNode (reusing the reorderNode op) as the accessible reorder path. Add a visible drop indicator (insertion marker / highlighted target) during dragover so placement is predictable.
- 🟠 **Raw <input>/<textarea> in Inspector bypass the <Field> primitive and break label↔control binding** (consistency · _forms/text-field (label association)_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:137-148 — <label className="chs-label">Метка</label> + bare <input className="chs-input">; same for Текст <textarea>`)
  - The design-system contract mandates <Field> for inputs (label↔control binding, aria-invalid, hint via aria-describedby). Here labels are plain <label> with no htmlFor and the inputs have no id/aria-labelledby, so they are programmatically unlabeled — clicking the label does not focus the control and screen readers announce an unnamed textbox. review_code flagged 'Missing Input Label'. This also hardcodes className="chs-input" instead of consuming the kit. (Textarea has no kit wrapper per the contract's noted absence, but it still needs a bound label.)
  - → Use <Field> for the 'Метка' text input. For the textarea, until a kit wrapper exists, bind the label explicitly (htmlFor + matching id, or wrap the control in the label) so the association is programmatic. Drop the hand-rolled .chs-input styling in favor of the primitive.
- 🟡 **Palette and field items are rolled-their-own <button>s instead of <Button>** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:49-66 (PaletteBlock), 69-83 (FieldChip), 112-117 (remove button)`)
  - These are app-content buttons (add block, add field, delete block) hand-rolled as <button className="chs-btn chs-btn--ghost"> with inline styles, not the kit <Button>. The contract explicitly flags this: only bespoke shell nav buttons may roll their own; app-content buttons must use <Button>. Hand-rolling means focus-ring/aria-busy/variant handling drift from the system. The remove control also uses a literal '×' glyph as an icon, where the contract calls for linear Lucide icons via currentColor (no glyph-as-icon).
  - → Use <Button variant="ghost"> (with glyph prop) for palette/field/remove actions, layering the draggable attributes on top. Replace the '×' with a Lucide-style X icon passed via the Button glyph prop; keep aria-label="Удалить блок".
- 🟡 **Successful save feedback is a static paragraph that never clears and uses a hardcoded color** (feedback-state · _feedback after action / Toast convention_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:427-428 — <p style={{color:'var(--chs-color-success)'}}>Сохранено.</p> and the error <p>`)
  - After save, 'Сохранено.' / error text renders inline in the inspector and persists indefinitely (no timeout, not announced). The contract designates Toast for transient outcomes (status/polite on success, alert/assertive on error). As inline text it is easy to miss (it's in a side panel below the fold), is not role=status/alert so screen readers may not announce it, and never resets — a later edit leaves a stale 'Сохранено.' visible while the doc is now dirty.
  - → Emit outcomes via useToasts(): success → status/polite toast, error → alert/assertive toast (the file already imports nothing for this; wire ToastViewport+useToasts at this tree). At minimum, give the inline messages role=status / role=alert and clear them when the document changes after save.
- 🟡 **Save error message swallows the server's reason — generic 'Не удалось сохранить.' with no retry** (feedback-state · _error recovery_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:438-453 (persistLayout) and 428 (error render)`)
  - persistLayout catches every failure into a single hardcoded 'Не удалось сохранить.' regardless of cause (network vs 4xx validation vs 5xx). The author gets no actionable detail and no retry affordance; the contract's ErrorState pattern pairs errors with Повторить. The Сохранить button re-enables, so retry is technically possible, but there is no guidance and server-side validation rejections are indistinguishable from transient network errors.
  - → Surface the server's error payload when present (e.g. read r.json() on !ok and show the message), distinguish validation rejections from transient failures, and offer an explicit Повторить affordance (toast action or inline retry button) consistent with ErrorState.
- 🟡 **Load errors are terminal and unrecoverable — ErrorState with no onRetry, and second fetch error overwrites the canvas** (feedback-state · _error recovery / loading states_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:215-230 (fetch .catch → setLoadError), 311 (return <ErrorState message={loadError}>)`)
  - Both the applications fetch and the registry-defs fetch funnel into a single loadError that short-circuits the entire component (return <ErrorState>). The contract's ErrorState supports onRetry, which is not passed, so a failed load is a dead end requiring a full page reload. Worse, a registry-defs failure mid-session replaces the whole working canvas (including an in-progress layout) with a full-screen error. There is also no loading state while registry-defs / schema are fetching after an app is picked.
  - → Pass onRetry to <ErrorState> to re-run the failed fetch. Scope the registry-defs error to its own region (an inline error in the palette) rather than blowing away the canvas. Add a LoadingState/Skeleton while registry-defs and the derived schema load after app selection.
- 🟡 **Validation errors render as a flat bullet list with no link back to the offending block** (feedback-state · _form validation UX_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:390-395 — <div role="alert"> ... validation.errors.map(e => <li>{e.message}</li>)`)
  - validateDocument returns errors and brokenKeys, but the UI only prints e.message as plain bullets at the bottom of the canvas. The author cannot tell which block each error refers to, and there is no inline marking on the offending CanvasNode (brokenKeys is computed but unused in the canvas). For a builder with many blocks this makes fixing validation failures a hunt. role="alert" on a list that re-renders on every keystroke also means the whole list is re-announced constantly, which is noisy for screen-reader users.
  - → Highlight the specific failing node(s) inline (use brokenKeys to mark the CanvasNode, e.g. danger border + per-node message) and make each error item focus/scroll to its block. Consider role="status" (polite) for the aggregate, reserving assertive announcements for on-submit so it isn't re-fired on every edit.
- ⚪ **Live preview is hidden inside a collapsed <details> with no styling/affordance** (ux-pattern · _progressive disclosure_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:398-405 — <details><summary>Предпросмотр</summary>`)
  - The whole value proposition of the file ('the live preview, identical to runtime') is buried in a default-collapsed native <details>/<summary> with no kit styling, no expand affordance icon, and no indication it updates live. Authors building a layout benefit from seeing the result; defaulting to collapsed and using an unstyled native disclosure undersells it and is visually inconsistent with the rest of the kit-styled UI.
  - → Consider showing the preview by default (or as a side-by-side pane), and if disclosure is kept, style the summary with a chevron affordance and a hint that it reflects runtime. Ensure the native <details> matches kit typography/spacing.
- ⚪ **Field 'used' state communicated by opacity alone (0.5) — color/contrast-only signal** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.4.1 Use of Color_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:74 (FieldChip) — opacity: used ? 0.5 : 1`)
  - Whether a schema field is already placed on the canvas is conveyed only by dimming the chip to 50% opacity. There is no text, icon, or aria cue, so the 'already used' state is invisible to screen-reader users and easy to miss for low-vision users; dimmed text can also drop below the AA contrast threshold.
  - → Pair the dimming with an explicit signal — e.g. an 'добавлено' label/checkmark icon and aria-disabled or a visually-hidden 'уже добавлено' note — so the state is non-visual-accessible and contrast-safe.
- ⚪ **Inspector option values leak raw widget/mode tokens as user-facing labels** (content-clarity · _no dev-jargon in UI_ · `FormDesigner.jsx:163 — options={widgetChoices.map(w => ({ value: w, label: w }))}; also fieldKey shown raw at 120, custom componentId 'custom-widget'`)
  - The 'Виджет' Select renders the raw internal widget identifiers (e.g. WIDGET_COMPAT tokens) directly as labels, and the canvas shows raw fieldKey ('↳ key') values. The contract bans dev-jargon/identifiers in the UI. Internal slugs as choices/labels are harder to read and inconsistent with the localized Russian copy elsewhere.
  - → Map widget tokens and field keys to human Russian labels for display (keep the token as the value). Show the field's title/label in the canvas node rather than the raw fieldKey, reserving the key for a tooltip if needed.

### `src/forms/FormDocumentRenderer.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡3 ⚪3
_The single declarative renderer that walks a form-document tree and draws every node type (sections, columns, tabs, fields, relations, readouts, line-item tables, sandboxed custom widgets) for both human-authored and bot-emitted forms._

- 🟠 **Hand-rolled tablist has no keyboard navigation and broken ARIA wiring** (accessibility · _WCAG 2.1.1 (Keyboard), 4.1.2 (Name/Role/Value); APG Tabs pattern_ · `TabsNode, lines 140-164`)
  - Tabs are bespoke buttons with role=tab/tablist/tabpanel but the pattern is incomplete: no Arrow/Home/End keyboard handling (APG expects left/right arrow to move between tabs), no roving tabindex, the active tab is not linked to its panel via aria-controls + id, and the tabpanel has no aria-labelledby pointing back at the active tab. Keyboard users get default Tab-through-each-button behavior instead of the expected single-stop arrow-driven tablist, and screen readers cannot associate panel content with its tab. There is no <Tabs> primitive in the kit (contract notes this gap), so the half-built ARIA here is actively misleading — it announces 'tab' semantics it does not fulfill.
  - → Either implement the full APG tabs pattern (roving tabindex, ArrowLeft/ArrowRight/Home/End handlers, aria-controls on each tab pointing to a panel id, aria-labelledby on the panel) or, preferably, extract a shared <Tabs> primitive into components.jsx and consume it here so every consumer gets correct semantics. At minimum add the id/aria-controls/aria-labelledby linkage and arrow-key navigation.
- 🟡 **Tab buttons roll their own <button> instead of the <Button> primitive** (consistency · _design-system: app-content buttons must use kit <Button>_ · `TabsNode, lines 147-157`)
  - The tab triggers are raw <button className="chs-btn chs-btn--ghost"> elements. The contract explicitly flags rolled-its-own <button> instead of <Button> as a consumption violation (the nav exception covers shell ThemeToggle/NavItem only; these are app-content controls). They bypass the primitive's focus-ring, aria-disabled and variant handling, and re-implement active state with a class string.
  - → Use the kit <Button variant="ghost"> (or a dedicated tab primitive) so focus-ring, ARIA and variant styling stay centralized and consistent with the rest of the app.
- 🟡 **Broken-binding and unknown-node messages leak developer keys/jargon into the UI** (content-clarity · _design-system: no dev-jargon in UI (no field codes/UUIDs); states explicit_ · `BrokenBinding line 329; unknown-node fallback line 354`)
  - BrokenBinding renders the raw fieldKey ('Поле «{fieldKey}» больше нет в схеме — привязка битая') and the unknown-node fallback renders 'Неизвестный узел: {node.type}' with the literal node.type token and the developer word 'узел'. Both surface internal identifiers and code-names to end users, which the principles ban. The error is also non-actionable: a business user seeing a field code cannot fix or report it meaningfully.
  - → Show a human-readable label where one is resolvable (fall back to a friendly 'Это поле было удалено из формы' rather than the raw key), drop the word 'узел'/raw node.type from user-facing copy (log it to console/telemetry instead), and give the message an action or guidance ('обратитесь к автору формы').
- 🟡 **Empty-form and empty-table states are bare text, not state primitives** (feedback-state · _design-system: every list/detail renders Empty component; <EmptyState role=status>_ · `FormDocumentRenderer empty branch line 375; TableNode empty row 256-261`)
  - The 'Пустая форма.' fallback and the table's 'Нет позиций' cell are ad-hoc styled text with no role=status, no CTA, and no use of the <EmptyState> primitive the contract requires for explicit states. The italic 'Нет позиций' cell in particular reads as data rather than an intentional empty state and offers no way to add a line item.
  - → Render <EmptyState> (role=status) for the empty document. For the table, use the empty-state treatment and, if the table is editable, surface the add-row affordance there so the empty state is actionable rather than a dead italic cell.
- ⚪ **Readout label is not associated with its value control** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 (Info & Relationships)_ · `ReadoutNode, lines 219-222`)
  - The readout renders a bare <label className="chs-label"> followed by a <div role-less aria-readonly value>. A <label> with no htmlFor and no wrapped control has no programmatic association, so assistive tech does not tie the label to the displayed value. Same shape repeats in TableNode's outer <label> (line 244) which labels a <table>, not a focusable control.
  - → For the readout, either render the value in an element referenced by the label (e.g. give the value div an id and the label an htmlFor-equivalent via aria-labelledby on a grouping role=group), or use a definition-list / aria-describedby pattern. For the table, point the section label at the table via aria-labelledby on the <table> rather than a free-floating <label>.
- ⚪ **Raw numeric font-weight instead of a weight token** (consistency · _design-system: weights via --chs-weight-* tokens; hardcoded size = violation_ · `ReadoutNode line 220 (fontWeight: 600); CustomNode/others use tokens correctly`)
  - The readout value uses fontWeight: 600 as a raw literal instead of the --chs-weight-semibold token. The contract treats hardcoded sizes/weights as token violations; the rest of the file correctly consumes --chs-* tokens for spacing/color/typography, so this is an inconsistency.
  - → Replace fontWeight: 600 with var(--chs-weight-semibold).
- ⚪ **Per-field errors are rendered but never surfaced as an aggregate, and no error state for unresolved schema** (feedback-state · _forms validation UX; design-system <ErrorState>_ · `FieldNode lines 184-191; FormDocumentRenderer ctx wiring lines 377-385`)
  - Errors are passed down to individual FieldControls, which is correct, but when bindings break across many nodes the user sees a scatter of inline red dashed boxes with no summary and no path to resolution. There is no top-level error/validation summary to orient the user when a form has multiple broken bindings or validation failures. (Note: the review_code 'missing onSubmit' flag is a false positive here — this is a pure renderer that does not own the <form>/submit; submission lives in the parent.)
  - → Consider an optional validation/broken-binding summary region (role=alert / <ErrorState>) at the top of the document when ctx.errors or broken bindings are non-empty, linking to the offending fields, so users are not left to hunt inline markers.

### `src/forms/FormViewer.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡4 ⚪3
_Renders a no-code form (purchase request / approval) inside a sandboxed iframe and shows the submit outcome (pending/success/validation/server-error) below it._

- 🟠 **Validation errors expose raw field keys instead of human labels (dev-jargon leak)** (content-clarity · _principles.md «no dev-jargon in UI»; ux-pattern: inline form validation_ · `FormViewer.jsx:228-238 (chs-form-result__field renders {key})`)
  - Validation errors are rendered as `{key}: {msg}` where `key` is the raw schema field key (e.g. `vendor_inn`, `line_items`). These machine identifiers are exactly the kind of dev-jargon the contract forbids in UI, and they don't match the human label the user saw in the form, so the user can't reliably map the error to the offending field. Worse, the errors render in a panel *below* the iframe while the actual inputs live *inside* the sandboxed iframe — there is no inline, at-field error placement and no way to focus/scroll to the bad field.
  - → Resolve each field key to its human label from the form schema (CHOROS_FORMS / form-defs) before display, and post the field errors back into the iframe via postMessage so the sandbox runtime can render them inline at each control (aria-invalid + aria-describedby) and move focus to the first invalid field. At minimum, show the label, not the key, in the summary list.
- 🟡 **Success/error outcome is not surfaced as a Toast and panel is not focus-managed** (feedback-state · _Design-system surface choice (Toast for transient outcome) + ux-pattern: post-action feedback_ · `FormViewer.jsx:211-244 (inline result panel)`)
  - Post-submit outcome (success / validation / server error) is rendered as a static panel appended below the iframe. The contract specifies Toast as the surface for a transient action outcome and provides `<Toast>`/`useToasts()`. The panel can render off-screen below a tall form, so the user may submit and see no visible confirmation. The success panel also has no focus move or auto-scroll, hurting both sighted and keyboard/AT users on long forms.
  - → Emit a Toast via useToasts() for success and server-error outcomes (success=status/polite, error=alert/assertive), or at minimum scroll the result panel into view and move focus to it on state change. Keep field-level validation inline at the controls rather than only in a summary panel.
- 🟡 **Rolled-its-own result UI instead of state primitives (LoadingState/ErrorState/EmptyState)** (consistency · _Design-system primitives: LoadingState / ErrorState(onRetry) / Toast_ · `FormViewer.jsx:212-244 (chs-form-result--pending / --success / --error)`)
  - The pending, success, and error states are hand-built `<div className="chs-form-result ...">` blocks rather than the kit's `<LoadingState>` / `<ErrorState onRetry>` / feedback primitives. This is exactly the 'bare hand-rolled states instead of state primitives' consumption flag in the contract. It also means the styling (`chs-form-result*`) is a bespoke class family that can drift from tokens, and the error state offers no Повторить (retry) affordance the contract's ErrorState mandates.
  - → Replace the pending block with `<LoadingState>` and the network_error block with `<ErrorState onRetry={() => handleSubmit(lastValue)} />` so the user can retry a failed submit without re-filling. Keep success as a Toast. Audit chs-form-result*.css to confirm it consumes --chs-* tokens (no hardcoded hex/px).
- 🟡 **Server-error message is shown verbatim (raw HTTP codes / internal text leak to user)** (content-clarity · _principles.md «no dev-jargon»; ux-pattern: error messaging_ · `FormViewer.jsx:171-179, 240-243 (`HTTP ${res.status}` / err.message)`)
  - On non-validation failures the panel renders `Ошибка сервера: {fieldErrors._server}` where the value falls back to `HTTP 404` / `HTTP 401` or a raw JS `err.message` (e.g. 'Failed to fetch', 'Network error'). These are developer-facing strings, untranslated, and give the user no recoverable action. A 401 in particular should read as a session/permission problem, not 'HTTP 401'.
  - → Map status codes to human Russian copy with next steps (401 → «Сессия истекла, войдите снова»; 404 → «Форма больше не доступна»; network → «Не удалось связаться с сервером, проверьте соединение»). Reserve the raw code for a console.debug, not the UI.
- 🟡 **Recovery/affordance gap on validation and server errors — no actionable path** (flow · _Affordance rule (enabled element must do something) + error recovery_ · `FormViewer.jsx:228-244`)
  - Both error panels are read-only dead ends. The network_error panel has no Повторить button (the submit lives inside the iframe, so the user has no obvious way to re-trigger without scrolling back up into the iframe). The validation panel lists errors but doesn't link/scroll to the offending fields inside the iframe. This breaks the contract's affordance expectation that an error state offers a recovery action.
  - → Add a retry control to the server-error state and make each validation list item a button that postMessages the sandbox to focus the corresponding field. This closes the loop between the out-of-iframe error panel and the in-iframe controls.
- ⚪ **recordId shown as a raw identifier without mono treatment or human framing** (consistency · _Design-system: <MonoId>/<Mono> for IDs; principles «no UUID/dev-jargon»_ · `FormViewer.jsx:222-224 (` Запись: ${submitResult.value.recordId}.`)`)
  - The success message interpolates `recordId` inline as plain body text. If recordId is a UUID/opaque key it reads as dev-jargon; the contract reserves IDs for the `--chs-font-mono` family and the `<MonoId>`/`<Mono>` primitives, and warns against surfacing raw UUIDs to users.
  - → Render the identifier via `<MonoId>` and, if it's a UUID, prefer a human-friendly reference (record number / link to the created record) over the raw key. If no friendly form exists, drop it from the success copy.
- ⚪ **Hard-coded two-form copy makes the viewer non-generic and risks stale labels** (content-clarity · _ux-pattern: maintainable labelling / single source of truth_ · `FormViewer.jsx:205, 209 (ternary `formKey === 'purchase' ? … : …`)`)
  - The iframe `title` and `aria-label` are derived from a hard-coded ternary that only knows 'purchase' vs everything-else. For any third form (a no-code platform will have many) the AT label silently falls back to 'Согласование закупки' — a wrong, misleading accessible name. The human title should come from the form schema, not a literal branch.
  - → Read the form's display title from CHOROS_FORMS / the form schema and use it for both `title` and `aria-label`, with a neutral fallback like «Форма». This keeps the accessible name correct as new forms are added.
- ⚪ **Submit pending state does not disable/lock the in-iframe submit button (double-submit risk)** (feedback-state · _ux-pattern: prevent duplicate submission_ · `FormViewer.jsx:140-141 (setSubmitState('pending')) and iframe boundary`)
  - While a submit is in flight (`pending`), the form's submit button lives inside the sandboxed iframe and is not informed of the pending state, so a user can press it again and trigger a second POST. The outside panel shows 'Отправка…' but the actual control remains live.
  - → postMessage a 'submitting' signal into the sandbox so the runtime disables/aria-busy's its submit button during the request, and re-enable on completion. This matches the kit's loading/aria-busy convention for buttons.

### `src/forms/field-renderer.jsx` — 🟠3 🟡3 ⚪2
_The single schema-driven form-field renderer: maps a binding-contract field descriptor to a labelled, controlled input (text/number/date/select/checkbox/multi-select/money/url/email/person) with per-step mode enforcement, used across inbox, record-create, and form-viewer screens._

- 🟠 **Validation error text is not programmatically tied to its input** (accessibility · _WCAG 3.3.1 Error Identification / 1.3.1 / 4.1.3_ · `field-renderer.jsx:133-137 (PersonPicker errorNode), 236-240 + every control (FieldControl errorNode)`)
  - The per-field error is rendered as a bare <span> with no id, the control carries aria-invalid but no aria-describedby pointing at the error, and the span has no role=alert/aria-live. A screen-reader user who lands on an invalid field hears 'invalid' but never hears WHY, and a newly-injected error after submit is not announced at all. The design-system <Field> primitive wires hint/error via aria-describedby specifically to avoid this.
  - → Give the error span an id (e.g. `${id}-error`), set aria-describedby={error ? `${id}-error` : undefined} on every control, and add role=alert (or aria-live=assertive) to the error span so it is announced when it appears. Better: render through the kit <Field>/<Select>, which already binds error via aria-describedby.
- 🟠 **Raw <input>/<select>/<textarea> instead of design-system <Field>/<Select>** (consistency · _ux-pattern: forms/text-field; DS contract §3 (rolled-your-own input flag)_ · `field-renderer.jsx:160-176, 300-316, 449-561 (all control branches)`)
  - Every control is hand-rolled with literal className='chs-input', inline style objects, and manually re-implemented label↔control binding, aria-required, aria-invalid, and the required '*' marker. The contract exports <Field> and <Select> primitives that own exactly this (label binding, aria-invalid, hint/error via aria-describedby, focus-ring). Re-implementing per-branch means each of the ~10 branches can (and here does) drift — e.g. some get readOnly, the select gets disabled, the structural-readout gets aria-readonly. This is the single largest consistency violation in the file.
  - → Route text/number/textarea/date/url/email/money through <Field> and enum through <Select>, passing label/required/error/readOnly props.
