Keep the contract-resolution logic; replace the JSX leaves with primitives. This collapses the duplicated label/error/required scaffolding and guarantees uniform a11y.
- 🟠 **Read-only select uses disabled, dropping it from tab order and from the submitted form** (feedback-state · _WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard / DS affordance rule_ · `field-renderer.jsx:167 (select disabled={readOnly}), 360 (multi-select checkbox disabled), 105/167 (PersonPicker)`)
  - For read-only mode the <select> sets disabled, but the text/number/date/textarea branches correctly use readOnly. disabled removes the control from the keyboard tab order (a read-only field a keyboard user can still focus and read is the intended pattern) AND a disabled <select>/checkbox is not serialized on native submit. The mode comment says read-only → 'rendered disabled', but disabled and readOnly are not interchangeable for selects/checkboxes. Behaviour is now inconsistent across field types for the same mode.
  - → Make read-only uniform and keyboard-reachable: native <select>/checkbox have no readOnly attr, so emulate it (e.g. render the resolved value as a read-only text readout when mode is read-only, or keep the control focusable with aria-disabled + onChange guard) so the value still round-trips and stays in tab order. Whatever you choose, apply it identically to select, multi-select, checkbox, and the scalar inputs.
- 🟡 **Multi-select checkbox group has no programmatic grouping (label not tied to the checkboxes)** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 Info and Relationships; ux-pattern forms/checkbox-group_ · `field-renderer.jsx:338-383 (multi-select branch)`)
  - The group's <label htmlFor={id}> points at an id that no element owns (the wrapper <div> has no id), so the group label is orphaned — this is the legitimate signal behind the review_code 'missing label' hits. The checkboxes are not wrapped in a <fieldset>/<legend> or role=group, so AT announces each option in isolation with no notion that they belong to one multi-value field, and aria-required/aria-invalid/error are not conveyed for the group at all.
  - → Wrap the option list in <fieldset> with the field label as <legend> (or a <div role='group' aria-labelledby={labelId} aria-describedby={error?`${id}-error`:undefined} aria-required>). Drop the dangling htmlFor. This also fixes the error/required propagation for the multi-value case.
- 🟡 **Loading / error / empty states bypass the kit state primitives and offer no retry** (feedback-state · _DS contract §3/§4 (<LoadingState>/<ErrorState onRetry>/<EmptyState>)_ · `field-renderer.jsx:140-157 (PersonPicker control states)`)
  - PersonPicker hand-rolls its loading ('Загрузка…' italic), error ('Не удалось загрузить список сотрудников'), and empty ('В организации нет сотрудников') states as styled <div className='chs-input'> blobs instead of the contracted <LoadingState role=status aria-busy>, <ErrorState onRetry>, <EmptyState>. The fetch error is terminal: there is no Повторить affordance, so a transient /api/org failure permanently blocks selecting an employee with no recovery short of reloading the page. The error div is only aria-live=polite, so it may not be announced.
  - → Render the three states via the kit primitives; give the error path an onRetry that re-invokes fetchEmployees (lift the fetch into a callable so retry re-runs it). At minimum add a Повторить <Button variant=ghost> and use role=alert for the error.
- 🟡 **Structural / read-only contracts render an italic prose readout with no value and weak semantics** (content-clarity · _DS affordance rule / states-always-explicit_ · `field-renderer.jsx:255-294 (non-scalarish branch)`)
  - For relation/collection/date-range/file/rollup the field shows muted italic text like «Поле типа X пока заполняется в другом месте» inside a .chs-input box. It looks like an input but does nothing (a silent no-op surface, which the affordance rule forbids), gives the user no path to where the field IS authorable, and for the read-only rollup case shows the type label but never the actual computed value — so a user filling the form sees an empty-looking control with no data. aria-readonly='true' on a non-form <div> is also not a valid use of that attribute.
  - → For the rollup/read-only case, render the resolved value (formatted) rather than only the type label, using a plain read-only text presentation. For not-yet-authorable structural types, make the message actionable (link/Button to where it is edited, or the «скоро» pattern) rather than a dead box, and use a neutral container (not .chs-input, not aria-readonly) so it does not masquerade as an editable field.
- ⚪ **Required '*' marker conveys requiredness by color/glyph only, with no text equivalent** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.4.1 Use of Color / 3.3.2 Labels or Instructions_ · `field-renderer.jsx:128-130, 279-281, 325-327, 374-376, 436-438, 571-573`)
  - The required marker is a danger-colored '*' wrapped in aria-hidden='true'. The control does carry aria-required, so AT users are covered, but sighted users who can't distinguish the danger color see only an asterisk with no legend, and there is no 'обязательное поле' text anywhere. Repeated inline span+style for the marker is also the kind of per-branch duplication <Field> would centralize.
  - → Keep aria-required, but pair the visual '*' with an accessible/visible convention (a one-line 'Поля со звёздочкой обязательны' legend at form level, or a non-color cue), and centralize the marker inside the shared <Field>/<Select> rather than re-emitting the styled span in six places.
- ⚪ **Pervasive hardcoded inline layout styles instead of tokenized utility classes** (consistency · _DS contract §1 (single source tokens; rolled-your-own styling)_ · `field-renderer.jsx:122, 233, 180/276/322 (marginBottom), 348, 482, 496 and throughout`)
  - Colors and spacing use --chs-* tokens (good), but the file scatters inline style objects for layout on nearly every element (display/width/boxSizing, flex rows for money, gap stacks for multi-select, per-field marginBottom). The contract centralizes density/spacing and expects primitives to own this. Inline style duplication across branches is how the money/multi-select/checkbox rows ended up with subtly different paddings and the table-cell (hideLabel) path needs special-casing of margin.
  - → Move recurring layout into the kit primitives or shared utility classes (e.g. .chs-field, .chs-control-row) so spacing/density come from tokens in one place; reserve inline style for truly one-off cases. This is mostly resolved automatically by adopting <Field>/<Select>.

### `src/screens/screen-audit.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡3 ⚪2
_A read-only, tenant-wide audit log screen: a single vertical stream of hash-chained events with action/actor filters, cursor pagination ("Загрузить ещё"), and explicit loading/error/empty states._

- 🟠 **Raw action code-name leaks into every event row** (content-clarity · _principles.md — no dev-jargon in UI (no code-names)_ · `screen-audit.jsx:32 — <MonoId>{ev.action}</MonoId> in AuditEventRow`)
  - Every row renders the raw machine action token (e.g. grant.role, agent.invoke) as a mono ID right after the human-readable summary. The design-system principles explicitly forbid dev-jargon / code-names in the UI, and ev.summary already carries the human meaning. This duplicates noise on every line, hurts scannability of a dense tenant-wide log, and exposes internal taxonomy to operators.
  - → Drop the bare <MonoId>{ev.action}</MonoId>. If the action code has operational value, surface it only on hover (Tooltip) or in a future detail Drawer, and always render ev.summary as the primary text. Keep the ExecGlyph + actor as the scannable anchor.
- 🟠 **Partial load-more error is a silent bare div, not an Error/Alert primitive** (feedback-state · _WCAG 4.1.3 Status Messages (AA); contract: Error = role=alert + Повторить_ · `screen-audit.jsx:176 — {error && <div className="chs-audit-loadmore__err">{error}</div>}`)
  - When 'Загрузить ещё' fails, the error is rendered as a plain <div> with no role=alert / aria-live, no Повторить action, and no dismiss. Screen-reader users get no announcement, and sighted users get a dead-end message — the only recovery is to click the still-present 'Загрузить ещё' button again, which is not signposted. This both breaks the kit's Error convention (ErrorState onRetry) and fails WCAG 4.1.3.
  - → Replace the bare div with the kit's ErrorState (or at minimum role=alert) scoped to the load-more region, wiring onRetry={loadMore}. Clear this error at the start of each loadMore attempt (currently it can persist after a later success since loadMore only sets error on failure).
- 🟡 **ev.target rendered as a raw ID chip (likely UUID/slug)** (content-clarity · _principles.md — no UUIDs in UI_ · `screen-audit.jsx:31 — {ev.target && <MonoId chip>{ev.target}</MonoId>}`)
  - The target is shown as a mono chip with no label or human name. If the server projection sends a UUID/slug (the comment says target is part of the redacted projection), operators see an opaque identifier with no indication of what kind of object it is or its human name, violating the 'no UUIDs / dev-jargon' principle and reducing the log's usefulness for non-technical admins.
  - → Have the server projection include a human label for the target (type + name) and render that as the primary text, keeping the raw id only inside a Tooltip or copy-on-click. If only an id is available, at minimum prefix it with the object type so the chip is self-describing.
- 🟡 **Load-more error is never cleared on a subsequent successful page fetch** (feedback-state · _feedback-state — stale error_ · `screen-audit.jsx:73-90 — loadMore() never calls setError(null)`)
  - loadMore sets error on failure but never resets it on entry or on success. After one failed 'Загрузить ещё', a subsequent successful click appends events while the stale error banner (line 176) remains visible, contradicting the now-successful state and confusing the operator.
  - → Call setError(null) at the start of loadMore (and/or on success). This pairs with converting the banner to a scoped ErrorState with retry.
- 🟡 **Timestamp shows time-only with no date grouping for a multi-day log** (content-clarity · _data-tables / scannability; principles.md — no 1970-01-01 leaks_ · `screen-audit.jsx:23 — <div className="chs-ev__time">{fmtTs(ev.ts)}</div> (see screen-audit.logic.js fmtTs)`)
  - A tenant-wide audit log spans days/weeks, but each row shows only what fmtTs returns in a single column. If fmtTs renders time-only (or a long absolute timestamp on every row), the stream is hard to scan and ambiguous about which day an event belongs to. There is no date separator/grouping between days. Verify fmtTs also guards against epoch/1970-01-01 for missing ts.
  - → Group events under day headers (e.g. «Сегодня», «Вчера», then dd.MM.yyyy) and show time-only within a group, using --chs-font-mono for the timestamp. Confirm fmtTs returns a placeholder (e.g. «—») rather than 1970-01-01 / Invalid Date for null ts.
- ⚪ **Filter bar inputs and submit button lack an accessible group/region label** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 Info and Relationships; 3.3.2 Labels_ · `screen-audit.jsx:118 — <form className="chs-auditbar"> ... </form>`)
  - Individual Field/Select are labelled (good), but the filter form itself has no accessible name (no aria-label / aria-labelledby) tying it to the «Журнал аудита» heading, and on submit there is no programmatic announcement of the result count. Screen-reader users get no confirmation that filtering happened or how many events matched.
  - → Add aria-label="Фильтры журнала" (or aria-labelledby pointing at the title) to the form, and announce the applied-filter result count via a polite live region (role=status), e.g. «Найдено N событий». This makes the filter outcome perceivable without sight.
- ⚪ **Actor filter is a free-text field expecting an exact slug/id with no validation or affordance** (content-clarity · _forms/text-field; affordance rule_ · `screen-audit.jsx:131-138 — <Field label="Актор" placeholder="slug или id актора" />`)
  - The actor filter requires the operator to know and type an exact slug or id, with no autocomplete, no validation, and no feedback when a typo yields zero results (the generic 'Ничего не найдено' empty state appears with no hint that the actor string was unmatched). For a non-technical B2B admin, 'slug или id актора' is itself jargon and a guessing game.
  - → Prefer a typeahead/Select populated with known actors (humans/agents/services) over free text. If free text must stay, soften the placeholder to «Имя или логин» and, on a zero-result filtered query, hint that the typed actor may not exist rather than only «Измените или сбросьте фильтры».

### `src/screens/screen-notifications.jsx` — 🟠3 🟡3 ⚪3
_In-app notification center (own notifications list with mark-read/mark-all) plus a per-event channel-preferences settings tab._

- 🟠 **Tabs and sub-tabs are bespoke buttons without ARIA tab roles** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2 Name/Role/Value; navigation/tabs_ · `chs-subtabs buttons (lines 271-276); chs-tabs filter buttons (lines 118-123)`)
  - The top-level Центр/Настройки switch and the Все/Непрочитанные filter are rendered as plain <button> with only aria-selected. There is no role="tablist"/role="tab"/role="tabpanel" wiring, no aria-controls, and no roving tabindex / arrow-key navigation. aria-selected on a generic button is semantically invalid (it is only meaningful on roles like tab/option), so screen readers get a confusing or ignored signal, and keyboard users cannot move between tabs with arrow keys as the tab pattern requires. The design-system has no <Tabs> primitive, so this is reinvented here inconsistently.
  - → Wrap each group in role="tablist", give each button role="tab" with aria-selected and aria-controls pointing at a role="tabpanel" region (id-linked), implement arrow-key navigation with roving tabindex, and mark inactive tabs tabindex=-1. Better: extract a shared <Tabs>/<SubTabs> kit primitive (the contract flags this as a missing primitive) so this and the shell's bespoke RightsSubTabs converge.
- 🟠 **No feedback after mark-read, mark-all, or preference save** (feedback-state · _feedback/toast; affordance rule_ · `markRead (lines 85-93), markAll (lines 95-112), toggle (lines 191-212)`)
  - Every successful mutating action is silent. Mark-all reloads the list; a single checkbox toggle in settings persists with no visible confirmation; on a fast network the only signal is a row re-render. The design-system ships <Toast>/useToasts() precisely for transient outcomes, and the principles require an enabled action to do something observable. Users cannot tell whether 'Прочитать все' or a preference change actually succeeded.
  - → Wire useToasts()+<ToastViewport> and emit a polite success toast on mark-all ('Все уведомления отмечены прочитанными'), and on preference save ('Настройки сохранены'). For mark-read the list re-render is a reasonable implicit signal, but mark-all and settings save need explicit confirmation.
- 🟠 **Errors from row actions replace the entire list/table** (feedback-state · _feedback/error-handling; states-explicit_ · `CenterTab error branch (lines 132-133) fed by markRead/markAll catch (lines 91, 107); SettingsTab error branch (lines 217-218) fed by toggle catch (line 207)`)
  - setError is shared between the initial load and the per-action mutations. If a single mark-read POST or a checkbox PUT fails, the catch sets error, and the render collapses the whole list/table into a full-screen ErrorState — wiping the user's context and all loaded rows for a localized, recoverable failure. The toggle also leaves the checkbox in its pre-click (server) state with no inline indication that THAT row failed.
  - → Separate load-error (full ErrorState) from action-error. For failed mutations, surface an error toast (role=alert/assertive) or an inline row-level message and keep the list rendered. On a failed toggle, revert/keep the checkbox and toast the failure so the user knows which preference did not save.
- 🟡 **Raw event-kind identifiers leaked into the UI as dev-jargon** (content-clarity · _principles: no dev-jargon in UI_ · `notification meta {n.event_kind} (line 146); settings sub-label {ev.kind} via chs-task__step (line 236)`)
  - The notification meta line renders the raw machine code 'task.assigned' / 'sla.breach' next to the timestamp, and the settings table shows 'task.assigned' as a secondary label under each human label. The design principles explicitly forbid dev-jargon (code-names, dotted machine keys) in the UI. There is already a human label map (EVENT_KINDS) that could be used in the notification list too.
  - → Map n.event_kind to its human label (reuse EVENT_KINDS) in the notification meta, and drop the raw ev.kind sub-label in settings (or keep it only as a title/tooltip for debugging, not visible body text). Body text must not show dotted identifiers per principles.
- 🟡 **Raw checkbox inputs without kit wrapper or visible per-cell label** (consistency · _design-system: raw <input> without Field/Checkbox wrapper_ · `settings <input type="checkbox"> (lines 240-246)`)
  - The preference matrix uses bare <input type="checkbox"> styled by class only. The contract flags raw inputs as a consumption violation and notes there is no <Checkbox> primitive yet — so styling, focus-ring, and disabled visuals will not match the kit's controls. The aria-label is present (good) but the visible association relies entirely on column-header proximity; there is no <th scope> wiring and the th cells are not marked scope="col"/the row label cell is not scope="row", so the checkbox's accessible context in the table grid is weaker than it should be.
  - → Introduce/consume a kit <Checkbox> (or at minimum ensure the raw input inherits kit focus-ring and disabled tokens), and add scope="col" to the channel <th> and scope="row" (a row-header th) to the event label cell so the grid relationship is programmatically determinable.
- 🟡 **Disabled checkbox during save gives no busy indication** (feedback-state · _states-explicit; loading feedback_ · `toggle savingKind / disabled input (lines 243, 233)`)
  - While a preference PUT is in flight, the whole row's checkboxes are disabled (savingKind===ev.kind) and a data-saving attribute is set, but there is no spinner, aria-busy, or visible 'saving' text. A user who clicks then sees the control briefly greyed has no clear model of what is happening, and assistive tech is not told the row is busy.
  - → Add aria-busy to the row or an inline <Spinner>/«сохранение…» affordance while savingKind matches, and ensure data-saving has a visible CSS treatment. Confirm with a success toast on completion (see feedback finding).
- ⚪ **Inconsistent control vocabulary: kit Button next to hand-rolled action/tab buttons** (consistency · _design-system: app-content buttons must use kit_ · `kit <Button> (line 126) vs raw chs-notif__action (lines 149-151) and chs-tab/chs-subtab buttons`)
  - The screen mixes the kit <Button> (Прочитать все) with hand-rolled <button> elements for the per-row 'прочитать' action and the tab strips. Per the contract, app-content buttons must use the kit; the row action in particular is an app-content control, not a nav exception. This produces divergent focus-ring, disabled, and aria-busy handling across the same screen.
  - → Render the row 'прочитать' action with <Button variant="ghost" size="sm" glyph="check"> so it inherits focus-ring/aria-disabled behavior. Tab strips are the acknowledged bespoke-nav exception but should still gain proper tab roles (see tabs finding).
- ⚪ **Missing explicit type attribute on hand-rolled buttons** (ux-pattern · _forms/submit-button_ · `chs-tab buttons (118,121), chs-subtab buttons (271,274), chs-notif__action (149)`)
  - All hand-rolled <button> elements omit an explicit type. Default button type inside a form is submit; if any of these ever render within a <form> they would trigger an unintended submit/reload. This is the review_code tool's primary recurring warning.
  - → Add type="button" to every hand-rolled button. (Migrating the row action to the kit <Button> resolves this for that element automatically.)
- ⚪ **'прочитать все' has no confirmation for a bulk state change** (flow · _destructive/bulk-action confirmation_ · `markAll Button (line 126) / handler (lines 95-112)`)
  - Marking all as read is a non-destructive but irreversible-in-perception bulk action that clears the unread badge in one click. It is reasonable to leave unconfirmed, but there is no undo affordance either, so an accidental click loses the unread distinction with no recovery.
  - → Lowest-effort improvement: pair the success toast (see feedback finding) with an 'Отменить' action, or leave as-is given it is non-destructive. No modal confirm needed — that would be over-friction for this action.

### `src/screens/screen-ops-overview.jsx` — 🟡3 ⚪4
_An operational overview dashboard showing three independent best-effort signal cards (process pulse, LLM spend, available reports), each fetching and rendering its own loading/error/empty/data state, with drill-down links to detail screens._

- 🟡 **«Обновить» refetches all cards but gives no progress or completion feedback** (feedback-state · _Feedback after action / loading affordance_ · `OpsOverviewScreen render, line 562 (reloadAll) and 569-577 (Обновить button)`)
  - The top-level «Обновить» button remounts the grid via key={reloadKey} (line 585), forcing every card to re-fetch. The button itself never enters a loading/busy state, and there is no toast or aggregate indicator that a refresh is in flight or completed. If cards are already populated and the network is fast, the user clicks and sees a near-instant flash with no confirmation the action did anything; if slow, the button still looks idle and invites repeated clicks. The remount also discards any already-loaded card data, so a refresh momentarily blanks healthy cards to LoadingState.
  - → Give the button a busy state (the kit <Button> supports loading) tied to the in-flight fetches, or emit a transient confirmation via useToasts()/<Toast> on completion (the contract's transient-outcome surface). Prefer having each card expose its reload imperatively (or lift fetch state) so refresh re-runs fetches in place rather than remounting and blanking populated cards.
- 🟡 **Card-level async state changes are not announced to assistive tech** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.3 Status Messages (AA)_ · `Card bodies, e.g. ProcessCard lines 294-347; SpendCard 397-457; ReportsCard 493-538`)
  - Each card swaps between LoadingState, ErrorState, EmptyState and data purely via conditional rendering inside a static role="region". There is no aria-live region wrapping the swap, so a screen-reader user who triggers «Обновить» (or lands during async load) is not notified when content transitions from loading to data/error. The kit's LoadingState (role=status) and ErrorState (role=alert) help per-component, but the data-success transition produces no announcement at all.
  - → Wrap each card body's state-swap container in an aria-live="polite" region (or aria-busy on the region while loading) so the resolved state is announced. Confirm the kit LoadingState/ErrorState carry their documented role=status/role=alert; if so, ensure they are inside the live region rather than conditionally mounted as siblings that AT may miss.
- 🟡 **Money formatted with fixed 4 decimals and bare currency code reduces scannability** (content-clarity · _Numeric formatting / data presentation_ · `fmtAmount, lines 188-194 (used in SpendCard 424, 447)`)
  - fmtAmount always renders n.toFixed(4), so a 30-day total shows e.g. '$1234.5678' — four decimals on large sums is noisy and hard to scan in a dense B2B dashboard, while still being imprecise for sub-cent token costs at other scales. Non-USD currencies render as a bare prefix code ('RUB 0.0123'), and there is no thousands grouping, unlike the token counts which correctly use toLocaleString('ru-RU') on line 428. This is inconsistent within the same card.
  - → Use Intl.NumberFormat with currency style and locale grouping (or a scale-aware decimal count: more decimals for tiny amounts, 2 for larger totals). At minimum apply thousands grouping consistent with the token formatting, and render currency via the same NumberFormat rather than a hand-built prefix.
- ⚪ **Numeric/money values not rendered in the mono font the contract reserves for them** (consistency · _Design-system typography contract_ · `metricValueStyle (119-125), miniValueStyle (169-176); applied to money and counts in all three cards`)
  - The design-system contract states --chs-font-mono (JetBrains Mono) is the font 'for IDs/timestamps/money/numeric cols'. The metric values here (LLM spend amounts, token counts, step counts, per-connection amounts) use the default sans stack with only fontVariantNumeric: tabular-nums. The kit even ships <Mono>/<MonoId> primitives for exactly this. Numbers across the observability surface will not align with the mono treatment used elsewhere in the product.
  - → Apply --chs-font-mono (or wrap values in the kit's <Mono> primitive) for the money amounts and token/step counts so numeric presentation matches the contract and the rest of the app's analytics screens.
- ⚪ **Entire screen is built from ad-hoc inline-styled divs instead of kit surface primitives** (consistency · _Design-system primitive consumption_ · `Style objects lines 31-176; card markup in all three cards`)
  - The card chrome (cardStyle/cardHeadStyle/cardBodyStyle/cardFootStyle), mini-list rows, and metric rows are all hand-rolled inline-style objects. The contract notes there is intentionally no <Card>/<Panel> or <Table> primitive, so some ad-hoc assembly is expected — but three near-identical card scaffolds plus a repeated mini-row pattern duplicated across cards invite drift (border radius, padding, separators are re-declared rather than shared). This is a consistency/maintainability smell rather than a hard violation.
  - → Extract the shared card scaffold (head/body/foot + region role + title) into a single local Card component within this screen (or propose a kit <Panel> primitive), and a shared MiniRow, so the three cards cannot diverge. Tokens are used correctly; the issue is repetition, not raw values.
- ⚪ **Drill-down affordance uses a ghost Button instead of a link, breaking navigation semantics** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / link-vs-button semantics_ · `Card footers: ProcessCard 348-357, SpendCard 458-467, ReportsCard 539-548`)
  - The «Подробнее →» / «Перейти к отчётам →» actions navigate to a route (navigate('/spend') etc.) but are rendered as <Button type="button">. Navigations that change the URL should be links (anchor) for correct AT semantics, middle-click/open-in-new-tab, and right-click context menu. The trailing '→' as inline text is also decorative; a Lucide chevron icon via the Button glyph prop would match the contract's linear-icon convention.
  - → Render route navigations as anchors (react-router <Link>, or a kit link variant) rather than type=button Buttons so they behave as links for keyboard, screen readers, and open-in-new-tab. Replace the literal '→' with a Lucide chevron via glyph if a visual indicator is desired.
- ⚪ **ReportsCard shows an app count but its data state and CTA conflate two different actions** (content-clarity · _Microcopy / signal clarity_ · `ReportsCard, lines 478-549 (esp. metric 'Приложений' 510-514, footer 539-548)`)
  - Unlike Process and Spend cards which surface an operational signal (bottleneck, money), the Reports card surfaces only a count of applications and a list of recent app names — it is a navigation tile dressed as a signal. The metric label 'Приложений' with hint 'доступных для построения отчётов' plus footer 'Перейти к отчётам' is slightly indirect: a count of apps is a weak proxy for 'reports' and may mislead (more apps does not mean more/healthier reports). The file's own header comment (lines 472-476) acknowledges this is a deliberate simplification.
  - → If a real report signal is unavailable, soften the framing so the card reads as an entry point (e.g. lead with the recent-apps list and the CTA, drop the large count as the hero metric), or surface a genuine reports metric (e.g. report pages count) so all three cards present comparable operational signals.

### `src/screens/screen-org.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡5 ⚪2
_Admin screen showing the org tree (department → position → employee/agent) on the left with create/delete CRUD, and a read-only executor card on the right including a live explain-PDP authorization trace._

- 🟠 **Interactive controls nested inside a <button> (invalid HTML, broken a11y)** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value / 1.3.1 Info and Relationships_ · `screen-org.jsx:268-297 (TreeRow)`)
  - The whole tree row is a native <button>, and inside it sit a <span onClick> twist toggle (line 274) and a <span role="button" tabIndex=0> delete control (line 285). Interactive elements nested inside a button is invalid HTML — browsers may not render or focus them predictably, and screen readers announce one button whose accessible name now includes the delete span. The twist toggle has no role, no tabIndex, and no keyboard handler at all, so the expand/collapse affordance is mouse-only.
  - → Restructure the row so the container is a non-button element (e.g. a div with role=treeitem / aria-expanded) and the select/toggle/delete are sibling buttons, OR keep a single button for select and render the delete as a real adjacent <Button variant=ghost size=sm> outside it. Give the twist a real <button> with aria-label «Развернуть/Свернуть» and aria-expanded so keyboard users can expand nodes.
- 🟠 **Tree is not a real keyboard-navigable tree (no roles, no arrow-key nav, no focus management)** (accessibility · _WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard / ARIA tree pattern_ · `screen-org.jsx:342-362 (OrgTree) and TreeRow`)
  - The container <div className="chs-tree"> and <div className="chs-org__tree"> carry no role=tree / role=group, rows have no role=treeitem / aria-level / aria-expanded, and there is no roving-tabindex or arrow-key handling. aria-selected is set on a plain button. Depth is conveyed only by visual padding (line 270), so a screen-reader user gets a flat list of buttons with no hierarchy and cannot expand branches from the keyboard.
  - → Adopt the WAI-ARIA tree pattern: role=tree on the list, role=treeitem + aria-level + aria-expanded + aria-selected on rows, role=group on child containers, and Up/Down/Left/Right/Enter handling with roving tabindex. If full tree semantics are out of scope, at minimum add aria-expanded to expandable rows and make the toggle keyboard-operable.
- 🟡 **Selected row state relies on color/visual only via aria-selected on a non-option element** (feedback-state · _WCAG 1.4.1 Use of Color_ · `screen-org.jsx:271 (aria-selected on chs-trow button)`)
  - aria-selected is valid only on listbox/grid/tree roles; on a bare button it is ignored by assistive tech, so the 'currently selected executor' state has no programmatic signal. Sighted users get a class-driven highlight, but there is no non-color cue confirmed in markup.
  - → Once the row is a role=treeitem, aria-selected becomes meaningful — keep it there. Additionally ensure the selected state is conveyed by more than background color (e.g. a left accent bar plus aria-current/aria-selected) to satisfy 1.4.1.
- 🟡 **explain-PDP verdict and slug ID expose dev-jargon and raw identifiers in the UI** (content-clarity · _design-contract §2 no dev-jargon / honest microcopy_ · `screen-org.jsx:414-453, 490 (MonoId person.id), 419 default "mcp://ledger.invoices"`)
  - The contract bans dev-jargon and raw IDs/UUIDs/code-names in UI. The executor card prints the raw slug as the identity (MonoId person.id, line 490) and even falls back to showing the slug AS the person's name (line 669: name: selected). The explain panel surfaces «explain-PDP», a prefilled resource string «mcp://ledger.invoices», and step rows rendered as raw <Mono>{s.step}</Mono> — internal engine vocabulary leaking to an admin-facing screen.
  - → Render a human display name as the primary identity and demote the slug to a muted secondary line (never use slug as the name). Relabel «explain-PDP» to plain Russian (e.g. «Проверка доступа»). Map PDP step codes to human-readable phrases instead of printing raw step identifiers, and use a friendlier placeholder/example for the resource field.
- 🟡 **explain-PDP panel never renders a loading state primitive (bare text only) and lacks an empty/initial cue** (feedback-state · _design-contract §3 LoadingState/EmptyState; principles 'states always explicit'_ · `screen-org.jsx:428-461 (ExplainPanel)`)
  - The panel uses an ad-hoc string state machine ('loading' / object) and shows feedback only by swapping button text to «Трасса…» and rendering raw <span> results. There is no LoadingState/Skeleton while the trace runs and no initial/empty hint telling the user what «Объяснить» does before first run. The result block is also not wrapped in role=status/alert, so a screen reader is not notified when the verdict appears.
  - → While running, render <LoadingState> (or aria-busy on the result region); on completion wrap the verdict/trace in role=status (and the 403/error in role=alert) so it is announced. Add a one-line initial hint under the controls describing what the action returns.
- 🟡 **Inline style objects used pervasively instead of token classes — design-system consumption drift** (consistency · _design-contract §1 tokens / 'hardcoded = violation' (and rolled-your-own layout)_ · `screen-org.jsx:292, 348, 356, 417, 433, 450, 498 (multiple style={{...}} blocks)`)
  - Many layout/color decisions are hand-written inline style objects (the CRUD toolbar row, the read-only notice, the explain panel layout, the detail note paragraph, the delete glyph). While they reference --chs-* vars (good), bypassing CSS classes for structural layout invites inconsistency, defeats theming overrides, and duplicates spacing logic the kit/CSS already owns. The contract explicitly flags rolled-your-own layout as a consumption smell.
  - → Move these repeated layout/color declarations into BEM classes in the screen's stylesheet (e.g. chs-org__crudbar, chs-org__readonly-note, chs-org__detailnote) so spacing/border/color stay token-driven but centralized. Reserve inline style for genuinely dynamic values like the depth padding calc.
- 🟡 **Slug placeholder displayed as a person's name when org-tree lookup fails** (content-clarity · _design-contract honest-empty (D2) / content-clarity_ · `screen-org.jsx:668-669`)
  - When `selected` is set but the person can't be resolved from the loaded tree, the card renders ExecutorDetail with person={{ id: selected, name: selected, type: 'human' }} — i.e. it shows the raw slug as both the name and ID and guesses the type as 'human'. This fabricates an identity (possibly mislabeling an agent as a human) rather than showing an honest 'not found / loading' state.
  - → If the selected id is not found in the tree, render a LoadingState (if departments still loading) or an EmptyState/ErrorState («Исполнитель не найден») instead of synthesizing a fake human record. Never default an unknown executor to type 'human'.
- ⚪ **Delete confirmation labels every dangerous action identically and shows no consequence detail** (ux-pattern · _destructive-action confirmation_ · `screen-org.jsx:639-649 (ConfirmDialog) and 642-643 message`)
  - ConfirmDialog title is a generic «Удалить?» for departments, positions, employees and roles alike. Deleting a department can cascade-fail (the code itself notes FK_IN_USE 409 handling), but the confirmation does not warn that the entity may have dependents or what «необратимо» entails. The user only learns of FK_IN_USE via an error toast after confirming.
  - → Make the title/entity-specific («Удалить подразделение?») and, where the backend reports references, surface a pre-check or at least a warning line in the dialog (e.g. «Возможны связанные должности/назначения»). Keep the destructive variant. This is minor since a ConfirmDialog (not window.confirm) is correctly used.
- ⚪ **Create modal submit shows no success focus return / the toast is the only confirmation** (feedback-state · _feedback after action / focus management_ · `screen-org.jsx:568-572 (handleCreated), 174 Modal`)
  - On 201 the modal closes and a success toast fires, but focus is not explicitly returned to the triggering «+ Подразделение» button, and the newly created node is not selected/scrolled-to in the tree. Keyboard users lose their place, and there is no visual confirmation in the tree of which item was just added beyond the transient toast.
  - → Return focus to the invoking control on close (the kit <Modal> focus-trap should restore focus to the opener — verify it does), and consider auto-selecting/scrolling to the newly created entity so the create action has a persistent, visible result, not just a transient toast.

### `src/screens/screen-overview.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡2 ⚪4
_Home/landing screen ("Обзор") that orients the user with one tile per IA section (Конструктор, Работа, Исполнители и доступ, Наблюдаемость), showing live best-effort counters and a primary CTA into each section._

- 🟠 **Clickable tile (<section onClick>) is mouse-only — not keyboard focusable or operable** (accessibility · _WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard / 4.1.2 Name,Role,Value_ · `screen-overview.jsx:96-100 (Tile <section> with onClick + cursor:pointer)`)
  - The entire tile is a click target (onClick={onGo}, cursor:pointer) but is a plain <section> with no role="button"/role="link", no tabIndex, and no key handler. A keyboard or screen-reader user cannot activate the whole-surface affordance; the visible cursor:pointer promises an interaction that keyboard users cannot reach. The comment claims keyboard access is covered "через кнопку", but the tile's own click handler remains an inaccessible duplicate interactive surface. This also creates a nested-interactive issue (a focusable Button inside a click-handling section).
  - → Either (a) drop the section's onClick entirely and let ONLY the kit <Button> be the interactive element (simplest, removes nested-interactive + stopPropagation hack), or (b) if whole-card click is desired, make the title a real link/button (card-as-link pattern: an <a>/<button> wrapping the heading with the tile as a styled container) so there is a single focusable, keyboard-operable, screen-reader-announced control. Avoid putting role=button+tabIndex+onKeyDown on the section while also nesting a Button inside it.
- 🟠 **⌘K "Быстрый поиск раздела" is advertised but does nothing (silent no-op affordance)** (feedback-state · _Affordance rule (principles.md): an enabled element MUST do something_ · `screen-overview.jsx:215-227 (search hint + <kbd>⌘K</kbd>)`)
  - The header shows a search icon, the text "Быстрый поиск раздела — ⌘K" and a styled <kbd> shortcut, but this file wires no keydown listener and no command-palette trigger. If no global ⌘K handler exists elsewhere, this is a promised feature that silently fails — exactly the silent no-op the design contract forbids. Even if a global handler exists, the affordance is non-clickable (only a keyboard shortcut), unreachable on touch, and gives no hint of where the palette lives.
  - → Confirm a global ⌘K command palette exists; if not, either remove this hint or label it «скоро». If it exists, make the hint an actual clickable trigger (kit <Button variant=ghost> opening the palette) so mouse/touch users can invoke it too, not just a decorative <kbd>.
- 🟡 **Counter loads with no error-retry path — a failed fetch is silently indistinguishable from genuinely-zero / empty** (feedback-state · _States always explicit (Empty vs Error must differ); ErrorState onRetry_ · `screen-overview.jsx:78-87 (Stat renders «—» for value==null) + fetchCount 132-142`)
  - fetchCount collapses every failure (network/HTTP/parse) to null, and Stat renders «—». That is a reasonable graceful-degrade for a dashboard, but there is NO way for the user to retry, and «—» is ambiguous: it reads as "unavailable" but a user may misread it as "no data / zero". There is no aria-live or status semantics on the number, so a screen reader announces a bare em dash with no context. The contract calls for explicit Loading/Error/Empty states and an ErrorState with Повторить.
  - → Give «—» an accessible label/title (e.g. aria-label="данные недоступны") so it is not announced as a bare dash, and consider a lightweight retry (a ghost "обновить" affordance on the header, or re-fetch on focus/visibility) so a transient failure is recoverable without a full page reload.
- 🟡 **Tile heading is not a semantic heading — poor screen-reader document structure** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 Info and Relationships_ · `screen-overview.jsx:103 (tileTitleStyle on a <span>) and 213 page-title span`)
  - Each tile's title ('Конструктор', 'Работа', …) is a styled <span>, and the page itself has no <h1>. The screen therefore exposes no heading hierarchy; screen-reader users get no rotor/heading navigation across the four sections, and the <section> elements have only aria-label (acceptable) but no programmatic heading. The page-level context line is also a muted <span>, so there is no h1 landmark title.
  - → Render an <h1> for the screen (can be visually subtle) and make each tile title an <h2> (or use aria-labelledby pointing the section to its heading id). This restores heading-based navigation without changing the visual design.
- ⚪ **Two tiles render an empty stats row — wasted vertical slot, inconsistent tile rhythm** (consistency · _States always explicit / visual consistency_ · `screen-overview.jsx:191 & 199 (stats: []) → statsRowStyle at 60-63`)
  - "Исполнители и доступ" and "Наблюдаемость" pass stats: []. The statsRowStyle div still renders (with marginTop:auto / marginBottom) producing an empty gap and pushing CTAs to a different baseline than the two tiles that do have counters, so the 2x2 grid looks visually misaligned. It is not broken, but the rhythm is inconsistent.
  - → Skip rendering the stats row when stats.length === 0 (conditionally render the div), or give those tiles a cheap counter/teaser so all four tiles share the same vertical structure and CTA baseline.
- ⚪ **Loading spinner sits where a number will appear, with no reserved width — layout shift / non-tabular jitter** (feedback-state · _Skeleton/LoadingState; CLS_ · `screen-overview.jsx:81-83 (LoadingState compact inside statValStyle span)`)
  - While loading, the stat slot shows <LoadingState compact label=""> inside a span styled for a 2xl mono number. When the number arrives the box resizes from spinner-width to digit-width, causing a small layout shift; the contract provides <Skeleton> precisely for reserving the final shape. An empty label="" on LoadingState also strips its accessible status text.
  - → Use a number-shaped <Skeleton> (fixed min-width matching ~2-3 mono digits) instead of a spinner so the slot does not jump, and give the loading state a real (visually-hidden) label like «загрузка счётчика» rather than label="".
- ⚪ **Pervasive inline styles instead of token-mapped classes — maintainability and theme drift risk** (consistency · _Design-system token consumption_ · `screen-overview.jsx:30-72 (style objects) and 207-226 (inline style literals)`)
  - The file correctly uses only --chs-* tokens (no hardcoded hex/px for color — good), but it expresses the entire layout as ad-hoc inline style objects and literals rather than the kit's surface/card conventions. There is no <Card>/<Panel> primitive (a known gap), so the tile re-rolls surface+border+radius+padding by hand; fontWeight:600 is a raw literal instead of var(--chs-weight-semibold); two fixed '28px' icon-box dims and '320px' grid min bypass spacing tokens. This invites visual drift from other screens that assemble cards differently.
  - → Replace raw fontWeight:600 with var(--chs-weight-semibold) and the 28px icon box with spacing/size tokens; longer term, extract a shared Card/Tile surface (or promote one to the kit) so the home screen and other dashboards consume one surface definition rather than per-file inline objects.
- ⚪ **stats list keyed by array index** (ux-pattern · _React list keys (stability)_ · `screen-overview.jsx:107-108 (key={i})`)
  - Stat list uses the array index as key. Harmless for this static 1-2 item list today, but it is the kind of pattern that breaks state/animation if stats ever become dynamic, and it is flagged elsewhere in the codebase's lint conventions.
  - → Key by s.label (stable and unique within a tile) instead of the index.

### `src/screens/screen-process-analytics.jsx` — ⚪6
_Read-only process analytics dashboard showing per-step cycle-time (with bottleneck highlighting) and executor-type workload, with an optional per-process drill-down filter._

- ⚪ **Ad-hoc <table> instead of design-system row/table primitives, with table styling defined locally** (consistency · _DS consumption — no <Table>/<DataTable> primitive exists_ · `CycleTimeTable, lines 331-412 (thStyle/tdStyle/tableStyle defined lines 134-169)`)
  - The contract explicitly notes there is no <Table>/<DataTable> primitive, so tables are assembled ad hoc. This is sanctioned, but the consequence is that every analytics table re-derives its own th/td padding, uppercase-label treatment, and border tokens locally. As more observability screens land (reports/drilldown per MEMORY), these hand-rolled table styles will drift apart. This is consistency debt, not a correctness bug.
  - → Acceptable for now given the missing primitive. To prevent drift, extract the shared table style objects (tableStyle/thStyle/tdStyle/tdRightStyle) into a shared module or, better, propose a lightweight <DataTable>/<Th>/<Td> primitive in components.jsx so the cycle-time and actor tables across observability screens consume one source.
- ⚪ **DurationBar conveys magnitude by color/length only and is aria-hidden, with no accessible value** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.4.1 Use of Color / 1.1.1 Non-text Content_ · `DurationBar lines 253-280; used line 395`)
  - The horizontal bar visualizes avg_duration relative to max, but is marked aria-hidden="true" with no aria-label or value. This is acceptable ONLY because the numeric duration text (fmtDuration) sits right next to it in the same cell, so screen-reader users still get the value. The mild gap: sighted low-vision users relying on the bar get no tick/scale reference, and the bar's meaning (relative-to-slowest-step) is never stated anywhere in the UI. Minor.
  - → Keep aria-hidden (the adjacent text carries the value — correct). Optionally add a one-line caption under the section head like «Полоса — доля от самого долгого шага» so the relative encoding is explained, and ensure the accent fill keeps ≥3:1 contrast against the --chs-color-border track in both themes.
- ⚪ **«Обновить» button gives no feedback that a refresh occurred** (feedback-state · _feedback-after-action / Toast convention_ · `Button lines 564-572; load() lines 494-526`)
  - Clicking «Обновить» when data already exists tears down to the full-screen LoadingState (setData(null)) and back. For a fast endpoint the screen flashes; for an unchanged dataset the user gets no positive confirmation that data was re-fetched (e.g. «Обновлено» / timestamp). There is also no «last updated» indicator anywhere, which matters for an analytics dashboard where staleness is a real question.
  - → Show a lightweight «Обновлено в HH:MM» timestamp near the header (updated on each successful load) instead of, or in addition to, a transient toast. Consider an in-place refresh (keep prior data visible with an aria-busy overlay) rather than collapsing the whole screen to LoadingState on manual refresh, to avoid the jarring flash.
- ⚪ **Process selector option labels expose raw process_key (potential dev-jargon leak)** (content-clarity · _principles.md — no dev-jargon/IDs in UI_ · `selectOptions lines 553-556; scopeLabel/title lines 549-550, 563, 618`)
  - The selector and the H1 suffix render process keys verbatim (label: k). If process_key is a technical identifier (e.g. 'purchase_req_v2' / a BPMN key) rather than a human process name, this is a dev-jargon leak surfaced in the title, the description «Сейчас показано», the EmptyState title «Нет данных по процессу «{selected}»», and every option. The contract flags UUID/code-name leaks as violations.
  - → If the API can return a human-readable process name, render that as the option label and title, keeping the key only as the value. If only keys are available, confirm they are human-authored slugs; otherwise add a server-side display-name field. At minimum, avoid putting the raw key in the H1.
- ⚪ **«узкое место» pill is a styled <span>, not the DS status/chip primitive** (consistency · _DS consumption — StatusChip/OpChip/DerivedChip family_ · `lines 375-392`)
  - The bottleneck marker is a hand-rolled pill (inline padding '1px 6px', radius-full, accent-soft bg, accent-border). The contract notes there is no generic <Badge> beyond status/exec/op chips, so a one-off is understandable, but a raw '1px 6px' padding bypasses spacing tokens and duplicates chip styling that the <DerivedChip>/<OpChip> family already encapsulates. The aria-label/title are good (meaning is dup'd in text + region).
  - → Reuse an existing chip primitive (e.g. a neutral/accent <DerivedChip> or a small chip variant) for the «узкое место» marker, or replace the literal '1px 6px' with --chs-space tokens (radius-1 for a chip, not radius-full) to stay token-compliant.
- ⚪ **Error and 401/403 states drop the process selector, trapping the user on one scope** (flow · _error-recovery / state composition_ · `selector render lines 582-601 vs error branch 607-613`)
  - If a drill-down fetch for a specific process fails (e.g. transient 500), the selector is hidden (it only renders when hasProcesses, and on error data===false so the whole data region collapses to ErrorState). The user can Retry the same failing scope but cannot switch back to «Все процессы» without it succeeding first — a mild recovery dead-end. The 401 «Войдите в систему» also has no actionable path (no login link/CTA).
  - → Keep the process selector visible during the error state (it can stay enabled, driven by the last-known processKeys) so the user can switch scope to recover. For the 401 case, give ErrorState an actionable CTA (link to login) rather than instructional text only.

### `src/screens/screen-reports.jsx` — 🟠3 🟡4 ⚪3
_Lists an application's report pages, renders Floor-1 aggregate metrics (cards + bar chart + grouped table), lets a user build/edit/publish a report from registry fields, and export to XLSX/CSV._

- 🟠 **No success feedback after save/publish/export — silent outcomes violate the toast convention** (feedback-state · _Toast / transient outcome (design-system §4)_ · `ReportBuilder.handleSubmit (lines 752-820); ReportViewer.handleExport (lines 501-507)`)
  - On successful Сохранить черновик / Опубликовать the only signal is the builder closing and the list reloading — there is no confirmation that the draft saved or that it was actually published. Export success is likewise silent (browser just downloads a file; if the OS download is hidden the user sees nothing happen). The contract mandates a Toast for transient outcomes and the screen never wires useToasts()/ToastViewport. Errors get an inline role=alert but the positive path is mute, which on a publish (a state transition with authz consequences) is a real ambiguity.
  - → Wire useToasts()+<ToastViewport> in this tree and emit a polite success toast on save («Черновик сохранён»), publish («Отчёт опубликован»), and export start/finish. Keep the inline role=alert for errors.
- 🟠 **Publish is a destructive/irreversible state transition with no confirmation** (ux-pattern · _ConfirmDialog for consequential action (design-system §3)_ · `ReportBuilder buttons, lines 928-930; promote call lines 793-804`)
  - «Опубликовать» moves the report draft→published in one click. Once published the report loses its «Изменить» affordance (ReportViewer hides edit when report.tier === 'published', line 518), so publishing is effectively irreversible from this UI — yet there is no confirmation step. The contract provides <ConfirmDialog> precisely for focus/confirm of consequential actions.
  - → Gate «Опубликовать» behind <ConfirmDialog> («Опубликовать отчёт? После публикации его нельзя будет изменить здесь.»), or provide an unpublish/edit-published path so the action is reversible.
- 🟠 **Partial publish failure is reported as if nothing went wrong** (feedback-state · _Error recovery / honest state_ · `ReportBuilder.handleSubmit, lines 798-814`)
  - When create/patch succeeds but the promote call fails, the code sets submitErr but does NOT return — execution falls through to onSaved({... tier: promote ? 'published' ...}) at line 806-814. So the builder closes, the list reloads, and the optimistic onSaved marks the row tier:'published' even though the server rejected publication. The user is told (via submitErr that gets discarded on close) and shown contradictory state: a 'published' badge for a report that is still a draft on the server. The list reload later corrects the badge, creating a flicker, but the submitErr alert is never seen because the builder unmounts.
  - → On promote failure, keep the builder open and surface submitErr (do not unmount), OR pass the real resulting tier to onSaved by reading the server's response rather than the optimistic promote flag. Do not claim 'published' when the promote response was not ok.
- 🟡 **App selector is a raw <select> instead of the kit <Select>/<Field>** (consistency · _Design-system primitive consumption (contract flags: raw <select> without <Select>)_ · `lines 1116-1127 (and styles appSelectorSelectStyle 86-95)`)
  - The application picker is a hand-rolled native <select> with a hardcoded style block (minWidth:200 px raw value, manual border/padding), while the rest of the file correctly uses the kit <Select>. This is exactly the 'rolled-its-own input' flag in the contract: inconsistent focus ring, no aria-invalid plumbing, and a raw px literal (minWidth: 200) outside the token system. It also duplicates the label/control wiring the kit already provides.
  - → Replace with <Select label="Приложение" value={selectedAppId} options={apps.map(...)} onChange={...}/>. Drop appSelectorSelectStyle and the raw minWidth px.
- 🟡 **Raw UUID / page_code leaks to the user as a visible title fallback** (content-clarity · _No dev-jargon in UI (principles: no UUIDs/code-names)_ · `report list label line 1190 (page.title ?? page.page_code ?? page.id); panel title line 516; builder/list`)
  - When a report has no title, the UI falls all the way back to page.id — a raw UUID — both in the sidebar list and as the <h2> panel heading. The contract explicitly forbids surfacing UUIDs/dev-jargon in the UI. A titleless report (possible since title is the user's free-text field) would show e.g. a 36-char UUID as its name, which is unscannable and unprofessional.
  - → Replace the id fallback with a human placeholder, e.g. «Без названия» (and optionally a muted <MonoId> chip for power users). Never render a bare UUID as the primary label.
- 🟡 **Grouped-metric table is assembled ad hoc with hardcoded px and no header scope** (consistency · _Table scannability + token usage (contract: no <Table> primitive, tables assembled ad hoc)_ · `MetricCard grouped branch, lines 419-442`)
  - The grouped table inlines padding:'4px 8px' as raw px literals (multiple occurrences) instead of spacing tokens, and th cells lack scope="col". For a numeric data table this also misses the contract's guidance that numeric/money columns use --chs-font-mono (the value column uses tabular-nums but not the mono family). It's a one-off table with no row-hover or zebra, hurting scannability for longer grouped results.
  - → Swap the 4px/8px literals for --chs-space tokens, add scope="col" on the th, and apply --chs-font-mono to the numeric result column to match the design contract for numeric columns.
- 🟡 **Bar chart conveys data by color/length only and truncates labels silently** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.1.1 / 1.4.1 — non-text content & info not by visual alone_ · `BarChart, lines 248-331 (label truncation line 314, group value text)`)
  - The SVG has a single generic aria-label («Столбчатый график») but the individual bar values/labels are <text> nodes that screen readers do not reliably expose as structured data, so the actual numbers are not programmatically available. Group labels are hard-truncated to 12 chars (slice(0,12)) with no title/tooltip, so a reader sees an ambiguous stub like «Поставщик ОО» with no way to get the full value. Bar value text is also a hardcoded fontSize="9" (below the token scale) which is tiny and outside the type system.
  - → Since MetricCard already renders the full grouped table right below the chart, mark the SVG aria-hidden="true" (data is available in the accessible table), OR give each bar a <title> with full group+value. Add a <title> element to each group <text> for the full untruncated key, and replace fontSize="9" with a token-based size (e.g. --chs-text-2xs).
- ⚪ **Per-metric field labels only render on the first row, leaving lower rows visually unlabeled** (accessibility · _Forms — persistent labels (WCAG 3.3.2)_ · `MetricRow, lines 619-648 (label={index === 0 ? ... : undefined})`)
  - Visible <Select>/<Field> labels render only on row 0; rows 2+ rely solely on aria-label. This is an intentional dense-table pattern and is screen-reader-accessible, but sighted keyboard/low-context users scanning row 3 have no visible column header tying the control to its meaning if the first row scrolls out of view. There is no <thead>-style column header for the metric grid.
  - → Acceptable as a dense pattern, but consider a persistent lightweight column-header row above the metric rows (Тип подсчёта / Поле / Подпись) so labels survive scrolling, rather than depending on row 0 staying visible.
- ⚪ **Two adjacent export buttons with inconsistent labeling («Выгрузить XLSX» vs «CSV»)** (content-clarity · _Microcopy consistency_ · `ReportViewer, lines 523-540`)
  - The XLSX button reads «Выгрузить XLSX» (verb+format) while the CSV button reads just «CSV» (format only). The asymmetry makes the CSV control read like a label/tab rather than an action, and three ghost buttons in a row (Выгрузить XLSX / CSV / Обновить) are visually undifferentiated.
  - → Make labels parallel — either «Выгрузить XLSX» / «Выгрузить CSV», or a single «Выгрузить ▾» split/menu button with format options, reducing the ghost-button cluster.
- ⚪ **Builder hides the report list with no way to view existing reports while editing** (flow · _Drawer/in-context detail vs full takeover_ · `ReportsScreen render, lines 1136-1174 (builder !== undefined hides the whole list)`)
  - Opening the builder swaps out the entire list+viewer area (comment line 1146: «список ниже скрыт»). A user building a new report can no longer reference the existing reports they're modeling against, and the «Обновить» / list controls are disabled. For an edit launched from a specific report, losing sight of that report's current rendered output is a minor context loss.
  - → Consider rendering the builder as a <Drawer> (detail-in-context per the surface-choice rule) or above the still-visible list, so reference data stays on screen during authoring.

### `src/screens/screen-spend.jsx` — 🟡3 ⚪5
_Read-only observability screen showing LLM-call spend: summary by time window, breakdown by connection, and a table of the 50 most recent calls._

- 🟡 **Tables are hand-rolled with inline-style th/td instead of design-system primitives** (consistency · _design-system consumption / data-table_ · `screen-spend.jsx:42-69 (tableStyle/thStyle/tdStyle/monoStyle) and tables at :246-275, :297-336`)
  - Both tables are assembled from local inline-style objects (thStyle, tdStyle, monoStyle) rather than any shared row/cell unit. The contract notes there is no <Table>/<DataTable> primitive, but it does expose <Mono>/<MonoId> for monospace IDs and provider codes — instead the file rolls its own monoStyle with a hardcoded '1px 5px' padding. This drifts from kit-rendered tables elsewhere and guarantees visual inconsistency (border weights, header casing, padding) versus other screens.
  - → Replace the local monoStyle usages (provider cell, :261) with the kit <Mono> primitive. Factor the th/td styling into the nearest shared table convention used by other observability screens, or at minimum lift the style objects into a shared module so all ad-hoc tables match. Flag the absence of a <DataTable> primitive to the design-system owners since several screens assemble tables ad hoc.
- 🟡 **Hardcoded pixel values violate token-only contract** (consistency · _design tokens_ · `screen-spend.jsx:68 (monoStyle padding '1px 5px'), :187 (maxWidth: 940), :330 & :312 (maxWidth: 200)`)
  - The file header claims 'строго OBLIK — только --chs-* токены', but several raw px values are present: monoStyle padding '1px 5px', the page maxWidth 940, and the description-cell maxWidth 200. Per the contract, hardcoded size = violation. The monoStyle padding is the clearest breach since a token-driven <Mono> would carry correct padding.
  - → Move monoStyle to the <Mono> primitive (removes the hardcoded padding). For the layout widths, either accept them as layout constants documented as exceptions or map them onto spacing/size tokens. At minimum remove the '1px 5px' literal.
- 🟡 **Two independent tables lack a shared refresh/error model; 'Обновить' gives no feedback** (feedback-state · _feedback after action / toast_ · `screen-spend.jsx:192-195 (Обновить button), loadOverview/loadRecent`)
  - Clicking 'Обновить' fires both loaders but produces no transient confirmation; the only signal is the inline LoadingState flashing inside each section. If both requests succeed silently and data is unchanged, the user cannot tell whether the refresh actually ran. The contract calls out that outcomes should use Toast for transient feedback and that every action should visibly do something.
  - → On successful manual refresh, surface a brief polite Toast (e.g. 'Данные обновлены') via useToasts()/ToastViewport, or show a subtle 'обновлено в HH:MM' timestamp near the button. Consider disabling/marking the button as loading (Button has a `loading` prop) while requests are in flight to prevent double-fires and signal progress.
- ⚪ **Refresh button is not disabled during in-flight requests** (feedback-state · _loading state / double-submit guard_ · `screen-spend.jsx:192-195`)
  - The 'Обновить' button stays fully enabled while loadOverview/loadRecent are running. Rapid clicks fire overlapping fetches; there is no loading affordance on the control itself. The kit Button supports a `loading` prop precisely for this.
  - → Track an in-flight boolean (e.g. derived from overview===null || recent===null after a manual refresh) and pass `loading` / `disabled` to the Button so repeat clicks are blocked and progress is visible on the control.
- ⚪ **Empty 'total' window renders a contradictory '$0.0000' alongside '— вызовов нет'** (content-clarity · _empty state / microcopy_ · `screen-spend.jsx:218-223`)
  - When a window has no data (d is undefined), the amount falls back to a literal '$0.0000' while the subline shows '— вызовов нет'. Showing a precise 4-decimal USD zero for a window that has literally zero calls is misleading (implies a measured $0.0000 in a currency the tenant may not even use) and the em-dash prefix on '— вызовов нет' reads awkwardly.
  - → When d is undefined, render '—' for the amount (matching the fmtAmount null convention) and a clean subline like 'Вызовов нет' without the leading dash. Reserve '$0.0000' for the case where d exists with a real zero total.
- ⚪ **Money formatting hardcodes 4 decimals and a '$' symbol, weak for non-USD and large sums** (content-clarity · _localization / number formatting_ · `screen-spend.jsx:105-109 (fmtAmount)`)
  - fmtAmount always prints amount.toFixed(4) and prefixes non-USD currencies as 'RUB 12.3400'. The contract designates --chs-font-mono and a ₽ currency convention; per memory the product uses ₽. A bare currency code prefix plus fixed 4-decimal precision is hard to scan for ruble sums and inconsistent with the platform's money formatting. The summary cards also do not use the mono font for money, though the contract says money/numeric columns should be mono.
  - → Use Intl.NumberFormat('ru-RU', { style:'currency', currency }) (or the platform's money helper) so ₽/локаль render correctly, and apply --chs-font-mono to the amount cells/cards per the typography contract. Keep 4-decimal precision only where micro-costs warrant it.
- ⚪ **Recent-calls table column headers 'Вх / Исх' are cryptic abbreviations** (content-clarity · _microcopy / table headers_ · `screen-spend.jsx:302-303`)
  - The recent table abbreviates prompt/completion token columns to 'Вх' and 'Исх'. Without a header tooltip or legend these are guessable but ambiguous for non-engineer admins (input vs output tokens). The contract values clear microcopy and dense-but-scannable B2B tables.
  - → Expand to 'Вход. токены' / 'Исход. токены' (or 'Промпт'/'Ответ'), or keep the short label but add a title/Tooltip on the th explaining each. Ensure the meaning is discoverable without domain knowledge.
- ⚪ **401 copy says 'Войдите в систему' but offers no path to log in** (flow · _error recovery_ · `screen-spend.jsx:142, :159 (rendered via ErrorState onRetry)`)
  - On a 401 the ErrorState shows 'Войдите в систему.' with only a Повторить (retry) action. Retrying the same unauthenticated fetch will just 401 again; the message tells the user to log in but provides no affordance to do so, which is a dead-end recovery flow.
  - → For the 401 branch, either route the user to the login flow (a primary action/link in the ErrorState) or rely on the app shell's global auth redirect. Avoid offering only 'Повторить' for an auth error since the retry cannot succeed without re-authentication.

### `src/canvas/agent-task-panel.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡3 ⚪2
_Typed configuration sub-panel inside the BPMN properties panel for an agentTask element: pick the executing agent, set the per-step autonomy ceiling, and declare which record fields the agent reads/writes._

- 🟠 **«Порог автономии» uses a raw native <select> instead of the <Select> primitive** (consistency · _design-system: rolled-its-own raw <select> without <Select>/<Field>_ · `agent-task-panel.jsx:189-196 (bio-properties-panel-select div + bare <select>)`)
  - The autonomy-level control is hand-built from a bare <label> + native <select> with bpmn vendor classes, while the sibling 'Агент' control on the very same panel uses the kit <Select>. This is inconsistent within one panel: the two dropdowns will not share styling, focus-ring, aria-invalid handling, or label↔control binding. The contract explicitly flags 'raw <select> without <Select>' as a violation, and the kit <Select> already exists and is imported in this file.
  - → Replace the bio-properties-panel-select block with <Select label="Порог автономии" options={AUTONOMY_LEVELS} value={autonomyLevel} onChange={(e) => handleAutonomy(e.target.value)} hint="..." />, matching the 'Агент' control. This gives free label binding, focus-ring via --chs-color-focus-ring, and visual parity.
- 🟡 **Native <select> label is not programmatically bound to the control** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 Info and Relationships / 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value_ · `agent-task-panel.jsx:190-191 (<label> 'Порог автономии' + <select>)`)
  - The hand-rolled control pairs a <label> with a <select> by visual proximity only — there is no htmlFor/id association and no aria-label on the <select>. Screen readers will announce the combobox without its 'Порог автономии' name, and clicking the label will not focus the control. The kit <Select> (used for 'Агент') handles this binding automatically; this bespoke block does not.
  - → Adopting the kit <Select> (see related finding) fixes this for free. If kept native, add id to the <select>, htmlFor to the <label>, and an aria-label fallback.
- 🟡 **Load failure shows a static apology with no retry affordance** (feedback-state · _design-system: <ErrorState onRetry> / affordance rule_ · `agent-task-panel.jsx:163-170 (agentsError branch); useAgents has no refetch path`)
  - On a non-401 load failure the panel renders 'Не удалось загрузить агентов: … Повторите позже.' as a dead-end paragraph. The contract's error convention is role=alert PLUS a «Повторить» action (ErrorState onRetry), and the affordance rule says the user must be given a way forward. Here the only recovery is to deselect and reselect the element (since the fetchedRef guard blocks re-fetch), which the author cannot guess. The copy literally says 'повторите' but offers no control to do so.
  - → Expose a retry from useAgents (reset fetchedRef + loading) and render the error via the kit <ErrorState onRetry={retry}> (or at least a kit <Button variant="ghost">Повторить</Button>). Drop the 'Повторите позже' wording once a real retry exists.
- 🟡 **Edits auto-commit on every keystroke/change with zero feedback that the change was saved** (feedback-state · _feedback after action (save) — Toast/status convention_ · `agent-task-panel.jsx:152-155 (handleAgentRef/Autonomy/Reads/Writes → commit) and 147-150 (commit → writeAgentConfig)`)
  - Each handler writes straight to the businessObject and fires element.changed with no confirmation, and writeAgentConfig's result is not checked. For the dropdowns the change is visible, but the two free-text field lists (reads/writes) silently persist on every keystroke with no saved/error signal. If a write ever throws it is swallowed (fireChanged is try/caught) and the author has no idea their config did or did not stick. This is the kind of silent outcome the toast/state convention exists to prevent.
  - → It is acceptable for properties-panel edits to be implicit-save, but surface failure: wrap writeAgentConfig in try/catch and raise a kit error <Toast> (role=alert) on failure. Consider a subtle 'сохранено' affordance consistent with the rest of the properties panel.
- ⚪ **Free-text field-key inputs have no validation or empty/invalid feedback** (content-clarity · _form validation UX_ · `agent-task-panel.jsx:204-213 and 216-229 (reads/writes <Field>)`)
  - Reads/writes fields are raw comma-separated key strings (mono). There is no validation that the entered keys correspond to actual record fields, no aria-invalid/hint-on-error, and no normalization feedback (e.g. trailing commas, duplicate keys, unknown field). An author can type a typo that silently produces a non-functional agent step at runtime — the cost of the error is deferred to execution where it is hard to diagnose.
  - → If the record's field catalog is available, validate entered keys against it and surface unknown keys via the <Field> hint/aria-invalid path. At minimum, trim/dedupe on blur and show a hint when a key is not recognized. A multi-select/token input over the known field list would remove the free-text failure mode entirely.
- ⚪ **Inline hardcoded style/color instead of consuming a kit component or class** (consistency · _design-system: tokenized styling, no inline style for semantic color_ · `agent-task-panel.jsx:167 (style color var(--chs-color-danger)) and 197/225 (style marginTop)`)
  - Error color and spacing are applied via inline style attributes. The token values themselves are correct (--chs-color-danger, --chs-space-2), so this is not a hex/px violation, but inline styling for a semantic error message bypasses the kit's <ErrorState>/<Toast> presentation and scatters one-off styles. The contract steers reviewers toward consuming primitives rather than re-styling paragraphs ad hoc.
  - → Route the error through the kit <ErrorState> (which already carries the danger styling and role=alert), eliminating the inline color. Keep spacing in the panel's CSS/PPEntry rather than per-element inline style.

### `src/canvas/bpmn-modeler-wrapper.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡2 ⚪1
_A controlled React wrapper that mounts the real bpmn-js BpmnModeler into a DOM container, imports BPMN XML (backend or default), applies Choros executor-type marker classes, and forwards the modeler instance plus onReady/onError to the parent process-editor screen._

- 🟠 **Canvas keyboard navigation is fully disabled — diagram is mouse-only** (accessibility · _WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard (Level A)_ · `bpmn-modeler-wrapper.jsx:172-173 (keyboard: { bindTo: null })`)
  - BpmnModeler is instantiated with keyboard.bindTo=null, so bpmn-js registers no keyboard handlers. The modeler canvas — the core editing surface of a process-authoring tool — cannot be panned, zoomed, undone/redone, or have selections moved via keyboard. The inline comment ('disabled for now') confirms this is unfinished, not a deliberate accessible alternative. The parent provides toolbar buttons for undo/redo/zoom, which mitigates a few actions, but element selection/movement on the canvas itself remains keyboard-inaccessible. This is a Level A failure for a primary interactive surface.
  - → Bind bpmn-js keyboard to the container: pass keyboard: { bindTo: containerRef.current } (or document) once the container is mounted, OR explicitly document and provide a complete keyboard-operable alternative for every canvas action. At minimum remove the 'for now' placeholder and make a deliberate decision; a no-code B2B authoring canvas that cannot be operated by keyboard is a hard accessibility gap.
- 🟠 **Canvas container has no accessible name or role — opaque to assistive tech** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value / 1.1.1 Non-text Content (Level A)_ · `bpmn-modeler-wrapper.jsx:242-249 (<div ref={containerRef} className="chs-bpmn-real-container">)`)
  - The mount div is rendered with no role, no aria-label/aria-labelledby, and no text alternative. bpmn-js fills it with an SVG diagram that is itself a wall of <g>/<path> with no semantic exposure. A screen-reader user reaching this region hears nothing meaningful — there is no 'Диаграмма процесса', no description of the process, and no fallback summary of the steps. The diagram is purely visual information with no text equivalent (the contract requires executor meaning to be dup'd with text; here even the diagram's existence is unannounced).
  - → Add role="application" (or role="img" with an aria-label if treated as a static figure) plus an aria-label like 'Редактор диаграммы процесса' on the container div, and accept the value via a prop so callers can name the specific process. Consider rendering a visually-hidden text summary of the steps/executors (sourced from the same model the markers walk) as a text alternative for the SVG, satisfying 1.1.1.
- 🟡 **No loading affordance during importXML — canvas is blank until parse resolves** (feedback-state · _States always explicit (LoadingState role=status aria-busy)_ · `bpmn-modeler-wrapper.jsx:189-191 (modeler.importXML(...).then) and the returned <div>`)
  - importXML is async. Between mount and resolve, the wrapper renders only an empty container with no loading indicator and no aria-busy. The parent screen-process-editor.jsx does show <LoadingState> for its own data-fetch phase (loadPhase==='loading'), but it flips to 'ready' and mounts this wrapper BEFORE the bpmn-js parse/layout completes — so there is a window where the user sees a blank canvas with no signal that rendering is in progress. For large/slow diagrams this reads as 'nothing happened'.
  - → Set aria-busy on the container until the importXML promise settles, and either render a kit <LoadingState>/<Skeleton> overlay inside the wrapper during that window, or expose an onLoadingChange/loading flag so the parent can keep its LoadingState mounted until onReady fires rather than the moment data arrives.
- 🟡 **Error path notifies parent but leaves a deceptively normal-looking empty canvas** (feedback-state · _ErrorState role=alert + Повторить_ · `bpmn-modeler-wrapper.jsx:220-230 (.catch → onError) `)
  - On importXML rejection the wrapper logs and calls onError(message), which is the right escalation and a real improvement over silent failure (T-0484). But the wrapper still renders its empty container, and the recovery (ErrorState / retry) depends entirely on the parent wiring onError into a real <ErrorState onRetry>. There is no retry mechanism in the wrapper itself, and onError is optional (typeof check) — if a future caller forgets it, the failure silently reverts to a blank canvas. The error message is also a fixed Russian string built here, mixing presentation concerns into the canvas wrapper.
  - → Keep delegating, but make the contract safer: in dev, warn when importXML rejects and no onError handler is provided; and ensure the parent's onError path renders the kit <ErrorState role=alert onRetry={remount}> with an actual retry (re-import). Consider passing a structured error (code/raw message) to onError rather than a pre-formatted UI string so the presentation layer owns copy.
- ⚪ **Hardcoded default diagram surfaces dev-jargon IDs and a fixed domain example** (content-clarity · _No dev-jargon in UI (principles.md)_ · `bpmn-modeler-wrapper.jsx:57-134 (DEFAULT_DIAGRAM_XML)`)
  - When a brand-new (blank) definition is created, the user is dropped into a pre-populated 4-step invoice-approval example ('Согласовать счёт' → 'Проверить реквизиты' → 'Провести платёж'). The element ids (Activity_user, Activity_agent, StartEvent_1, Flow_1) are technical and may leak into properties panels/labels; more importantly, a fixed business example as the default-new state can confuse users authoring an unrelated process — they must first delete someone else's invoice flow. This straddles content-clarity and onboarding rather than a true blank-start.
  - → For a genuinely new definition, default to a minimal Start→End (or empty) canvas with an EmptyState-style hint ('Перетащите шаг из палитры, чтобы начать'), and reserve the rich invoice example for an explicit 'вставить пример' action. Ensure technical element ids never surface as user-facing labels in any consuming panel.

### `src/canvas/bpmn-properties-panel.jsx` — 🟠3 🟡4 ⚪3
_Right-hand BPMN properties panel: lets the process author edit the selected canvas element's name, executor type, role assignment, BYO-LLM, step outcomes/branches, form binding, message correlation, and timers._

- 🟠 **Collapsible group header is a div with role=button but no keyboard handler** (accessibility · _WCAG 2.1.1 (Keyboard) / 4.1.2 Name-Role-Value_ · `PanelGroup, bpmn-properties-panel.jsx:213-225`)
  - The group header that expands/collapses every panel section uses <div role="button" aria-expanded onClick> with no tabIndex and no onKeyDown. It is unreachable and unoperable by keyboard — a keyboard or screen-reader user cannot open or close any section (Общее, Назначение, Исходы шага, etc.). Both MCP tools flagged this (review_code line 79, check_accessibility 2.1.1 critical). The kit has no <Accordion> primitive, but a real <button> must be used.
  - → Replace the header <div role="button"> with a real <button type="button"> (transparent, full-width, styled via the bio-properties-panel-group-header class), keeping aria-expanded. This gives free Enter/Space activation and focus-ring. If a div must stay for layout, add tabIndex={0} and an onKeyDown handler for Enter/Space.
