- 🟠 **Raw <input>/<select> used instead of kit <Field>/<Select> across the outcome and flow editors** (consistency · _design-system: <Field>/<Select> label-control binding_ · `OutcomeRow (lines 392-463), OutcomesPanel preset picker (575-583), SequenceFlowOutcomePanel (680-687), General ID/name/exec-type (1304-1352)`)
  - The contract names raw <input>/<select> without <Field>/<Select> as an explicit consumption violation. MessageCorrelationPanel and UserTaskFormBindingPanel correctly use kit <Field>, but OutcomeRow (name, button-label, color, target, target-id), the preset picker, the sequence-flow outcome-name field, and the entire General group all hand-roll <input className="bio-properties-panel-input"> / <select> with a sibling <label> that has no htmlFor binding. review_code flagged 8 of these as 'Missing Input Label' — the labels are not programmatically associated, so aria-invalid/hint/describedby are also absent. Result: inconsistent focus-ring, no aria-invalid path, and label/control association breaks for screen readers.
  - → Migrate these controls to kit <Field> (text inputs) and <Select> (color, target-kind, preset dropdowns), which handle label↔control binding, aria-invalid and hint. Where the bio-properties-panel-* visual is required for parity with bpmn-js chrome, at minimum bind each <label htmlFor> to a generated input id so the association is real.
- 🟠 **Destructive 'Удалить исход' deletes immediately with no confirmation and no undo feedback** (feedback-state · _destructive-action confirmation; affordance rule_ · `OutcomeRow remove button (lines 486-502) → handleRemoveOutcome (561-567)`)
  - Clicking 'Удалить исход' filters the outcome out of state and commits to the businessObject in one click, with no ConfirmDialog and no toast. The contract requires <ConfirmDialog> in place of silent destructive actions and a Toast for transient outcomes. An author can wipe a configured branch (with its routing target, requiresComment, confirm flags) by a single mis-click and gets zero feedback that it happened or how to revert.
  - → Either gate the removal behind <ConfirmDialog> (the outcome carries real config), or — since this lives inside the modeler — route the change through bpmn-js modeling so it joins undo/redo, and surface a Toast ('Исход удалён') with the standard kit pattern. At minimum show a confirm for outcomes that have a non-default targetKind/target.
- 🟡 **No save/feedback after any property edit — all writes are silent mutations** (feedback-state · _feedback after action; States always explicit_ · `handleNameChange (1190-1203), handleExecTypeChange (1152-1184), handleRoleChange (1208-1216), OutcomesPanel.commit (518-528), MessageCorrelationPanel handlers (808-832)`)
  - Every edit mutates the businessObject and fires element.changed, but nothing in this panel confirms the change persisted. There is no toast, no 'сохранено' affordance, no dirty/saved indicator. In a no-code process editor the author has no signal that the executor type, role, outcome config, message-correlation key, or BYO endpoint they just typed is actually captured — they must trust that closing the modeler will saveXML. The kit provides Toast + useToasts exactly for this transient-outcome case.
  - → Surface a lightweight saved/applied signal. For the live recolor (exec-type) the canvas already reflects it; for off-canvas config (outcomes, message correlation, BYO, role) add a subtle 'изменения применяются к процессу' status or a debounced toast, or a panel-level 'не сохранено / сохранено в схеме' indicator so the author trusts the writes.
- 🟡 **Dev-jargon and internal identifiers leak into user-facing labels and hints** (content-clarity · _principles: no dev-jargon in UI_ · `SequenceFlowOutcomePanel label 'Имя исхода (choros:outcomeName)' (679); exec-type note 'bpmn:ServiceTask + choros:executorType="agent"' (1360-1362); MessageCorrelationPanel 'Канал отправки (effect_resource id)' (879) and placeholder 'messaging_channel uuid' (882); EXEC_OPTIONS / BPMN_TYPE_LABELS show raw 'bpmn:UserTask' style strings`)
  - The contract forbids dev-jargon, code-names, and UUIDs in the UI. This panel surfaces moddle attribute names (choros:outcomeName), raw BPMN $type strings (bpmn:ServiceTask, bpmn:UserTask), internal table/column names (effect_resource id, messaging_channel uuid) directly in labels, descriptions, and placeholders. For an internal no-code/process audience this is confusing — these are implementation identifiers, not domain concepts.
  - → Strip implementation identifiers from labels: 'Имя исхода (choros:outcomeName)' → 'Имя исхода (ветки)'; exec-type note should describe behavior in plain Russian, not the BPMN/attr expression; 'Канал отправки (effect_resource id)' → 'Канал отправки' with a human placeholder. Keep technical identifiers, if needed, in a tooltip/hint rather than the primary label.
- 🟡 **Hardcoded hex color fallbacks violate the token contract** (consistency · _design-system: tokens are the single source; hardcoded color = violation_ · `OutcomeRow remove button color 'var(--chs-color-danger, #c00)' (495); OutcomesPanel add-outcome color 'var(--chs-color-brand, #2563eb)' (625); MessageCorrelationPanel warning 'var(--chs-color-warning, inherit)' (904)`)
  - The contract states hardcoded color = violation and that semantics should be consumed directly. Several styles embed raw hex fallbacks (#c00, #2563eb) inside var() defaults. Also --chs-color-brand is referenced but the contract's accent token is --chs-color-accent — the brand var may be undefined, in which case the literal #2563eb (a non-token blue) renders, defeating theming and WCAG-AA guarantees in dark theme.
  - → Drop the hex fallbacks and use the canonical semantic tokens: danger → var(--chs-color-danger), accent for the add action → var(--chs-color-accent). Verify --chs-color-brand exists; if not, switch to --chs-color-accent so the link-style buttons theme correctly.
- 🟡 **Rolled-its-own <button> for add/remove actions instead of kit <Button>** (consistency · _design-system: app-content buttons must use <Button>_ · `OutcomeRow remove (487-502), OutcomesPanel add-outcome (618-632)`)
  - Both action buttons are bespoke <button style={{background:'none',border:'none',...}}>. The contract allows bespoke buttons only for shell nav (ThemeToggle/NavItem); app-content buttons must use <Button variant…>. These lose the kit's focus-ring, aria-disabled/aria-busy handling, and consistent sizing. The 'Удалить исход' should be variant="danger" or ghost; '+ Добавить исход' should be a ghost/secondary Button.
  - → Replace with <Button variant="ghost" glyph="plus">Добавить исход</Button> and <Button variant="danger" size="sm">Удалить исход</Button>, removing the inline styles so focus-ring and theming come from the kit.
- ⚪ **Checkbox controls hand-rolled with no kit wrapper and label-association by nesting only** (consistency · _forms/checkbox; design-system gap (no <Checkbox> wrapper)_ · `OutcomeRow flags (467-482), MessageCorrelationPanel broadcast (860-871)`)
  - Checkboxes use raw <input type="checkbox"> wrapped in a styled <label> with inline fontSize/cursor. The kit has no <Checkbox> primitive (a noted absence), so this is partially unavoidable, but the inline-styled labels duplicate styling logic across components and rely solely on label-nesting for association. They also don't get the kit focus-ring token. The broadcast checkbox has aria-label but the OutcomeRow checkboxes rely on adjacent text only.
  - → Given the missing primitive, flag a <Checkbox> kit addition as follow-up; in the interim factor a single local Checkbox helper so styling/focus-ring/association are consistent, and ensure each input carries an explicit id+htmlFor or aria-label.
- ⚪ **EmptyState is bespoke instead of the kit <EmptyState> primitive and offers no action** (consistency · _design-system: <EmptyState role=status> + CTA; States always explicit_ · `EmptyState component (993-1034)`)
  - The 'nothing selected' state is hand-built rather than using the kit <EmptyState> (which provides role=status and a CTA slot). It is purely informational text in --chs-color-text-faint with no role=status for assistive tech. This is a minor, defensible deviation (it must mimic the bpmn-js panel chrome), but it diverges from the contract's state-primitive convention.
  - → Either adopt <EmptyState> for the no-selection case (role=status, centered icon + 'Выберите элемент' copy) or, if the bio-properties chrome must be preserved, add role="status" to the message container so the empty state is announced consistently with the rest of the app.
- ⚪ **Roles loading state uses a bare paragraph instead of the kit <LoadingState>** (feedback-state · _design-system: <LoadingState role=status aria-busy>_ · `Назначение group, rolesLoading branch (1405-1406)`)
  - Role loading renders <p>Загрузка ролей…</p> with no role=status/aria-busy. The error branch correctly uses role="alert", and the error microcopy is honest (good), but loading is a bare paragraph, so assistive tech does not announce the busy state and it is visually inconsistent with the kit's <LoadingState>/<Spinner>. The error branch also lacks a retry affordance (contract: Error = role=alert + Повторить), telling the user to 'Повторите позже' without a button.
  - → Use <LoadingState> (or at least role="status" aria-busy) for the loading branch, and give the error branch an actionable retry — either <ErrorState onRetry> or a small Повторить button that re-triggers the tenant-state fetch — rather than the passive 'Повторите позже' text.

### `src/canvas/gateway-condition-panel.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡4 ⚪2
_Properties-panel section for an exclusive gateway: lets the modeler name a routing variable and assign per-branch values (and one default branch) that compile to BPMN conditionExpressions._

- 🟠 **Raw <input> elements instead of design-system <Field> primitive** (consistency · _forms/text-field_ · `gateway-condition-panel.jsx:185 (branch value), :431 (routing var)`)
  - Both text inputs are hand-rolled bare <input class="bio-properties-panel-input"> wrapped in bpmn-js panel divs. The contract names <Field> as the mandated input primitive (handles label↔control binding, aria-invalid, hint via aria-describedby, focus ring). App-content inputs must use the kit; this rolls its own, so it misses aria binding, invalid state, and consistent styling with the rest of the app.
  - → Replace both inputs with the kit <Field label="..." hint="..."> primitive, or — if the bpmn-js properties-panel DOM contract must be preserved — at minimum bind label↔input via htmlFor/id and wire the description <p> as aria-describedby. Document the bpmn-js-DOM exception explicitly if the bespoke markup is intentional.
- 🟠 **Labels not programmatically associated with their inputs** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / 4.1.2 (Info and Relationships / Name, Role, Value)_ · `gateway-condition-panel.jsx:183+185, :429+431`)
  - review_code flagged 3 inputs with no associated label. Each <label class="bio-properties-panel-label"> is a visual sibling of its <input> but has no htmlFor, and the input has no id/aria-label/aria-labelledby. Screen readers announce these inputs as unlabeled, and clicking the label does not focus the field.
  - → Give each input a stable id (e.g. `gw-var-${gatewayId}`, `gw-branch-${flowId}`) and set htmlFor on the matching label. Also add aria-describedby pointing at the description/preview <p> so the routing-variable hint and the branch preview are announced.
- 🟡 **Internal flow id (dev-jargon) rendered in the branch label** (content-clarity · _principles.md — no dev-jargon/UUIDs in UI_ · `gateway-condition-panel.jsx:176 (`${flowName} (${flowId})`) and the bare `flowId` fallback`)
  - When a flow has no name, the heading shows the raw BPMN element id (e.g. `Flow_1a2b3c`/`SequenceFlow_0xyz`). When it does have a name, the id is still appended in parentheses. The contract explicitly forbids surfacing element ids/UUIDs/code-names to end users; this is exactly that leak, and it makes branch rows hard to scan for a non-technical no-code user.
  - → Show a human label only: the flow name, or a friendly fallback like «Ветка 1», «Ветка 2» (index-based) or the name of the target node the flow points to. Drop the `(${flowId})` suffix entirely, or move the id behind a <MonoId> tooltip if it must remain available for debugging.
- 🟡 **No feedback after writes; saves are silent** (feedback-state · _feedback — Toast/transient outcome_ · `handleBranchValueChange (:337), handleSetDefault (:371), handleVarNameChange (:303)`)
  - Every edit calls modeling.updateProperties + fireChanged with no user-visible confirmation. Setting a default flow silently clears that flow's condition expression and rewrites the gateway default — a meaningful, non-obvious mutation. Renaming the routing variable silently rewrites every parseable branch condition. The user gets no signal that these cascading changes happened. Errors are also swallowed (catch (_) {} on the elementRegistry/modeling path), so a failed write looks identical to a successful one.
  - → This panel auto-persists into the bpmn-js undo stack, so a per-keystroke toast would be noisy — but the cascading actions deserve signal: when selecting a default flow, surface that its condition was cleared (inline note is partially there, add a transient confirmation); when renaming the variable rewrites N branch conditions, show a brief toast/inline note «Условия N веток обновлены». At minimum, do not silently swallow write failures — surface an ErrorState/toast so a failed save isn't indistinguishable from success.
- 🟡 **Default-branch radio cannot be unset; "exactly one default" not steerable** (ux-pattern · _forms/radio-group_ · `gateway-condition-panel.jsx:227-233 (radio per row), hint at :465`)
  - Each row has a standalone radio sharing name="gateway-default". Once any branch is marked default there is no affordance to return to the "no default" state — radios are not individually deselectable. The hint says exactly one branch must be default, but the UI lets the model exist in a no-default state initially with no clear call-to-action to fix it, and never lets the user clear a default once set. There is also no fieldset/legend grouping the radios, so the relationship between the radio set and the «по умолчанию» constraint is not conveyed to assistive tech.
  - → Wrap the per-row radios in a single <fieldset><legend>Ветка по умолчанию</legend>, or render them as one radio group with an explicit «Нет ветки по умолчанию» option so the state is reversible. If a default is required, validate and surface an inline ErrorState/warning when none is selected rather than relying on prose.
- 🟡 **Strip-warning is not an announced live region** (feedback-state · _WCAG 4.1.3 (Status Messages) / form validation UX_ · `gateway-condition-panel.jsx:196-200`)
  - When the user types a quote/backslash, a warning <p> appears explaining the chars will be stripped, but it is a plain paragraph with no role=alert / aria-live. Screen-reader users get no announcement, and the message is purely informational after-the-fact rather than tied to the field via aria-describedby. The character is also stripped silently on persist while the raw value stays in the box, so the displayed value and stored value diverge without explanation.
  - → Give the warning role="status" (polite) or wire it as the input's aria-describedby so it is announced and associated. Consider preventing the disallowed char on input (or echoing the sanitized value back) so the field shows what is actually stored.
- ⚪ **Empty / no-outgoing state is hand-rolled, not the EmptyState primitive** (consistency · _states — EmptyState (role=status + CTA)_ · `gateway-condition-panel.jsx:398-413`)
  - The no-outgoing-flows branch renders a bare <p> «Нет исходящих потоков…» instead of the contract's <EmptyState> primitive (role=status + CTA). It conveys the right message but isn't announced as a status and doesn't match the explicit-states convention used elsewhere. The instruction «Соедините шлюз с элементами диаграммы» is also not an actionable CTA the user can click.
  - → Render <EmptyState> with the message as the body. If the bpmn-js panel DOM forbids importing the kit component here, at least add role="status" to the wrapper so the empty condition is announced.
- ⚪ **Hardcoded font-weight and hex color fallback violate token-only rule** (consistency · _tokens.css — no hardcoded color/size_ · `gateway-condition-panel.jsx:175 (fontWeight: 600), :197 (color fallback #b45309), :166 (--chs-color-surface-alt fallback)`)
  - fontWeight: 600 is a raw weight instead of var(--chs-weight-semibold). The warning color falls back to a raw hex #b45309 if --chs-color-warning is undefined, and the row background falls back to a possibly-nonexistent --chs-color-surface-alt token. The contract treats hardcoded color/size and consuming raws/undefined tokens as violations; a missing token should fail to a defined semantic token, not a hex literal.
  - → Use var(--chs-weight-semibold) for the label weight and var(--chs-color-warning) with no hex fallback (or fall back to another defined semantic token). Confirm --chs-color-surface-alt exists; if not, use --chs-color-surface-2 or -surface-raised from the contract instead of an unknown token name.

### `src/canvas/timer-deadline-panel.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡5 ⚪1
_Properties-panel group in the BPMN modeler for configuring a timer event's deadline (duration / fixed date / record field) and its escalation target (manager / owner / specific role) on fire._

- 🟠 **Required-deadline error «Укажите срок.» is computed but never shown** (feedback-state · _forms/validation feedback_ · `timer-deadline-panel.jsx:209 (render guard `deadlineErr && deadline.trim() !== ''`)`)
  - validateDeadline returns 'Укажите срок.' for an empty value, but the error <p> only renders when `deadline.trim() !== ''`. So the most common authoring mistake — leaving the deadline blank — produces no visible feedback at all. The author saves a timer with no deadline and only discovers the problem at publish time (the comment says this panel mirrors the publish-time linter precisely to avoid that).
  - → Show the empty-required error inline once the field has been touched/blurred (track a `touched` flag) rather than suppressing it whenever the value is empty. At minimum surface a non-blocking hint that a срок is required, so the author sees the gap before publish.
- 🟠 **Inputs/selects have no programmatic label association (WCAG 1.3.1)** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / 4.1.2 — name, role, value_ · `timer-deadline-panel.jsx:188, 199, 222, 237 (raw <select>/<input> inside PPEntry)`)
  - The visible labels are rendered by PPEntry's `label` prop, but the controls carry no `id`/`htmlFor` binding, `aria-label`, or `aria-labelledby`. Confirmed by both ux-patterns review_code (Missing Input Label) and check_accessibility (1.3.1, critical impact). Screen-reader users hear an unlabeled combobox/textbox; clicking the visible label does not focus the control.
  - → Bind each control to its label: give each control a stable `id` and have PPEntry's label use `htmlFor`, or pass `aria-labelledby` pointing at the label node. Prefer routing these through the kit `<Field>`/`<Select>` primitives which handle label↔control binding and aria-invalid automatically (see consistency finding).
- 🟡 **Deadline error not linked to the input (no aria-invalid / aria-describedby, not announced)** (accessibility · _WCAG 3.3.1 / 4.1.3 — error identification & status messages_ · `timer-deadline-panel.jsx:199-216 (input + error <p>)`)
  - When the deadline format is invalid, the error text is a plain <p> with no `role=alert`/`role=status`, and the <input> has no `aria-invalid` and no `aria-describedby` pointing at the error or the hint. Assistive tech gets no signal that the field is in error or what the message is, and the hint text (deadlineHint) is likewise unassociated.
  - → Set `aria-invalid={!!deadlineErr}` on the input, give the hint and error elements ids and reference them via `aria-describedby`, and make the error container `role="alert"` (or use the kit's `<Field hint error>` which wires this per the contract's aria-describedby convention).
- 🟡 **Hardcoded hex fallback `#c00` on the error color** (consistency · _design-token contract (no hardcoded color)_ · `timer-deadline-panel.jsx:212 `color: 'var(--chs-color-danger, #c00)'``)
  - The design-system contract states hardcoded color is a violation and `--chs-color-danger` is a defined semantic token that is always present. The `#c00` fallback is dead per the contract and, if it ever rendered, would be an off-palette red that fails the both-themes WCAG AA guarantee. Inline `color` styling also bypasses the established danger styling.
  - → Drop the fallback: use `var(--chs-color-danger)` (or the kit error styling). Since the token is guaranteed by the contract, the literal hex is unnecessary and off-contract.
- 🟡 **Raw <select>/<input> instead of kit <Field>/<Select> primitives** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `timer-deadline-panel.jsx:188-192, 199-204, 222-226, 237-241`)
  - All four controls are hand-rolled native elements styled with bpmn-js `bio-properties-panel-*` classes rather than the kit `<Field>`/`<Select>`. The contract explicitly flags 'raw <input>/<select> without <Field>/<Select>' as a consumption violation. This is what produces the label-binding and aria-invalid gaps above, and it diverges visually/behaviorally from the rest of the app's forms. Note: this panel mounts inside the third-party bpmn-js properties chrome, so full kit adoption may be constrained — this is a real but context-bound gap, not a free swap.
  - → Where the bpmn-js chrome allows, replace the native controls with `<Select>` and `<Field>` (they bring label binding, aria-invalid, hint-via-aria-describedby for free). If the host panel forbids it, at minimum add the missing aria wiring manually and document why the kit is bypassed here.
- 🟡 **Loading / empty / error sub-states use bare <p>, not state primitives** (feedback-state · _explicit states (Loading/Empty) — <LoadingState>/<EmptyState>_ · `timer-deadline-panel.jsx:233-234 (Загрузка ролей…), 244-248 (Роли недоступны…)`)
  - Role loading renders a plain 'Загрузка ролей…' <p> with no `role=status`/`aria-busy`, and the no-roles case is a plain <p> with no `role=status`. The contract requires every list/detail to render explicit Loading/Empty/Error components and that Loading=`role=status aria-busy`, Empty=`role=status`. These bare-text states are not announced and are visually inconsistent with the rest of the app.
  - → Use the kit `<LoadingState>` for the roles-loading case and `<EmptyState>` (with the existing helpful CTA copy and a link to Оргструктура) for the no-roles case, or at minimum add `role="status"`/`aria-busy` to the existing nodes.
- 🟡 **Choosing «Конкретная роль…» with no role selected leaves an invalid escalation silently** (flow · _forms/validation — required dependent field_ · `timer-deadline-panel.jsx:167-175 (handleEscalateSelect clears the slug), 231-249 (role picker)`)
  - Selecting 'Конкретная роль…' commits an empty escalateTo (clears the slug), and the role <select> defaults to the '— выберите роль —' empty option. Unlike the deadline field, there is no inline validation telling the author they must pick a role; the empty escalation persists silently and surfaces only at publish. This is the same 'silent invalid config' risk the deadline validator was built to prevent, but escalation has no equivalent check.
  - → Add inline validation for the role picker mirroring validateDeadline: when target is 'role' and no slug is chosen, show a 'Выберите роль' message (associated via aria-describedby/aria-invalid). Consider disabling/marking the entry as incomplete so the gap is visible before publish.
- ⚪ **Format placeholders/examples (PT24H, ISO timestamps, dueDate) lean technical for a no-code audience** (content-clarity · _microcopy clarity_ · `timer-deadline-panel.jsx:51-71 (deadlinePlaceholder/deadlineHint)`)
  - Per the principles, the product targets a no-code/process audience and avoids dev-jargon. Asking authors to type ISO-8601 like `PT24H` / `2026-07-01T14:00:00Z` is inherently technical. The hints do a good job explaining the syntax (mitigating the issue), but a free-text ISO field is a higher-friction, error-prone affordance than the domain warrants.
  - → Consider a structured duration input (number + unit select: часы/дни/минуты) and a date/time picker for the 'date' kind, falling back to raw ISO only for advanced/`${...}` expression cases. Keeps the no-code promise and removes a whole class of format errors. Low priority since hints currently cover it.

### `src/screens/rights/ra-criticality.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡3 ⚪2
_A dual-control approval queue where a second employee approves or rejects pending changes to access rights (grants and role assignments)._

- 🟠 **Destructive reject (and approve) fires immediately with no confirmation** (ux-pattern · _destructive-action confirmation_ · `ChangeRequestRow actions, ra-criticality.jsx:144-152 (Отклонить) and :135-143 (Одобрить)`)
  - Both 'Одобрить' and 'Отклонить' commit an irreversible dual-control security decision on a single click. Rejecting another employee's rights request is destructive and consequential, yet there is no confirmation step. A mis-click approves or denies an access-rights change with no undo. The design-system contract provides <ConfirmDialog> precisely to replace bare-click destructive flows.
  - → Gate at least 'Отклонить' (and ideally critical 'Одобрить') behind <ConfirmDialog>, summarizing what is being approved/rejected and who initiated it. The dialog also gives a natural place to require focus confirmation before the POST fires.
- 🟠 **Reject never captures a reason despite the API supporting one** (flow · _form validation / audit justification_ · `handleReject -> decideChangeRequest(id, 'reject', null), ra-criticality.jsx:197-208; reason param defined at :73`)
  - decideChangeRequest(id, action, reason) accepts a reason and forwards it to the dual-control API, but handleReject always passes null. For an access-rights denial in a dual-control flow, the absence of a justification is both a UX gap (the initiator gets no explanation) and an audit gap. The reason field is wired end-to-end but unreachable from the UI.
  - → When rejecting, prompt for a reason (a <Field>/textarea inside the ConfirmDialog) and pass it through to decideChangeRequest. Make it required, or at minimum offered, so denials carry an explanation back to the initiator and the audit log.
- 🟡 **One in-flight action disables every button in the entire list with no explanation** (feedback-state · _loading / disabled affordance_ · `ChangeRequestRow buttons disabled={busy !== null}, ra-criticality.jsx:138 and :147; busy state at :166`)
  - busy is a single global string, so approving/rejecting one row sets disabled on the Одобрить/Отклонить buttons of ALL rows. Only the acting button shows a spinner (loading); every other row's buttons go silently disabled with no visible reason, reading as a frozen/broken UI on a list of many pending requests. The contract's affordance rule says a disabled element must be explained, not a silent dead state.
  - → Either scope disabling to the acting row only (allow parallel decisions on independent requests), or if serialization is intentional, surface why other rows are disabled (e.g. a row-level 'ожидание…' hint or an aria-disabled with descriptive text). Avoid globally freezing an otherwise-actionable queue.
- 🟡 **Success toast for the final approval/rejection is lost — ToastViewport unmounts on empty** (feedback-state · _toast feedback_ · `ToastViewport rendered only in the list branch, ra-criticality.jsx:276; empty branch :237-249 has none`)
  - <ToastViewport> is mounted only in the list-render branch. When the user approves/rejects the LAST pending request, load() re-renders into the EmptyState branch, which has no ToastViewport, so the 'Изменение одобрено./отклонено.' toast pushed in the handler never appears. The most consequential confirmation — clearing the queue — gives no feedback. (The contract notes each tree must wire its own ToastViewport; here it is wired in only one of four render branches.)
  - → Hoist <ToastViewport toasts={toasts} /> to the always-rendered root of CriticalityScreen (outside the loading/error/empty/list branching), so action feedback is visible regardless of which state the screen settles into after reload.
- 🟡 **Bespoke chip variants (chs-chip--grant / --assignment) outside the documented chip set** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `KindChip, ra-criticality.jsx:100-107`)
  - KindChip hand-rolls a <span> with chs-chip--grant / chs-chip--assignment classes. The contract enumerates only StatusChip/ExecutorBadge/OpChip/DerivedChip and explicitly notes there is no generic <Badge>/chip primitive. These ad-hoc variant classes risk diverging from token-backed colors and from the rest of the chip family (shape, radius chip=2, type scale), and are invisible to the kit's consistency guarantees.
  - → Confirm chs-chip--grant/--assignment are defined purely from --chs-* tokens (no hardcoded color). Better: promote a small typed chip into the kit (or reuse OpChip/neutral StatusChip styling) so 'Грант'/'Назначение' render consistently with the rest of the system rather than as a one-off in this screen.
- ⚪ **Hand-rolled Russian pluralization is incorrect for the teens/hundreds** (content-clarity · _microcopy / i18n pluralization_ · `count label, ra-criticality.jsx:258-262`)
  - The ternary `length === 1 ? 'запрос' : length < 5 ? 'запроса' : 'запросов'` mishandles Russian plural rules. 11–14 should be 'запросов' but render as '11 запроса'…'14 запроса'; 21 should be '21 запрос' but renders '21 запроса'; 102 renders '102 запроса' (correct) yet 111 renders '111 запроса' (wrong). The rule must key off n%100 and n%10, not raw <5.
  - → Use a proper plural helper (e.g. Intl.PluralRules('ru') with few/many/one forms, or a small util) instead of the inline ternary, so the count label is grammatically correct across all ranges.
- ⚪ **Buttons lack explicit type attribute** (ux-pattern · _forms/submit-button_ · `Button usages, ra-criticality.jsx:135 and :144 (review_code lines 24-25)`)
  - review_code flags the action buttons as missing an explicit type. This is handled if the kit <Button> defaults to type="button", but cannot be confirmed from this file. If <Button> renders a native <button> without a type, it defaults to type="submit", which can trigger unintended form submission if ever nested in a <form>.
  - → Verify <Button> in components.jsx sets type="button" by default; if not, pass type="button" on these non-submit action buttons. Low priority but cheap to confirm.

### `src/screens/rights/ra-data.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡3 ⚪2
_Shared data module for the «Права и доступ» section: org-tree/scope/resource/preset constants plus the section's reusable presentational primitives (criticality badge, axis list, scope token, provenance tag, section head, segmented switch)._

- 🟠 **Axis meaning carried only by single-letter glyph + title tooltip — not keyboard/touch/AT accessible** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.4.13 Content on Hover/Focus; 1.1.1 Non-text Content_ · `ra-data.jsx:296-300 CriticalityBadge axes`)
  - Each criticality axis renders as a single uppercase letter (G/E/S from ax.short[0]) whose only explanation is a native title tooltip (title={ax.desc}). title tooltips are unreachable by keyboard and touch and are inconsistently announced by screen readers, so the three axes are effectively unlabeled. The design-system contract requires meaning-bearing glyphs to duplicate with text, not hide it in a tooltip.
  - → Give each axis an accessible name independent of title: add aria-label (e.g. aria-label={`${ax.label}: ${axes[ax.id] ? 'активна' : 'не активна'}`}) on the axis span, or render visually-hidden text. Keep the visible letter as a compact cue but ensure the full label/state is exposed to AT and on focus, not hover-only.
- 🟠 **AxisList toggle buttons do not expose on/off state to assistive tech** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value_ · `ra-data.jsx:313-327 AxisList button`)
  - When onToggle is provided the rows are real toggle buttons, but the on/off state is conveyed only through CSS classes (chs-axisrow--on / --heavy) and a visible flag word. There is no aria-pressed, so a screen-reader user cannot tell whether an axis is active, and the 'повышает критичность' vs 'активна' vs '—' distinction is purely visual text inside the button with no programmatic state.
  - → Add aria-pressed={!!on} to the button when it is interactive (onToggle present). Consider including the state in the accessible name. The disabled (read-only) variant is fine, but the interactive variant must announce its pressed state.
- 🟡 **Segmented control lacks group label and proper selected-option semantics** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / 4.1.2; ux-pattern segmented-control / tabs_ · `ra-data.jsx:363-373 Segmented`)
  - role="group" is applied with no accessible name (no aria-label/aria-labelledby), so AT announces an unlabeled group. Options use aria-pressed toggle-button semantics which works, but with no group name a user navigating button-by-button has no context for what the choice controls (mode switch). The visible o.hint is appended inside the button with no separation, so it becomes part of the accessible name unparsed.
  - → Require/forward an aria-label (or labelledby) prop on the wrapping div, e.g. <div role="group" aria-label={label}>. If this is a single-select mode switch, prefer radiogroup semantics (role=radiogroup + role=radio + aria-checked) or the kit's bespoke RightsSubTabs/tab pattern for consistency. Ensure the hint is either in aria-label text or marked aria-hidden if purely decorative.
- 🟡 **Bespoke section primitives duplicate kit gaps instead of reusing design-system components** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `ra-data.jsx:352-373 (SectionHead, Segmented); 333-349 (ScopeToken, ProvenanceTag)`)
  - This module hand-rolls a section header (SectionHead), a segmented/tab switch (Segmented), and several chip-like tokens (ScopeToken, ProvenanceTag, CriticalityBadge). The contract notes the kit has no <Tabs>/<Card>/generic <Badge> primitive, so each section inventing its own raises the inconsistency risk the contract explicitly flags. Segmented in particular overlaps with the shell's bespoke RightsSubTabs, meaning two different tab idioms can coexist in the same section.
  - → Either promote these into the shared kit (a real <Tabs>/<Segmented>, <Badge>, <Card/SectionHead>) so the whole Rights section consumes one implementation, or explicitly align Segmented with the existing RightsSubTabs idiom. At minimum, document that these are the canonical section primitives so sibling screens import them rather than re-rolling.
- 🟡 **Dev-jargon leaks in user-facing data: raw op codes, mcp:// URIs, grant IDs, ms-precision timestamps** (content-clarity · _principles.md — no dev-jargon in UI_ · `ra-data.jsx:15 OP_LABEL; 50-61 RESOURCES uri; 273-284 TRAIL (id grt-…, res mcp://…, ts with .NNN ms)`)
  - OP_LABEL exposes English machine codes (read/write/invoke/approve) while OP_RU exists alongside — risk that the English codes reach the UI. TRAIL rows carry grant IDs (grt-9f4a2c), raw mcp:// resource URIs, and full ms-precision timestamps (2026-06-08 14:21:06.318). The contract bans ticket IDs/UUIDs/code-names and raw timestamps in the UI; resource URIs and grt- ids are exactly that kind of leak if rendered verbatim.
  - → Ensure consuming screens render OP_RU (human labels), map mcp:// URIs to RES_BY_URI[...].name, and format timestamps without milliseconds via a shared formatter (and mask/shorten grant IDs behind a <MonoId> with a copy affordance rather than showing the raw token as primary content). If these fields are truly internal, keep them out of rendered output.
- ⚪ **TRAIL mock references resources/ops absent from the canonical catalog** (consistency · _data integrity / single source of truth_ · `ra-data.jsx:276,281,282 (mcp://ocr.extract, mcp://bus.publish), op:"write" vs OP_LABEL`)
  - TRAIL entries reference URIs (mcp://ocr.extract, mcp://bus.publish) that are not in RESOURCES, so RES_BY_URI lookups for them return undefined; rows also use op:"write" while the canonical op vocabulary is read/write/invoke/approve plus the presets' create/update — the op set is inconsistent between TRAIL, OP_LABEL, and PRESETS. Any screen that joins TRAIL rows to RESOURCES for a friendly name will fall back to the raw URI for these rows.
  - → Make the mock self-consistent: either add the referenced resources to RESOURCES or change TRAIL to use catalog URIs, and reconcile the op vocabulary across OP_LABEL/OP_RU, PRESETS (create/update), and TRAIL so consumers can always resolve a human label.
- ⚪ **ProvenanceTag / ScopeToken decorative glyphs are empty spans with no fallback if adjacent text is hidden** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.1.1 Non-text Content_ · `ra-data.jsx:334-343 ScopeToken; 346-349 ProvenanceTag`)
  - The leading glyph spans (chs-scopetok__glyph, chs-prov__glyph) are empty and styled via CSS only. This is acceptable because adjacent visible Russian text carries the meaning, but they should be explicitly marked decorative so AT never tries to interpret a CSS-rendered glyph, and the '#' / '≤' operator glyphs are raw text that will be read aloud out of context.
  - → Add aria-hidden="true" to the purely decorative glyph spans, and ensure the '≤'/'#' operator characters are paired with text the screen reader can use (e.g. wrap range tokens with an accessible label like 'не более' for ≤). Low priority since the visible text remains the primary carrier.

### `src/screens/rights/ra-grant-trail.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡7 ⚪3
_An append-only audit log table showing the history of access-rights grants/revocations/narrowings, with action-type filter tabs, a "critical only" toggle, export, and pagination._

- 🟠 **Export button is a silent no-op** (feedback-state · _Affordance rule (enabled element must do something)_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:139 <Button variant="secondary" size="sm">Экспорт</Button>`)
  - The Экспорт button has no onClick handler. It renders fully enabled, so a user clicks it and nothing happens — a direct violation of the contract's affordance rule ('an enabled element MUST do something — else disabled/aria-disabled or «скоро», never a silent no-op'). For an audit/compliance screen, export is a primary expected action, so silent failure is especially damaging.
  - → Wire an onClick that triggers the export (e.g. GET /api/grant-trail/export honoring the active filter+critOnly), with a Toast on success/failure. If export is not yet built, mark the button disabled with a «скоро» tooltip or label rather than leaving it live.
- 🟠 **No loading or error state on initial fetch** (feedback-state · _States always explicit (Empty/Loading/Error)_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:85-99 (useEffect fetch), render block 143-183`)
  - The initial fetch sets `loading` but the render never consumes it for the table — there is no <LoadingState>/<Skeleton> on first load, and the .catch silently swallows errors (keeps seed data) with no <ErrorState onRetry>. The contract mandates every list render an explicit Loading and Error component. Worse, on API failure the user silently sees stale seed/demo rows presented as a real audit log, with no indication the data is not live — dangerous for a compliance surface.
  - → Render <LoadingState> (or <Skeleton> rows) while the first fetch is in flight, and on .catch render <ErrorState onRetry={refetch}> instead of silently falling back to seed. If a static fallback is intentionally kept, show a non-blocking warning banner ('показаны демо-данные, журнал недоступен') so the staleness is honest.
- 🟡 **Filter tabs are not a real ARIA tablist** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value / ARIA tabs pattern_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:127-133 (.chs-tabs / .chs-tab buttons)`)
  - Buttons carry aria-selected but live in a plain <div> with no role="tablist" / role="tab" and no roltabpanel relationship. aria-selected is only valid on elements with an appropriate role (tab/option/row/gridcell); on a bare button it is ignored by AT, so screen-reader users get no 'selected tab' announcement and no tablist arrow-key navigation. Also the bespoke .chs-tabs duplicates the shell's RightsSubTabs bespoke pattern — there is no shared <Tabs> primitive, inviting inconsistency.
  - → Either give the container role="tablist" and each button role="tab" with arrow-key handling, or — since these are content filters, not view tabs — model them as a radiogroup / toggle-button group and use aria-pressed instead of aria-selected. Best: extract a shared Tabs/SegmentedControl primitive so this and shell's RightsSubTabs converge.
- 🟡 **Count chips can mislead while filtered to «только критичные»** (content-clarity · _Filter feedback / scannability_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:118-121 (counts) vs 117 (rows), tab counts 130`)
  - Tab counts are computed from allRows ignoring the critOnly toggle, while the visible table is filtered by both filter AND critOnly. With «только критичные» on, a tab may read e.g. 'Выдачи 42' but the table shows 3 rows, with nothing explaining the gap. The user cannot reconcile the badge with what they see.
  - → Compute tab counts against the same critOnly predicate as the visible rows (so the badge reflects what clicking the tab would actually show), or visually indicate the crit filter is narrowing results (e.g. '3 из 42'). Keep the count and the rendered set consistent.
- 🟡 **Raw UTC ISO timestamp leaks machine formatting** (content-clarity · _No dev-jargon / 1970-01-01 in UI_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:37-39 (ts derivation) rendered at :159`)
  - occurred_at is formatted via new Date(...).toISOString().replace('T',' ').replace('Z','') yielding '2026-06-29 14:03:22.581' — millisecond precision, no localization, and the column header claims 'UTC+3' while toISOString() emits UTC (UTC+0), so the displayed time is off by the labeled offset and mislabeled. When occurred_at is null/undefined the fallback String(r.occurred_at) prints literally 'null'/'undefined'. The contract flags dev-jargon timestamps (e.g. 1970-01-01) as violations.
  - → Format with a locale-aware formatter in the intended timezone (Intl.DateTimeFormat 'ru-RU' with Europe/Moscow if the header says UTC+3), drop milliseconds, and render a graceful placeholder (e.g. '—') when the timestamp is missing instead of stringifying null/undefined.
- 🟡 **Empty state is a bare styled div, not the kit primitive** (consistency · _States always explicit / <EmptyState role=status + CTA>_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:182 (.chs-trail__empty 'Записей по фильтру нет.')`)
  - The no-results case renders a plain <div> string instead of the kit <EmptyState>. It lacks role="status" (so AT users get no announcement when filtering empties the list) and offers no recovery affordance (e.g. 'Сбросить фильтры'). This diverges from the contract's required state primitives and from other screens.
  - → Replace with <EmptyState> carrying role="status" and a CTA to reset the active filter / critOnly toggle, so an accidentally-empty filter is recoverable and announced.
- 🟡 **JSON dumped into the Scope column for non-node scopes** (content-clarity · _No dev-jargon leaks / scannability_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:46-53 (scopeDisplay), rendered :171`)
  - For any scope kind not in {node, tags, interval} the fallback is JSON.stringify(s), printing raw '{"kind":"...",...}' into a business-facing audit column. Node scope also renders 'hierarchy:nodeId' which may surface internal node IDs (dev-jargon per contract). Audit reviewers need human-readable scope, not serialized objects.
  - → Map every known scope kind to a human label and provide a safe human-readable default for unknown kinds (e.g. 'особый охват' with a Tooltip/Popover holding the detail) instead of JSON.stringify. Resolve node IDs to node names where possible.
- 🟡 **Confirmation glyphs use icon-only success marks with no text equivalent** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.1.1 Non-text Content / executor-glyph dup-with-text rule_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:174-177 (.chs-trailconfirm KitIcon name="success")`)
  - The 'Происхождение' cell renders one or two <KitIcon name="success"> marks to indicate single vs dual confirmation, with no accessible name. A screen-reader user hears only the confirmer name string with no notion that it represents a confirmation, and the one-vs-two-checkmarks distinction (single vs dual sign-off — a meaningful compliance signal) is purely visual. The contract requires meaning to be carried by text, not glyph alone, and forbids ✓ as a decorative icon without semantics.
  - → Give the confirmation an accessible label (e.g. aria-label 'Подтверждено: N' or visible text 'подтвердил(и): …') and convey single-vs-dual sign-off in text, not just by checkmark count. Ensure each KitIcon is aria-hidden when its meaning is duplicated in adjacent text.
- 🟡 **Data table built ad hoc from div/span with no semantic table roles** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 Info and Relationships_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:144-183 (.chs-trailtable grid of divs/spans)`)
  - The audit log is a real tabular dataset (8 columns, header row of <span>s) rendered as CSS-grid divs/spans with no table semantics. Screen-reader users get no column-header association — a cell value like a scope or confirmer is announced with no 'Охват'/'Происхождение' context, and there is no row/column navigation. The contract notes there is no shared <Table> primitive; this assembles one ad hoc without roles.
  - → Add ARIA grid/table semantics (role="table"/"row"/"columnheader"/"cell", or use a real <table>) so header-to-cell relationships are exposed. Longer-term, extract a shared <DataTable> primitive so the rights screens stop hand-rolling tables.
- ⚪ **«только критичные» toggle lacks pressed-state semantics** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2 / toggle button aria-pressed_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:135-137 (.chs-trail__crit button)`)
  - The critical-only filter is a toggle button whose on/off state is conveyed only by a CSS class (chs-trail__crit--on) and a colored dot. There is no aria-pressed, so AT users cannot tell whether the filter is active, and the colored dot alone is a color-only state cue.
  - → Add aria-pressed={critOnly} to the button (and ensure the active state has a non-color cue such as a label change or checkmark). Consider using the kit <Button> with a toggle treatment for consistency.
- ⚪ **loadMore failures are silently swallowed** (feedback-state · _Error feedback after action_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:113 (.catch(() => {}) in loadMore)`)
  - When 'Загрузить ещё' fails, the catch is empty: the spinner stops and nothing changes, so the user cannot tell whether they reached the end of the log or the request failed. On an audit trail, an undetected truncation is a correctness/trust issue.
  - → On loadMore error, surface a Toast (error/assertive) and keep hasMore true / re-enable retry so the user knows pagination failed rather than ended.
- ⚪ **Unused imports signal dead/incomplete wiring** (consistency · _Code hygiene_ · `ra-grant-trail.jsx:8 (SectionHead imported, never used)`)
  - SectionHead is imported from ra-data.jsx but never used in the file. Minor, but unused imports are noise and can hint at half-finished layout intent.
  - → Remove the unused SectionHead import (and the unused React default import if the JSX runtime is automatic).

### `src/screens/rights/ra-intents.jsx` — 🔴1 🟠2 🟡5 ⚪1
_Admin screen for everyday rights operations (hire / fire / substitute / urgent-revoke) expressed as intent operations that the server expands into grants via presets._

- 🔴 **Destructive intents (Уволить / Срочно отозвать) fire immediately with no confirmation** (ux-pattern · _destructive-action confirmation_ · `FireForm submit / UrgentRevokeForm submit (ra-intents.jsx:184-188, 269-273)`)
  - «Отключить сотрудника» atomically revokes ALL of an employee's assignments and grants in one transaction, and «Отозвать сейчас» immediately kills a grant (and halts an active agent run, fail-closed). Both are irreversible and high-blast-radius, yet the primary Button calls submit() directly with no confirmation step. A single mis-click — especially after picking the wrong person from the OrgPicker — permanently strips access. The contract mandates Modal for confirm and ships <ConfirmDialog> as a window.confirm replacement, which is unused here.
  - → Wrap the fire and urgent-revoke submits in <ConfirmDialog> with an explicit, named summary of consequences (e.g. «Снять все права у П. Петров? Действие необратимо.»). For the critical/dual-control preset path, surface that requirement in the confirm copy too.
- 🟠 **On-error the form is a silent dead end — no retry affordance, error not announced** (feedback-state · _error-handling / ErrorState_ · `ResultBanner err branch (ra-intents.jsx:114-118); useOrgDirectory error (35-52)`)
  - When an intent POST fails, ResultBanner renders a plain colored <div> with the raw reason string and no role=alert, so screen readers do not announce it and there is no Повторить affordance — the contract requires Error=role=alert + Повторить via <ErrorState onRetry>. The reason can also be a raw server code (e.g. SUBSTITUTION_WIDENS / HTTP 422) which is dev-jargon, not human microcopy. The directory-load error is only shown inline inside OrgPicker hints, never as a screen-level <ErrorState>.
  - → Render failures through the kit feedback path: a <Toast variant=error> (role=alert/assertive) and/or an inline <ErrorState onRetry={submit}>. Map known server reasons (SUBSTITUTION_WIDENS, ENGINE_UNAVAILABLE, HTTP 422) to plain-Russian messages; keep the raw code only as secondary detail.
- 🟠 **Success feedback dumps raw JSON to the user** (content-clarity · _feedback after action_ · `ResultBanner ok branch (ra-intents.jsx:109-110)`)
  - On success the banner shows a human message AND a <pre>{JSON.stringify(result.data)}</pre> of the entire server payload. Raw JSON (UUIDs, internal field names, tier codes) is dev-jargon leak that violates principles.md «no dev-jargon / UUIDs in UI» and is unreadable to an admin. The persistent inline banner is also the wrong surface for a transient outcome — the contract prescribes Toast for transient outcomes.
  - → Remove the <pre> JSON dump entirely; show only the composed human message. Move the outcome to a <Toast> (status/polite) via useToasts(); if any structured detail is genuinely useful (e.g. grants issued count) present it as labeled text, never raw JSON.
- 🟡 **Native <select> used instead of a kit primitive; OrgPicker re-rolls <Field> markup by hand** (consistency · _design-system primitive consumption_ · `OrgPicker (75-84), HireForm Тип/Пресет selects (145-163), Substitute/UrgentRevoke selects (279-285)`)
  - Every dropdown is a hand-built <label class=chs-field><span class=chs-label><select class=chs-input>. The contract notes there is no <Select> kit primitive, so raw <select> is the current reality — but the screen also hand-rolls the label/binding wrapper that <Field> provides, duplicating its aria wiring inconsistently (the inline HireForm/UrgentRevoke selects have NO htmlFor/id binding at all, unlike OrgPicker which at least uses useId). This is the rolled-your-own flag from the contract.
  - → Centralize select rendering in one helper (or push for a kit <Select> primitive) so every dropdown gets the same label↔control id binding, aria-invalid, and hint plumbing. At minimum give the inline Тип/Пресет/Тип-принципала selects an id + htmlFor like OrgPicker already does.
- 🟡 **Hardcoded color fallbacks and inline styles bypass the token system** (consistency · _design tokens_ · `ResultBanner (108, 115), loadErr note (328)`)
  - Inline styles use literal color fallbacks `var(--chs-color-success, green)` and `var(--chs-color-danger, red)`. The literal `green`/`red` fallbacks are hardcoded colors — a token violation per the contract (hardcoded color = violation), and `green`/`red` are not WCAG-tuned and ignore theme. Layout/spacing is also applied via inline style attributes rather than the class-driven token system used elsewhere.
  - → Drop the literal fallbacks (rely on the defined --chs-color-success/-danger tokens, which always exist) and move the marginTop/fontSize inline styles into the chs-intent__result CSS class so styling stays token-driven and themeable.
- 🟡 **No loading or empty state while presets / org directory load** (feedback-state · _LoadingState / EmptyState_ · `IntentsScreen presets fetch (306-313); useOrgDirectory loading flag is computed but never rendered (37, 304)`)
  - The contract requires every list/detail to render an explicit Loading/Empty/Error state. While presets are loading, the Пресет-роль select silently shows only «— выбрать пресет —» with no options and no spinner, and the Роль/Сотрудник pickers render an empty options list with no indication they are still loading vs genuinely empty — useOrgDirectory even returns a `loading` boolean that the UI ignores. An admin cannot tell 'still loading' from 'no employees exist' from 'failed'.
  - → Consume dir.loading and a presets-loading flag: show <LoadingState>/<Skeleton> in the selects while fetching, and an <EmptyState> with guidance (e.g. «Нет сотрудников — сначала заведите оргструктуру») when a directory list is genuinely empty.
- 🟡 **Disabled submit buttons give no reason; required fields are not marked** (feedback-state · _form validation UX_ · `All four submit Buttons (171, 202, 255, 292)`)
  - Submit stays disabled until a cluster of fields is filled (e.g. Substitute needs 5 fields) but nothing tells the admin WHICH field is missing or that the field was required — a silent disabled control. The Affordance rule allows disabled, but with no required markers (no aria-required, no «обязательно» hint) and no validation messaging, the user is left guessing why «Объявить подмену» won't activate.
  - → Mark required fields (visually + aria-required) and either keep the button enabled and validate on submit with inline field errors, or add a short hint near the disabled button listing what's still needed. The kit <Field> supports hint/aria-describedby for this.
- 🟡 **Urgent-revoke still asks the admin to type a raw grant UUID** (content-clarity · _no dev-jargon / input affordance_ · `UrgentRevokeForm (278)`)
  - The «UUID гранта» field with placeholder `g0000000-…` requires the admin to hand-type an internal UUID — exactly the dev-jargon/error-trap pattern that the OrgPicker work (audit #10, lines 25-33) eliminated for the other forms. principles.md forbids UUIDs in the UI. Typing a 36-char id by hand for the single most destructive action (immediate revoke + agent halt) is high-risk.
  - → Replace the free-text UUID field with a picker over the principal's active grants (label by human capability/role + scope, submit the underlying id), consistent with OrgPicker. If a grant list endpoint isn't ready, keep the raw field only as the honest-degrade fallback, not the happy path.
- ⚪ **Inconsistent / unlocalized success and error microcopy** (content-clarity · _microcopy_ · `HireForm message (135), SubstituteForm message (230), error reason (98/116)`)
  - Success strings expose internal vocabulary — «статус ${r.data.state}» renders a raw server enum, and «(Tier-${r.data.tier})» / «выдан TTL-грант» mix English technical terms into Russian admin copy. Error reasons fall back to bare codes like `HTTP 500`. This is inconsistent register and leaks implementation terms to a non-technical admin.
  - → Map server enums (state, tier) and error codes to plain Russian phrases in one place; reserve technical terms (TTL, Tier) for tooltips/secondary text rather than the primary outcome line.

### `src/screens/rights/ra-role-editor.jsx` — 🟠4 🟡6 ⚪2
_Role-permission editor: lets an org owner edit a role's grants (resource · operation · scope) via advanced/simple modes or an LLM proposal, submit them for application, and complete dual-control second-confirmation for critical grants._

- 🟠 **Destructive actions (delete grant, reject proposal) have no confirmation** (ux-pattern · _confirmation / undo for destructive actions_ · `GrantEditRow delete button (line 147); proposed reject button (line 702)`)
  - Clicking the close (×) icon on a grant row immediately removes it from state with no confirm and no undo, and 'Отклонить' permanently marks an LLM proposal rejected. The design-system contract requires <ConfirmDialog> for destructive operations and warns against silent destructive ops. On a security-sensitive permissions screen, accidentally deleting a grant is costly and unrecoverable within the session.
  - → Wrap grant deletion in <ConfirmDialog> (or provide an undo toast via useToasts) summarising what is being removed (resource + ops). For proposal reject, either make it reversible (allow re-confirm) or confirm; at minimum show a toast so the action is acknowledged.
- 🟠 **Raw <input>/<textarea> used instead of <Field> primitive; range input has no accessible label** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / 4.1.2 (Name, Role, Value); forms/text-field_ · `Numeric range input (lines 89-97); LLM textarea (line 667)`)
  - The numeric range input is a bare <input> whose only label is an adjacent decorative '≤ ₽' span and a separate non-associated heading div — there is no <label htmlFor>/aria-label, so screen readers announce an unnamed edit field. The LLM textarea likewise has only a placeholder (placeholders are not labels). The contract mandates <Field> for inputs (label↔control binding, aria-invalid, hint via aria-describedby) and flags raw <input> as a violation. review_code flagged both as 'Missing Input Label' errors.
  - → Replace both with <Field> (textarea variant or an explicit aria-label until a textarea wrapper exists). Give the range input an aria-label like 'Потолок суммы, ₽' and the textarea a real label 'Описание роли'. Do not rely on placeholders for naming.
- 🟠 **Second-confirmer (dual-control) input is a raw free-text login field with no validation** (ux-pattern · _forms/text-field; error prevention on critical action_ · `Dual-control panel input, lines 568-574; confirmActor state default 'e-owner2'`)
  - The security-critical second authorizer is entered as a free-text login into a raw <input> prefilled with a dev slug ('e-owner2'). There is no label-control binding, no list of eligible admins, and no validation/feedback until the server round-trips. A typo silently fails, and the prefilled dev slug is jargon that leaks into a high-stakes flow. This both violates the <Field> contract and is poor error-prevention for an irreversible privilege-escalation confirmation.
  - → Replace with a <Select> of eligible second administrators (excluding the first confirmer) backed by the org roster, or a <Field> with inline validation and a real label. Remove the dev-slug default; show an empty 'Выберите второго администратора' prompt instead.
- 🟠 **Loading / empty / error states are hand-rolled divs with inline styles instead of state primitives; error has no retry** (feedback-state · _states always explicit — <LoadingState>/<EmptyState>/<ErrorState onRetry>_ · `RoleEditRail rail states (lines 735-749); header role states (lines 519-526)`)
  - The role-rail and header render 'Загрузка ролей…', 'Ошибка загрузки ролей: {error}', and 'Ролей нет…' as ad-hoc <div>s with hardcoded inline color styles, instead of the contract's <LoadingState> (role=status aria-busy), <ErrorState onRetry> (role=alert + Повторить), and <EmptyState> (role=status + CTA). The error state offers no retry, so a transient fetch failure leaves the user stuck reloading the page. The raw '{error}' (e.g. 'HTTP 500') is dev-jargon leaked to the UI.
  - → Use <LoadingState>, <ErrorState onRetry={refetch}>, and <EmptyState cta=…> for both the rail and the header. Add a retry path in useRoleList. Replace the raw 'HTTP {status}' message with a human sentence ('Не удалось загрузить роли. Повторите.').
- 🟡 **'Отмена' silently discards results / does nothing visible** (feedback-state · _affordance rule — enabled element must do something_ · `Cancel button, line 529 (onClick={() => setSubmitResult(null)})`)
  - The 'Отмена' button only clears submitResult. It does not revert grant edits, close the editor, or navigate away, so to the user it appears to do nothing (or mysteriously clears the success/error banner). This violates the affordance rule and confuses the mental model of what 'Cancel' reverts.
  - → Make Cancel revert the in-progress grant edits to the last loaded state (or navigate away / discard), and confirm if there are unsaved changes. If it is only meant to dismiss the result banner, relabel it (e.g. an × on the banner) rather than a top-level 'Отмена' action.
- 🟡 **Save/confirm feedback shown as inline text, not the Toast/transient-outcome convention** (feedback-state · _surface choice — Toast for transient outcome; feedback after action_ · `Submit result block (lines 540-553); confirm result (lines 584-588)`)
  - After 'Запросить применение' the outcome appears as an inline coloured span/divs under the header ('N грант(ов) применено' / per-error rows) rather than a <Toast> (status/polite for success, alert/assertive for error). It can be scrolled out of view and is not announced assertively for errors. The contract designates Toast for transient action outcomes and notes errors must be role=alert/assertive.
  - → Emit outcomes through useToasts()/<ToastViewport>: a success toast (polite) and an error toast (assertive) per failure, or a single summary alert region with role=alert for the error case. Keep a persistent inline summary only if errors are actionable in place.
- 🟡 **Non-functional 'Поиск роли' search affordance (silent no-op)** (ux-pattern · _affordance rule — enabled element must do something_ · `RoleEditRail search, line 733`)
  - 'Поиск роли' renders as a styled <div> with a search icon that looks like a search box but is not an input and has no handler — it does nothing when clicked or typed into. The contract's affordance rule forbids silent no-ops; either it must work or be marked «скоро»/disabled.
  - → Make it a real <Field> search input that filters the role list, or remove it / mark it «скоро» if filtering is not yet implemented.
- 🟡 **Dev-jargon and fake hardcoded data leak into the UI** (content-clarity · _no dev-jargon in UI (no UUIDs/code-names/code snippets)_ · `Role UUID rendered (line 514); code snippet hint (line 669); 'confirmed_by М. Соколов' (line 705); footer 'scope-set из N узлов' (line 105)`)
  - The header prints the raw role UUID (selectedRole.id) as visible text; the LLM hint shows a literal code expression 'proposed_by llm → confirmed_by human'; a confirmed proposal shows a hardcoded fake reviewer name 'М. Соколов' regardless of the actual actor; the scope footer exposes engineer phrasing 'scope-set из N узлов'. The principles explicitly ban UUIDs, code-names, and dev-jargon in UI, and a hardcoded fake name is misleading provenance on a security screen.
  - → Hide the UUID (or show a copy-on-click MonoId affordance only, not as title metadata); rewrite the hint in plain Russian ('Предложения ИИ требуют подтверждения человеком'); replace 'М. Соколов' with the real authenticated actor's display name; reword 'scope-set из N узлов' to 'охват: N узлов'.
- 🟡 **App-content interactive controls rolled their own <button> instead of kit <Button>** (consistency · _design-system consumption — use <Button> for app-content buttons_ · `Op toggles (line 140), scope toggle (143), scope nodes/tags (60,81), presets (620), '+ грант' (647), proposal reject/confirm (702-703)`)
  - Many interactive app-content controls are bare <button className=chs-…> rather than the kit <Button> (which handles focus-ring, aria-disabled/aria-busy, variants). The contract flags 'rolled-its-own <button> instead of <Button>' for app content as a violation (only shell nav buttons are exempt). This produces inconsistent focus rings, hover, and disabled semantics versus the primary/secondary <Button>s used elsewhere on the same screen.
  - → Where they are simple actions ('+ грант', 'Отклонить', 'Подтвердить грант'), use <Button variant=ghost/secondary/primary size=sm>. For the multi-select toggle controls (ops, scope nodes/tags, presets) which carry selected state, use aria-pressed and the kit's selectable control styling rather than ad-hoc classes, so keyboard/focus/selected semantics are consistent and exposed to AT.
- 🟡 **Toggle/selection controls don't expose selected state to assistive tech** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2 (Name, Role, Value) — toggle state_ · `Op buttons (line 140), scope node buttons (60), scope tag buttons (81), preset buttons (620)`)
  - These act as multi-select toggles (visual --on class) but convey their pressed/selected state only via CSS class. There is no aria-pressed (toggles) or role=checkbox/aria-checked, so a screen-reader user cannot tell which operations, nodes, tags, or presets are currently selected.
  - → Add aria-pressed={selected} to the op/tag/preset toggle buttons (or model them as role=checkbox with aria-checked). Ensure the locked/disabled scope nodes also expose disabled state (they already use disabled, good).
- ⚪ **Unicode glyph '▾' used as an icon instead of a Lucide currentColor icon** (consistency · _icons linear Lucide-style currentColor, no emoji/glyph-as-icon_ · `Scope expand caret, line 145 ('▾')`)
  - The expand/collapse affordance uses a literal '▾' character as an icon. The principles require linear Lucide-style icons via currentColor and forbid glyph-as-icon; the rest of the file correctly uses <KitIcon>/<Icon>. Mixed iconography reduces visual consistency and the caret has no accessible expanded-state semantics.
  - → Replace '▾' with <KitIcon name="chevron-down"/> (rotated when open) and add aria-expanded to the scope toggle button so the disclosure state is programmatically exposed.
- ⚪ **Grant and proposal lists keyed by array index** (ux-pattern · _list rendering stability_ · `grants.map key={i} (line 653); proposed.map key={i} (line 680)`)
  - Grants (which can be added/removed/reordered) and proposals are rendered with key={i}. Index keys cause React to mismatch DOM/state on removal — e.g. deleting a middle grant can visually shift edit/open state (openIdx) and input focus to the wrong row, a real UX glitch given rows contain expandable ScopePickers and focused inputs.
  - → Key grants by a stable id (g.uri or a generated id) and proposals by atom uri+op, and track openIdx by that id rather than positional index.

### `src/screens/rights/ra-shell.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡1 ⚪3
_A standalone preview/layout shell for the "Права и доступ" (Rights & Access) admin area — renders the left nav, breadcrumbs, theme toggle, and a sub-tab switcher that mounts one of four rights sub-screens (role editor, criticality, SoD, grant trail)._

- 🟠 **Sub-tabs use aria-selected on plain buttons with no tablist/tab roles** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value / ARIA tabs pattern_ · `ra-shell.jsx:108-114 (.chs-subtabs and .chs-subtab buttons)`)
  - The section switcher renders <button aria-selected=...> inside a plain <div className="chs-subtabs">. aria-selected is only valid on elements with role tab/option/row/gridcell/treeitem. On a bare button it is an invalid ARIA state that screen readers ignore or mis-announce, and there is no role=tablist container, no roving tabindex, and no arrow-key navigation. Users on AT get no indication of which of the four sections is active, and the contract explicitly calls out this RightsSubTabs as a bespoke, non-primitive control.
  - → Implement the standard tabs pattern: wrap the buttons in role="tablist" (aria-label="Разделы раздела прав"), give each button role="tab", aria-selected, tabIndex (0 for active, -1 for others), and id; give the rendered .chs-screen role="tabpanel" with aria-labelledby pointing at the active tab. Add Left/Right arrow-key roving focus. Better still, extract a shared <Tabs>/<SubTabs> primitive (the contract notes none exists) so all section switchers behave identically.
- 🟡 **Disabled nav items are silent no-ops with no explanation** (feedback-state · _Affordance rule (enabled element must do something) / disabled-state communication_ · `ra-shell.jsx:72 (chs-navitem with disabled={item.id !== "rights"})`)
  - Every nav item except "Права и доступ" is hard-disabled. Real destinations like Инбокс задач (count 18), Процессы (count 7), Оргструктура and Аудит look like live entries with badges but are unclickable. Only the soon:true item (Бюджеты) shows a «скоро» tag; the rest are disabled with only a title tooltip, so a sighted user sees a count badge yet nothing happens on click and no reason is given. This is the affordance violation the contract warns about — disabled with no «скоро»/explanation reads as broken.
  - → If these routes are genuinely unavailable in this preview harness, mark them with the «скоро» affordance (or a tooltip explaining why) consistently, and consider hiding count badges on disabled items so they don't imply actionable, unread work. If they are real destinations, wire them up. Since the file header says this shell is a non-imported design harness, document that intent or gate the dead nav behind a clearly preview-only treatment.
- ⚪ **Theme toggle buttons may be announced icon-only (no programmatic label on the control)** (accessibility · _WCAG 4.1.2 / 1.1.1 — accessible name for buttons_ · `ra-shell.jsx:38-39 (RA_ThemeToggle buttons)`)
  - review_code flagged these as icon-only buttons missing aria-label. In practice each button contains visible text ("Тёмная"/"Светлая") after the <Icon>, so they do have an accessible name — but only if <Icon> renders decoratively (aria-hidden). If the Icon emits its own label/role, the name doubles up; if the text node is ever dropped to icon-only in a denser layout, the name disappears. The aria-pressed pair also expresses a single-choice setting as two independent toggles, which is slightly ambiguous to AT.
  - → Ensure <Icon> is aria-hidden="true"/decorative so the visible Russian label is the sole accessible name. Consider expressing the light/dark choice as a role="radiogroup" with two role="radio" buttons (or a single toggle), rather than two aria-pressed buttons, to communicate mutual exclusivity. Prefer the kit ThemeToggle primitive the contract references rather than this bespoke copy.
- ⚪ **Sub-screens have no Loading/Empty/Error boundary at the shell switch point** (feedback-state · _States always explicit (Empty/Loading/Error)_ · `ra-shell.jsx:116-121 (.chs-screen conditional render)`)
  - The shell mounts RoleEditorScreen/CriticalityScreen/SoDScreen/GrantTrailScreen directly with no error boundary or suspense/loading fallback. If a sub-screen throws or is slow to load its data, the user sees a blank .chs-screen with no LoadingState/ErrorState. The contract mandates every list/detail render an explicit state; the shell is the natural place to guarantee a fallback for the whole panel.
  - → Wrap the .chs-screen switch in an error boundary that renders <ErrorState onRetry> and, if the sub-screens fetch lazily, a <LoadingState role="status" aria-busy>. This makes the section panel fail visibly rather than going blank, per the states-always-explicit principle. (Individual sub-screens still own their own data-level empty/loading states.)
- ⚪ **Dev-jargon leaks into user-visible chrome** (content-clarity · _No dev-jargon in UI_ · `ra-shell.jsx:63 ("control-plane · 214 исп.") and :86 ("Оператор control-plane")`)
  - The nav brand subtitle and the footer user role both surface the internal term "control-plane" verbatim. The contract bars dev/code-name jargon in UI; "control-plane" is infrastructure terminology that means nothing to a business rights-administrator and is untranslated English inside otherwise-Russian chrome.
  - → Replace with a human Russian label for the administration surface (e.g. «Администрирование» / «Панель управления» / a tenant or instance name) and a role title like «Администратор доступа». Keep internal identifiers out of the visible shell. Note this is a preview harness, so confirm whether these strings ever ship before investing, but the same strings likely originate in the real AppShell.

### `src/screens/rights/ra-sod.jsx` — 🟠4 🟡2 ⚪3
_Manage the tenant's Separation-of-Duties (SoD) rule registry — list/create/delete conflict-of-interest constraints between roles — and run an ad-hoc conflict check for a given employee's held roles._

- 🟠 **Dev-jargon leaks throughout user-facing UI (UUID fields, JSON scope, raw subjectId, raw error text, truncated IDs)** (content-clarity · _principles.md — no dev-jargon in UI (no UUIDs, code-names); G5 honest-text gate_ · `ra-sod.jsx:234,245,238,249 (Роль A/B UUID inputs + placeholders); :276-285 (scope JSON textarea); :471 (subjectId); :104-106 (rule.id.slice)`)
  - This screen exposes raw implementation to end users in direct violation of the no-dev-jargon principle and the file's own stated G5 invariant. Users must type role UUIDs by hand ('Роль A (UUID)', placeholder 'xxxxxxxx-xxxx-...'), hand-author a JSON scope object ('Область применения (scope, JSON)' prefilled with JSON.stringify(EMPTY_SCOPE)), and read a raw subjectId echoed as 'Сотрудник: <subjectId>'. The rules table also shows a truncated UUID column ('ID', rule.id.slice(0,8)+'…'). A no-code B2B operator cannot reasonably supply a UUID or valid JSON.
  - → Replace the two UUID text inputs with a role <Select> (or searchable picker) populated from the roles API, keyed by name. Remove the JSON scope textarea from the primary path — offer a guided scope builder or hide it behind 'Дополнительно' with sensible defaults. Echo the employee by display name, not subjectId. Drop or relabel the raw 'ID' column (e.g. show nothing, or a human rule name); a sliced UUID is not actionable to users.
- 🟠 **Form labels not programmatically associated with their controls** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / 4.1.2; forms/text-field (Missing Input Label)_ · `ra-sod.jsx:220-228 (Тип правила select), :234-242 (Роль A), :245-253 (Роль B), :276-285 (scope textarea), :437-446 (Сотрудник input)`)
  - Every label except the dynamic-mode checkbox is a bare <label className="chs-field__label"> placed adjacent to the control, with no htmlFor/id binding. Screen readers will not announce the label when focus lands on the select/input/textarea, and clicking the label text does not focus the control. The 'Сотрудник' field uses a <span> as its visual label with no association at all. review_code flagged these as missing-label errors.
  - → Use the kit <Field>/<Select> primitives, which wire label↔control binding, aria-invalid and aria-describedby per the contract. If kept hand-rolled, add matching id/htmlFor to each label/control pair and give the 'Сотрудник' input an aria-label or a real bound <label>.
- 🟠 **Hand-rolled inputs/select/textarea/buttons/overlay instead of design-system primitives** (consistency · _Contract §3 — consume <Field>/<Select>/<Button>; reviewer flag: raw <input>/<select> and rolled-its-own <button>_ · `ra-sod.jsx:221 (<select>), :235/:246 (<input>), :279 (<textarea>), :263 (<input type=checkbox>), :125/:295/:302/:395/:447 (className="chs-btn…" buttons), :406 (hand-rolled raised card)`)
  - App-content controls bypass the kit. Buttons are raw <button className="chs-btn…"> rather than <Button variant size loading disabled>, so loading state is faked with a literal '…' / 'Сохранение…' string instead of the kit's aria-busy spinner, and focus-ring/aria-disabled handling is re-implemented ad hoc. Inputs/select/textarea skip <Field>/<Select>. There is no <Checkbox> wrapper (acknowledged kit gap) but the rest have primitives. This is exactly the 'app-content buttons must use kit' consumption violation.
  - → Swap raw <button> for <Button> (use glyph + loading props instead of literal '…'), inputs for <Field>, the type selector for <Select>. Render the create-rule panel inside a kit surface rather than a hand-built raised div. Where a primitive is genuinely missing (checkbox), keep it but file the kit gap.
- 🟠 **Destructive delete uses window.confirm instead of ConfirmDialog** (feedback-state · _Contract §3 — <ConfirmDialog> replaces window.confirm; reviewer flag_ · `ra-sod.jsx:90 (handleDelete) inside SodRuleRow`)
  - Deleting a SoD rule — a security-governance control — is gated by a native window.confirm(). This is an explicit contract violation (ConfirmDialog exists for exactly this), is unstyled/unthemed, not focus-trapped via the kit convention, and gives no context about which rule (it just says 'Удалить правило разделения обязанностей?' with no role pair). It also can't convey the security weight of removing a separation-of-duties constraint.
  - → Replace with <ConfirmDialog> (danger variant) showing the specific role pair being unprotected and a clear consequence line. Wire its confirm to handleDelete.
- 🟡 **No success feedback after create or delete** (feedback-state · _Contract §3/§4 — Toast + useToasts for transient outcomes_ · `ra-sod.jsx:371 (handleCreated), :366 (handleDelete), :208 (createSodRule call)`)
  - On successful create the form just closes and the list refetches; on successful delete the row vanishes. Neither emits a Toast, so the user gets no positive confirmation that a governance-critical rule was added or removed — only the absence of an error. The contract mandates Toast for transient outcomes and the kit provides useToasts()+ToastViewport.
  - → Emit a status Toast on successful create ('Правило добавлено') and delete ('Правило удалено'); emit an error Toast on failure paths. Wire useToasts()+ToastViewport at this tree (no toast provider exists, per contract).
- 🟡 **Delete error rendered as inline raw string with no recovery affordance** (feedback-state · _Contract §4 — Error=role=alert + Повторить; states always explicit_ · `ra-sod.jsx:134-138 (deleteError span)`)
  - A failed delete shows the raw err.message (e.g. server text or 'HTTP 500') in a small danger-colored <span> appended after the button, with no role=alert (not announced to screen readers) and no retry. The message may be raw backend text / status code (dev-jargon), and after a failure 'deleting' is reset but the user has no guided next step.
  - → Surface delete failures via an error Toast (role=alert) or the kit error convention with a human message and a retry, rather than an unannounced inline raw string. Map backend errors to user-readable copy.
- ⚪ **Conflict-check input has no Empty/initial result feedback when subject has no display and no client validation** (flow · _forms/text-field; affordance + explicit states_ · `ra-sod.jsx:438-454 (subject input + Проверить), :466-489 (result block)`)
  - Минор flow gaps: the 'Проверить' button is always enabled even with empty input (intentionally = current user, but the placeholder 'Логин (пусто — текущий пользователь)' is the only signal). There is no validation/feedback if a typed login is unknown beyond whatever the API returns. The 'нет' fallback for held roles ('Роли: нет') is fine but terse.
  - → Keep empty-allowed behavior but make it explicit (e.g. helper text under the field). Consider distinguishing 'unknown employee' from 'employee with no roles' in the result copy.
