- ⚪ **Status severity conveyed by uppercase Russian labels in colored chips — verify non-color cue and contrast** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.4.1 (use of color); contract status tokens_ · `ra-sod.jsx:329-331 ('БЛОКИРОВКА'/'ИНФОРМАЦИЯ' sev chip), :79-83 (KindChip), :327/:477 (decorative glyph divs)`)
  - Severity/kind is shown via class-driven color chips (chs-conflict__sev--block/warn, chs-sodsev--block/warn) plus a purely decorative empty <div className="chs-conflict__glyph"/>. The text label provides a non-color cue (good), but the glyph divs carry no text/aria and the color contrast of these custom classes is not verifiable here (the contract requires AA in both themes, and these are custom chs-sodsev/chs-conflict classes, not the audited StatusChip primitive).
  - → Confirm the chs-sodsev/chs-conflict color classes meet AA contrast in light and dark themes; prefer the kit StatusChip/severity tokens. Ensure decorative glyph divs are aria-hidden and that meaning never rests on color alone (it currently also has text, which is good — keep that).
- ⚪ **Inline style objects with layout/spacing literals scattered across render** (consistency · _Contract §1/§2 — tokens via classes; G6 no new inline styles (file's own invariant)_ · `ra-sod.jsx:104,113,135,219,261,284,289,294,389-391,406-407,432,445,470 (style={{…}} blocks)`)
  - The file's own header asserts invariant 'G6 — no new inline styles or raw colour literals', yet the render is full of inline style objects for layout, spacing, font and color (e.g. style={{ fontSize:'var(--chs-text-2xs)', color:'var(--chs-color-text-faint)' }}, flex/gap/margin blocks, the raised-card border). They do use tokens (so not raw literals), but they are inline styles the invariant disallows and they duplicate what utility classes/primitives should own, hurting consistency and theming.
  - → Move recurring layout/spacing/typography into the chs-* class layer (or kit primitives) so the JSX carries semantics, not style objects — aligning with the stated G6 invariant and easing dark-theme/token changes.

### `src/screens/rights/screen-rights.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡3 ⚪3
_Read-only "Rights and access" screen: a left rail lists roles grouped by department, the right pane shows the selected role's grant matrix (resource · operation · scope), its holders, and the auto-derived tools/visible-form-fields._

- 🟠 **«Поиск роли» — фальшивый элемент управления (silent no-op)** (feedback-state · _Affordance rule (design contract): enabled element MUST do something_ · `screen-rights.jsx:110 — <div className="chs-rights__search"><Icon name="search"/><span>Поиск роли</span></div>`)
  - The search box looks like an interactive input (search icon + placeholder-style label) but is a static <div> with no input, no handler, no filtering. It is a silent no-op affordance — exactly what the design contract forbids. Users will click/type and nothing happens, with no «скоро» indication. It is also not keyboard-focusable.
  - → Either (a) implement it as a real filter over the rail using the kit <Field> with a search affordance and wire role-name filtering, or (b) if search is not in this layer, remove the element or render it visibly disabled with a «скоро» label and aria-disabled, matching the honest-degradation done for the «Запросить изменение» button.
- 🟠 **Список ролей в рейле жёстко зашит по dev-ID; роли вне трёх отделов молча исчезают** (flow · _data-driven list / honest-empty_ · `screen-rights.jsx:12-16 (ROLE_GROUPS) and 111-123 (rail render)`)
  - The rail renders only roles whose id is in the hardcoded ROLE_GROUPS array (role-fin-control, role-cs-l1, …). Any role returned by /api/rights that is not in this list is silently omitted from the rail — the user cannot select it, and there is no «прочие роли» bucket. The header count {roles.length} can therefore disagree with the number of visible rows, and new roles created in a tenant never appear until code is edited. This also bakes dev codenames into layout logic.
  - → Group roles dynamically from the API payload (e.g. by a role.dept field), rendering an «Прочее»/ungrouped section for anything without a known department, so every role in {roles.length} is reachable. Drive grouping from data, not a static id whitelist.
- 🟡 **Утечка dev-идентификатора роли в UI (role.id codename)** (content-clarity · _principles.md: no dev-jargon / UUIDs / code-names in UI_ · `screen-rights.jsx:136 — <Mono style={{color:'var(--chs-color-text-faint)'}}>{role.id}</Mono>`)
  - The role detail subtitle prints the raw machine id (e.g. «role-fin-approve-250») as user-facing text. The design principles explicitly bar code-names/IDs in the UI. The faint colour also signals this is decorative, yet it is the only secondary identifier shown. MonoId is imported (line 8) but never used — the intended primitive for machine ids is being bypassed.
  - → Show a human-readable secondary descriptor (e.g. role purpose/department) instead of role.id, or drop the id entirely. If a technical id must be surfaced for support, render it through the dedicated <MonoId> primitive (which is already imported) and keep it out of the primary heading area — ideally behind a copy/tooltip affordance.
- 🟡 **Отключённая кнопка «Запросить изменение» сообщает причину только через title** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.3.1 / 4.1.2 — state and instructions must be programmatically available_ · `screen-rights.jsx:143 — <Button ... disabled title="…эндпойнт ещё не реализован…">`)
  - The honest-degradation intent is good, but a fully disabled button is removed from the tab order, so keyboard and screen-reader users can neither focus it nor hear the title; native title tooltips also don't appear on keyboard focus and are not reliably announced. The only signal that the primary action is unavailable is a mouse-hover tooltip.
  - → Use aria-disabled="true" (keeping the element focusable) with a visible «скоро» affordance, and convey the reason via an aria-describedby hint or a kit <Tooltip> that is keyboard-triggerable — rather than relying solely on the native title attribute.
- 🟡 **Заголовки колонок матрицы грантов не связаны с ячейками семантически** (ux-pattern · _data-table / WCAG 1.3.1 info-and-relationships_ · `screen-rights.jsx:164-180 — .chs-grants colhead + .chs-grant rows (all <div>)`)
  - The grant matrix is a 3-column tabular structure (Ресурс / Операции / Охват) built entirely from <div>s with a separate colhead row. There is no table semantics or ARIA grid roles, so screen-reader users get a flat stream of cells with no column association, and the header row is just visual. The contract notes there is no <Table> primitive, so this ad-hoc construction is expected — but it ships without any relationship semantics.
  - → Add role="table"/role="row"/role="columnheader"/role="cell" (or use a real <table>) so the column header «Охват» etc. is associated with each cell. At minimum give the colhead aria semantics and scope so the relationship is programmatic, not purely visual.
- ⚪ **Глифы носителей роли в рейле передают тип только цветом/формой без текста** (accessibility · _principles.md executor glyphs must duplicate with text; WCAG 1.1.1_ · `screen-rights.jsx:29-31 — rolerow holders: <ExecGlyph type={h.type} size={8}/> with title={h.name}`)
  - In the rail, each holder is shown as an 8px ExecGlyph whose only accessible name is a hover title on the wrapping span (name, not type). The executor type (Human/Agent/Service) — which the principles say must be conveyed by shape+color AND text — is not exposed as text or as an accessible label here. Title on a non-interactive span is unreliable for assistive tech and absent for keyboard users.
  - → Provide an accessible label that includes the executor type and name (e.g. aria-label on the glyph wrapper combining type + h.name), and consider the kit <ExecutorBadge> or a visible count for the rail. Ensure the glyph is not the sole non-text carrier of meaning.
- ⚪ **Кастомная кнопка-строка роли без явного type** (consistency · _forms/submit-button (review_code)_ · `screen-rights.jsx:23 — <button className="chs-rolerow" …>`)
  - review_code flagged the rolerow as a <button> without an explicit type; inside any form context it would default to submit. It is also a hand-rolled <button> rather than a kit primitive — though for a dense selectable list row this is a reasonable nav-style exception, it should at least be defensively typed.
  - → Add type="button" to the rolerow button. (The kit <Button> already manages its own type, so the second review_code hit is a false positive for the imported primitive.)
- ⚪ **Состояние выбранной роли: initialRole может молча игнорироваться** (flow · _selection state / honest feedback_ · `screen-rights.jsx:99 (role = roles.find(r=>r.id===sel) || roles[0]) and 66-72`)
  - If an initialRole prop or a sel value points at a role not present in the loaded set, the screen silently falls back to roles[0] with no indication that the requested role was not found — a deep-link to a removed/renamed role lands the user on an unrelated role without explanation.
  - → When a requested initialRole/sel cannot be resolved after load, surface a brief toast or inline note («Роль не найдена, показана первая») rather than silently substituting roles[0], so a stale deep-link doesn't mislead the user about which role they're viewing.

### `src/app-shell/icon.jsx` — 🟡1 ⚪2
_A presentational icon factory: renders one of ~20 named inline SVG glyphs (linear, currentColor, 16x16, aria-hidden) used across the app shell and screens._

- 🟡 **Unknown icon name renders an empty SVG with no fallback** (feedback-state · _Empty/error state — graceful degradation_ · `icon.jsx:11-32 (the chain of name === ... && expressions)`)
  - The component is a flat chain of `name === "x" && (...)` branches with no else/default. If a caller passes a name that is not in the list (typo, renamed icon, future icon not yet added), the SVG renders empty — a silent 16x16 blank box. There is no dev warning and no visible placeholder, so a missing glyph is indistinguishable from a layout gap and is easy to ship unnoticed. This is the icon-level analogue of the contract's 'states always explicit / never bare emptiness' principle.
  - → Add a default branch: when `name` matches nothing, render a visible fallback glyph (e.g. a thin square or a '?' box drawn with the same stroke params `p`) so a missing icon is obvious in the UI, and emit a `console.warn(\`Icon: unknown name "${name}"\`)` in development to surface typos early. Consider deriving the known names into a Set/map so the lookup is data-driven rather than a 20-deep boolean chain.
- ⚪ **Icon is purely decorative with no accessible-label path for standalone/meaningful use** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.1.1 Non-text Content_ · `icon.jsx:12 (svg aria-hidden="true")`)
  - Every instance is hardcoded `aria-hidden="true"`. That is correct when the icon sits next to a visible text label (the common case in nav/buttons), but the component offers no way to expose an accessible name when an icon is used alone as the sole content of an interactive control (e.g. an icon-only 'dots'/menu or 'search' button). In that case callers must remember to put aria-label on the wrapping button; the icon itself cannot carry a title, so the failure mode is silent — a screen reader announces an unlabeled button.
  - → Accept an optional `title`/`label` prop. When provided, drop `aria-hidden`, set `role="img"` and render a `<title>` (and `aria-labelledby`); when absent, keep the current `aria-hidden="true"` default. This keeps the decorative default safe while giving icon-only controls a correct path. Alternatively document loudly that icon-only buttons MUST supply their own aria-label on the control.
- ⚪ **Hardcoded geometry/size with no token-driven sizing hook** (consistency · _Design-system token consumption_ · `icon.jsx:10 (strokeWidth: 1.6) and icon.jsx:12 (width="16" height="16")`)
  - Fixed `width="16" height="16"` and `strokeWidth: 1.6` are baked in. Per the contract, sizes/strokes should be expressible via tokens (`--chs-*`). Color is correctly tokenizable via `currentColor`, but size is not: any caller wanting a larger icon must override via className and fight the explicit width/height attributes, and stroke weight won't scale proportionally. This is a minor, low-severity deviation since SVG attributes are scaled by CSS, but it limits consistent multi-size usage.
  - → Drop the fixed `width`/`height` attributes (or default them to `1em`) and let size flow from `font-size`/className so callers can size via spacing/text tokens; keep `viewBox` for the coordinate system. Optionally accept a `size` prop mapping to token values. Stroke 1.6 at a 16-unit viewBox is acceptable as the intended optical weight, but document it as the canonical value.

### `src/app-shell/shell.jsx` — 🟠2 🟡6 ⚪3
_The app shell: left navigation, topbar with breadcrumbs and per-screen actions, theme toggle, command palette (Cmd+K), auth bootstrap/login gate, and client-side routing for every product screen._

- 🟠 **downloadAuditLog uses raw alert() instead of the toast/state convention** (feedback-state · _Toast / feedback-after-action (principles.md §4)_ · `shell.jsx:248-274 (downloadAuditLog), invoked by «Экспорт лога» button at shell.jsx:316`)
  - All three error/permission paths (401, non-ok HTTP, network catch) surface a native window.alert(). The contract explicitly flags this as a violation: the kit provides <Toast>+<ToastViewport>+useToasts() for transient outcomes, and ConfirmDialog/state primitives replace native dialogs. alert() is blocking, unstyled, untranslatable in tone, breaks WCAG-consistent focus handling, and gives no success feedback at all — a successful export shows nothing, so the user cannot tell the action worked versus silently failed.
  - → Wire useToasts() at the shell tree and emit a status toast on success («Лог аудита выгружен») and an assertive error toast (role=alert) on each failure branch, replacing all four alert() calls. Since the contract notes there is no toast provider/context, mount one <ToastViewport> in AppShell and pass the emitter down (or via context) so the topbar export action and future actions share it.
- 🟠 **Command-palette listbox is not keyboard-navigable (no arrow-key roving focus)** (accessibility · _Combobox/Listbox keyboard interaction; WCAG 2.1.1 (Keyboard)_ · `shell.jsx:454-473 (role=listbox + role=option items); input at shell.jsx:445`)
  - The palette declares role="listbox" with role="option" children, but there is no arrow-key handling, no aria-activedescendant, and every option has aria-selected={false} hardcoded. Keyboard users can only Tab through every option one by one or press Enter to pick the first match (onSubmit). A command palette is a keyboard-first surface; the ARIA listbox pattern it advertises promises Up/Down navigation and a visible active option that is not delivered, which is misleading to assistive tech.
  - → Implement roving selection: track an active index, handle ArrowUp/ArrowDown/Enter on the input, set aria-activedescendant to the active option id and aria-selected on that option, and scroll it into view. Alternatively drop the listbox/option roles and present it as a simple filtered link list — but given it claims the listbox pattern, completing the keyboard interaction is the right fix.
- 🟡 **No loading / error state while the audit export is in flight** (feedback-state · _Loading state on async action (principles.md §2 «States always explicit»)_ · `shell.jsx:316 «Экспорт лога» <Button>; downloadAuditLog shell.jsx:248`)
  - The export button has no loading/busy state. The fetch+blob round-trip can take seconds on a large log; during that window the button looks idle and re-clickable, inviting double-submits and giving zero feedback that anything is happening. The kit <Button> supports a `loading` prop (aria-busy) precisely for this.
  - → Lift export state into a small handler component: track an `exporting` flag, render <Button loading={exporting} disabled={exporting}> while the fetch runs, and clear it in a finally block. This gives an explicit busy state and prevents concurrent exports.
- 🟡 **Bare loading screen during auth bootstrap bypasses the state primitive** (feedback-state · _LoadingState primitive (principles.md §4: Loading = role=status aria-busy)_ · `shell.jsx:652-658 (!authReady branch)`)
  - The first-paint loading gate renders a hand-rolled <div className="chs-login-loading"><p>Загрузка…</p></div> with no role=status / aria-busy. The contract requires every loading surface to use <LoadingState>/<Spinner> with role=status aria-busy so assistive tech announces the wait. A bare <p> is silent to screen readers and inconsistent with the rest of the app.
  - → Replace the bespoke loading markup with the kit <LoadingState> (or at minimum add role="status" aria-busy="true" and a <Spinner>). This is the very first thing every user sees, so consistency and the announced wait matter.
- 🟡 **Auth bootstrap has no error/retry surface — failures fall silently to login** (feedback-state · _ErrorState onRetry (principles.md §4: Error = role=alert + Повторить)_ · `shell.jsx:508-564 (bootstrap effect), tenant resolve catch shell.jsx:556 and crumb-app fetch catch shell.jsx:598`)
  - When loadAuthConfig/keycloak callback throws, authError is captured but only LoginScreen receives it; a failed tenant resolve (orgLabel) is swallowed and the sidebar silently shows «—» as the org name with no indication that resolution failed versus a genuinely unnamed tenant. There is no ErrorState/retry anywhere in the shell for these network failures, contrary to the explicit-states principle.
  - → For the org-label resolution failure, distinguish «not resolved yet / failed» from «empty» (e.g. keep the placeholder but expose a retry or at least don't conflate with a real empty value). For hard auth-config failures, ensure the surfaced authError is shown via an ErrorState-style alert with a Повторить affordance rather than only a fall-through to login.
- 🟡 **Logout button is a bespoke <button> instead of kit <Button>** (consistency · _Design-system primitive consumption (contract §3 / Flags)_ · `shell.jsx:762-764 (chs-nav__logout-btn)`)
  - «Выйти» is an app-content action (it mutates session state / redirects), not a navigation item, yet it is a raw <button> with a bespoke class. The contract's nav exception covers ThemeToggle/NavItem only; app-content buttons «must use kit». A rolled-its-own button loses the kit's focus-ring, aria-disabled/aria-busy handling and visual variant consistency. Logout is also mildly destructive (ends the session) and offers no confirmation.
  - → Render logout with <Button variant="ghost" size="sm"> (or secondary). Optionally gate it behind <ConfirmDialog> given it ends the session, but at minimum adopt the kit button so focus-ring and states are consistent.
- 🟡 **Command-palette search uses raw <input> instead of <Field>** (consistency · _Field primitive / label binding (contract §3 / Flags: raw <input> without <Field>)_ · `shell.jsx:445-453 (chs-palette__input)`)
  - The palette filter is a bare <input className="chs-input"> relying only on aria-label and placeholder. The contract flags raw <input> without <Field> as a violation; <Field> provides the label↔control binding, aria-invalid and aria-describedby hint wiring consistently. Placeholder-as-label also disappears on input.
  - → Wrap the search input in <Field> (visually-hidden label «Поиск раздела» is acceptable for a palette) so it consumes the kit's accessible labeling/hint conventions rather than a one-off aria-label + className.
- 🟡 **Disabled topbar buttons rely on a Tooltip that keyboard/touch users cannot trigger** (accessibility · _Disabled control discoverability; affordance rule (principles.md §2)_ · `shell.jsx:304-313 («Исполнитель») and shell.jsx:321-323 («Экспорт прав»)`)
  - These honest-disabled buttons carry their explanation («доступно владельцу» / «функция в разработке») only in a wrapping <Tooltip>. A `disabled` <button> is removed from the tab order and receives no pointer events, so the tooltip never fires on keyboard focus and is unreliable on touch — the very reason for the disabled state becomes invisible to a large class of users. The intent (make the affordance honest) is good but the explanation is undiscoverable.
  - → Use aria-disabled="true" instead of the native disabled attribute (keeping the button focusable but inert via handler guard) so the tooltip can fire on focus, OR move the reason into always-visible helper text / a «скоро» style inline badge next to the button rather than tooltip-only. Ensure the tooltip content is also wired via aria-describedby.
- ⚪ **Palette «Ничего не найдено» empty result is a styled <li>, not the EmptyState primitive** (feedback-state · _EmptyState (principles.md §4: Empty = role=status + CTA)_ · `shell.jsx:455-457`)
  - The no-results case renders a plain <li role="presentation">Ничего не найдено</li> with no role=status, so screen-reader users filtering the palette get no announcement that the result set went empty. The contract requires explicit Empty components.
  - → Render the empty result via <EmptyState> (or at least add role="status" aria-live="polite" to the container) so the «nothing found» transition is announced. A CTA is not needed here, but the announced status is.
- ⚪ **Theme toggle pressed-state is announced but visually pressed state must not be the only signal** (feedback-state · _Toggle button selected state; WCAG 1.4.1 (Use of Color)_ · `shell.jsx:190-201 (ThemeToggle)`)
  - ThemeToggle uses aria-pressed correctly (good), but the active/inactive distinction is delivered through CSS class styling not visible in this file. If the selected theme is conveyed by color/background alone, it risks failing 1.4.1. The bespoke buttons also lack `type="button"` (review_code lines 4/7) — harmless here since they are not in a form, but inconsistent with the kit which always sets type.
  - → Verify in tokens/CSS that the pressed segment has a non-color affordance (e.g. filled background + border or a check) and sufficient contrast in both themes. Add type="button" to both toggle buttons for consistency and to avoid accidental form submission if ever nested.
- ⚪ **Bespoke nav/toggle/search buttons omit explicit type="button"** (consistency · _forms/submit-button (review_code)_ · `shell.jsx:193,196 (ThemeToggle), shell.jsx:230 (NavItem), shell.jsx:688 (nav search), shell.jsx:762 (logout)`)
  - review_code flagged 8 buttons missing an explicit type. The kit <Button> always sets type; these hand-rolled buttons (the acceptable nav exception) do not. Not a defect in the current non-form context, but a latent footgun if any is ever moved inside a <form> (it would default to submit), and it is an inconsistency with the kit contract.
  - → Add type="button" to all bespoke <button> elements in the shell (ThemeToggle x2, NavItem, nav search already-typed/logout). Low effort, removes the warning class and a latent submit bug.

### `src/components/components.jsx` — 🟠1 🟡3 ⚪3
_Exported domain + system design-system primitives (buttons, fields, overlays, state components, status/executor chips, meters, toasts) consumed by all Choros app screens._

- 🟠 **RoleAssignment uses a clickable <div> — not keyboard-operable, no role/focus** (accessibility · _WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard (Level A); ux-pattern accessibility/non-semantic-click-handler_ · `components.jsx:211-221 (RoleAssignment, <div className="chs-asgn ..." onClick={onOpen}>)`)
  - When onOpen is provided the row becomes interactive via onClick on a bare <div>. It is not in the tab order, cannot be activated by Enter/Space, exposes no role/aria-label, and shows no focus ring. Keyboard and screen-reader users cannot open the assignment detail. Both audit tools flagged this (check_accessibility: critical 2.1.1; review_code: non-semantic click handler). This directly violates the contract's affordance rule ('an enabled element MUST do something' — and must be reachable).
  - → When onOpen is set, render a real interactive element: either wrap the row content in a <button type="button"> styled as a row, or add role="button", tabIndex={0}, an aria-label (e.g. `Открыть назначение: ${role}`), and an onKeyDown handler activating on Enter/Space. Keep it a plain <div> (no handlers) when onOpen is absent.
- 🟡 **Modal/Drawer overlay closes on mousedown — accidental dismissal and text-selection drag closes the dialog** (feedback-state · _overlays/modal — dismissal semantics_ · `components.jsx:353 (Modal overlay) and 386 (Drawer overlay), onMouseDown with e.target === e.currentTarget`)
  - Close-on-overlay fires on mousedown rather than click. If a user presses inside the panel and drags onto the backdrop while selecting text (common in forms/textareas), the release target differs and behavior is surprising; more importantly mousedown-to-dismiss removes the chance to abort by releasing elsewhere. For dialogs that may hold unsaved input this risks silent data loss with no confirmation.
  - → Switch backdrop dismissal to onClick (target===currentTarget) so a full press-and-release on the backdrop is required, matching native dialog conventions. For Modals wrapping editable forms, consider gating overlay/Esc dismissal behind a dirty-state check or routing through ConfirmDialog.
- 🟡 **Popover lacks focus management and labelling expected of role="dialog"** (accessibility · _WCAG 2.4.3 Focus Order / 4.1.2 Name,Role,Value; overlays/popover_ · `components.jsx:473-498 (Popover), inner <div className="chs-popover" role="dialog">`)
  - The popover panel declares role="dialog" but, unlike Modal/Drawer, does not move focus into the panel on open, does not trap or restore focus, and has no aria-label/aria-labelledby. A role="dialog" with no accessible name and no focus entry is announced ambiguously and is awkward for keyboard users (focus stays on the page behind it). It also has no aria linkage between trigger and panel (no aria-expanded/aria-controls).
  - → Either drop role="dialog" for a lightweight menu (use role="menu"/listbox or no role for pure context) OR bring it to dialog parity: focus first focusable on open, restore on close, and accept a labelId/aria-label. Add aria-expanded + aria-controls on the trigger so the relationship is exposed. Given the contract positions Popover as 'light context', the simpler non-dialog path is preferable.
- 🟡 **Tooltip content is not reliably reachable/announced and depends on CSS hover/focus only** (accessibility · _WCAG 1.4.13 Content on Hover or Focus; overlays/tooltip_ · `components.jsx:503-512 (Tooltip)`)
  - The trigger is a generic <span tabIndex={0}> with no role; aria-describedby points to a bubble that is shown purely via CSS :hover/:focus-within. There is no Esc-to-dismiss and the bubble is not hoverable-persistent in markup, so it may not satisfy WCAG 1.4.13 (dismissable, hoverable, persistent). A focusable span with no role also adds an unlabelled tab stop for screen-reader users when the child is non-interactive.
  - → Ensure the bubble stays visible while hovered (CSS already may, verify) and add Esc-to-hide. If the wrapped child is already interactive (button/link), put aria-describedby on the child instead of adding a separate focusable span to avoid a redundant/empty tab stop. Confirm the bubble is not removed from the a11y tree when visually shown.
- ⚪ **Inline hardcoded style values bypass design tokens in BudgetMeter** (consistency · _design-system token consumption_ · `components.jsx:168 (BudgetMeter label inline style fontSize/color) — also raw style usage at 174, 198-200, 247`)
  - The contract mandates tokens as the single source and flags hardcoded color/size as a violation. Line 168 sets the label via an inline style object referencing tokens (acceptable values) but as an inline style rather than a class, which is inconsistent with the rest of the kit (every other label uses a chs- class). The percentage-width inline styles (174, 198-200) are legitimate dynamic values, but the static font-size/color pair on 168 is presentational and should live in CSS. ReservationMeter's equivalent label correctly uses chs-resv__label (192).
  - → Replace the inline style on the BudgetMeter label with a class (e.g. chs-budget__label) defined in CSS using the same tokens, mirroring ReservationMeter. Keep only the genuinely dynamic width:%/left:% values inline.
- ⚪ **Toast queue has no provider/context — each consumer must wire useToasts + ToastViewport, inviting inconsistent feedback** (feedback-state · _feedback/toast — global notification surface_ · `components.jsx:539-561 (useToasts / ToastViewport)`)
  - There is no ToastProvider/context; the contract itself lists this as a gap. Because every React tree must independently call useToasts() and mount a ToastViewport, screens can easily ship action feedback inconsistently (some toast on save/delete, some silently succeed), and toasts fired from deep components require prop-drilling the push fn. This undermines the contract's 'feedback after actions' guarantee across the app.
  - → Add a ToastProvider + useToastContext() (or a singleton toast store) so any component can push without local wiring, and document one app-root ToastViewport. This is a kit-level addition, not a per-screen fix.
- ⚪ **useToasts auto-dismiss uses setTimeout with no pause-on-hover/focus — transient errors may vanish before they are read** (feedback-state · _feedback/toast — timing & dismissibility_ · `components.jsx:543-549 (push) and 521 (error toast)`)
  - All toasts, including tone="error" (role=alert), auto-dismiss after the same default 4000ms with no pause when hovered or focused. For a dense B2B tool a failed save/delete error that disappears in 4s and cannot be paused harms recoverability; the timer also is not cleared on manual dismiss (minor leak / double-dismiss is guarded by filter but the timer still fires).
  - → Default error/warning toasts to no auto-dismiss (duration 0) so the user must acknowledge them, or pause the timer on pointerenter/focus and resume on leave. Clear the per-toast timeout in dismiss() to avoid stray timers.

### `src/design/showcase.jsx` — 🔴1 🟡2 ⚪3
_A static brandbook/showcase page that previews the Choros design-system foundation — tokens (color, type, spacing), domain primitives, and the mandatory OBLIK kit (surfaces + states) — for both light and dark themes._

- 🔴 **ReactDOM used but never imported — page crashes at runtime** (consistency · `showcase.jsx:4, :529`)
  - Line 4 imports only `React` (`import React, { useState } from 'react'`), but line 529 calls `ReactDOM.createRoot(document.getElementById("root")).render(<App />)`. `ReactDOM` is an undefined reference, so the entire showcase throws `ReactDOM is not defined` on load and renders nothing. The whole brandbook preview — the artifact other screens are supposed to visually inherit from — is non-functional.
  - → Add `import ReactDOM from 'react-dom/client';` at the top (React 18 client API). Verify the page actually mounts in the dev build, since a brandbook that never renders cannot serve as the consistency reference it claims to be.
- 🟡 **Glyph character ▾ used as an icon — violates no-emoji/Lucide rule** (consistency · `showcase.jsx:457 (Popover trigger `Меню ▾`)`)
  - The Popover trigger label is `Меню ▾`, using the Unicode caret `▾` as a dropdown affordance glyph. The design-system principles mandate linear Lucide-style icons drawn with `currentColor` and explicitly forbid using glyph/emoji characters as icons (the same rule that bans ✓/✗). A literal `▾` will not inherit icon sizing, stroke, or color tokens, and is read aloud by screen readers as 'black down-pointing small triangle'.
  - → Replace the `▾` text with a `<KitIcon name="chevron-down" />` (the kit's icon primitive, already used elsewhere in this file via `KitIcon name="plus"`/`"inbox"`), rendered with `aria-hidden` so it is decorative, and keep the visible label as just `Меню`.
- 🟡 **Popover menu items rolled as raw <button> instead of kit primitive, with no menu semantics** (accessibility · _navigation/menu_ · `showcase.jsx:460-462 (`chs-menuitem` buttons inside Popover)`)
  - The action menu (Открыть / Дублировать / Архивировать) is built from bare `<button className="chs-menuitem">` elements. Per the design-system flags, app-content interactive controls must use the `<Button>` kit primitive rather than hand-rolled `<button>`. Additionally the list has no menu semantics: no `role="menu"` on the container, no `role="menuitem"` on the items, and no arrow-key navigation — so it does not behave like the dropdown menu it visually presents. The ux-patterns review also flagged these buttons for a missing explicit `type` attribute (defaulting to `type="submit"` inside any form is a latent bug).
  - → Render menu items via the kit (e.g. `<Button variant="ghost">` or a dedicated menu-item primitive) and add `type="button"`. Give the container `role="menu"` and each item `role="menuitem"`, with roving-tabindex/arrow-key handling, so keyboard users get standard menu navigation. Since the contract notes there is no `<Menu>` primitive, this showcase is the right place to demonstrate the correct accessible pattern rather than an ad-hoc one.
- ⚪ **Raw <button> theme toggle missing explicit type attribute** (accessibility · _forms/submit-button_ · `showcase.jsx:497-498 (`chs-theme-toggle` buttons)`)
  - The two theme-toggle buttons are bespoke raw `<button>` elements without a `type` attribute (flagged by ux-patterns review_code). A bespoke nav button is an acceptable design-system exception, but an omitted `type` defaults to `submit`; if this markup is ever nested in a form it will trigger unintended submission. The `aria-pressed` + `role="group"` labeling is otherwise correct.
  - → Add `type="button"` to both toggle buttons. Optionally consider modeling this as a two-option segmented control with `role="radiogroup"`/`aria-checked` since it is a single-select choice rather than two independent toggles, but `aria-pressed` is acceptable.
- ⚪ **Demo form controls are inert no-ops (uncontrolled, no submit handler)** (feedback-state · _forms/form-validation_ · `showcase.jsx:281 (`Field` ID инстанса), :283-293 (`Select` Приоритет), :360-373 (Create-process Modal)`)
  - Several inputs render as visual specimens only: the `ID инстанса` Field and `Приоритет` Select have no `onChange`/state binding, and the 'Создать процесс' modal's Создать button just closes the modal with no validation or submit feedback. In a true product screen this would violate the affordance rule ('an enabled element MUST do something'). This is largely acceptable for a brandbook, but the modal in particular demonstrates a primary 'Создать' action that silently does nothing — a poor pattern to enshrine in the reference the rest of the app copies.
  - → Acceptable as static specimens, but make the intent explicit: for the modal, wire the Создать button to a toast (`push({tone:'success'...})`) so the showcase models post-action feedback, mirroring how the Toast row already demonstrates outcomes. This keeps the brandbook honest about the affordance + feedback conventions it is supposed to teach.
- ⚪ **Hardcoded px sizes in inline styles bypass spacing/density tokens** (consistency · `showcase.jsx:147,153,157,163 (`marginBottom: 6`), :215-221 (`width: 60, height: 48`, `gap: 8`), :459 (`minWidth: 160`)`)
  - Multiple inline styles use raw pixel numbers (`marginBottom: 6`, `width: 60`, `height: 48`, `gap: 8`, `minWidth: 160`) instead of the `--chs-space-*` / sizing tokens. Per the contract, hardcoded color/size/shadow values are a token violation. It is mildly ironic in the file whose job is to demonstrate disciplined token consumption (the shadow-demo swatches at lines 215-221 hardcode their own dimensions while showcasing token-based shadows).
  - → Replace raw px with `var(--chs-space-*)` where a step matches (6→space-3, 8→space-4, 16-ish via space-6), and route fixed demo-tile dimensions through tokens or a small CSS class so the showcase itself models 100% token consumption.
